\displaystyle=

\displaystyle y^{\xi_{A}}C_{ApA}\,\xi_{p}^{2}.

(158)

These can be obtained using the method given in Appendix
C.1
or as done in Appendix
D
.
Substituting the above matrix elements and
K_{(2)}^{ij}
in (
155
), we obtain

\mathcal{F}_{1}(\Delta_{p},\xi_{p};\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|c_{L},c_{M}|\rho)=2+\frac{\xi_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1)}{\xi_{A}}+\pi i\rho\frac{\xi_{p}^{2}}{\xi_{A}}-\frac{\Delta_{A}}{2\xi^{2}_{A}}\xi_{p}^{2}.

(159)

Note that this is a polynomial in
\xi_{A}
,
\Delta_{A}
,
\xi_{p}
and
\Delta_{p}
. In later section we would be interested in the large
\xi_{A}
limit. In this limit we could see that the leading term is 2, which is equal to the number of states and the subleading term is
\frac{\xi_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1)}{\xi_{A}}+\pi i\rho\frac{\xi_{p}^{2}}{\xi_{A}}
. Similarly, we have calculated some of the details of the torus block for level 2. They are described in appendix
C
.

6.2
Large
\xi_{A}
limit of BMS torus blocks

While deriving an asymptotic form for the BMS 3-point coefficients, we will need to consider the torus block of a heavy exchanged operator with dimension

\xi_{A}\gg c_{M},\xi_{p},\Delta_{p}.

We shall call this the
large
\xi_{A}
limit
. It is not required to know the relative values (ratios) of
\xi_{A}
,
\Delta_{A}
and
c_{L}
for the same, so, the asymptotic formula thus obtained will be valid for all values of
\Delta_{A}
, irrespective of any comparison to
\xi_{A}
and
c_{L}
. We will find the leading and the sub-leading terms in the asymptotic expansion in
\frac{1}{\xi_{A}}
of the torus block
\mathcal{F}_{N}
in the large
\xi_{A}
limit along with
\xi_{A}\gg\Delta_{A}
.

6.2.1
Summary of results

Since this particular part of the paper may become difficult to read, for the sanity of the reader who is mainly interested in the results, we will present the main results of the section.

Leading term in BMS torus block

The leading term of the BMS torus block in the large
\xi_{A}
limit is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\Delta_{A},\xi_{A},c_{L},c_{M}}^{\Delta_{p},\xi_{p}}(\sigma,\rho)=\sum_{N}q^{N}\widetilde{\text{dim}}_{N}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\xi_{A}}\right)=\frac{q^{\frac{1}{12}}}{\eta(\sigma)^{2}}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\xi_{A}}\right),

(160)

where

\eta(\sigma)=e^{\frac{2\pi i\sigma}{24}}\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}(1-e^{2\pi i\sigma n})=q^{\frac{1}{24}}\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}(1-q^{n}).

Subleading term in BMS torus block

We calculate the sub-leading terms in the BMS torus one-point function by intrinsic methods in a 2d BMSFT and the combined result of the leading and sub-leading orders is given by:

\mathcal{F}_{N}=\left(1+\frac{\xi_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1)}{2\xi_{A}}N\right)\widetilde{\text{dim}}_{N}+\pi i\rho\frac{\xi_{p}^{2}}{\xi_{A}}\sum_{k=0}^{N}p(N-k)p(k)(N-k)(N-2k)+\mathcal{O}(\xi_{A}^{-2}).

(161)

Here
\mathcal{F}_{N}=\mathcal{F}_{N}(\Delta_{p},\xi_{p};\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|c_{L},c_{M}|\rho)
which is defined by (
149
).
p(N)
counts the number of partitions of a given number
N
. The formula above is the result of a long and tedious calculation and a number theoretic conjecture (
205
) which we provide evidence for in Appendix
L
. We reproduce the same answer in Appendix
H
as a limit of analogous answers in 2d CFT (relevant details of which are reviewed in Appendix
A
).

6.2.2
Back to details

We now dive head-long into the details of the computation of the BMS torus blocks. A number of appendices would give further details of the computations carried out in the following analysis. We make heavy use of the BMS mode expansions developed earlier to prove several of these relations. Some of the formulae useful for what we will calculate and their proofs are presented in Appendix C.

For computing the BMS torus block to an arbitrary order, one of the principal tasks is to compute the inverse Gram-matrix to that order. Following our discussions of the generic form of the Gram matrix in Sec. 3, we will investigate this in detail now.

We are at first interested in isolating the elements of the inverse Gram matrix that would contribute to our computation of the torus block. This matrix has all its elements vanishing above the anti-diagonal barring those that are situated at the positions corresponding to the Gram-matrix elements that are norm-squared of the self-conjugate states. So, we don’t have to know the value of
\langle A;N,a|\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi iM_{0}}|A;N,b\rangle
for which
K^{ab}_{(N)}=0
since they will not contribute to
\mathcal{F}_{N}
in (
148
). In other words we only have to know the value of
\langle A;N,a|\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}|A;N,b\rangle
for which the corresponding Gram matrix elements

K_{ab}^{(N)}=\langle A;N,a|A;N,b\rangle=\langle A|(\prod_{j=1}^{N}M_{j}^{k_{j}^{\prime}})(\prod_{j=1}^{N}L_{j}^{N_{j}^{\prime}-k_{j}^{\prime}})(\prod_{j=1}^{N}L_{-j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}})(\prod_{j=1}^{N}M_{-j}^{k_{j}})|A\rangle,

(162)

lie on the anti-diagonal Gram-matrix-elements or vanishing Gram-matrix-elements lying below the anti-diagonal or are norm-squared of the self-conjugate states. Such a Gram-matrix element must have:

\text{len }M\geqslant\text{min}\{\sum_{j=1}^{N}N_{j},\sum_{j=1}^{N}N_{j}^{\prime}\}\geqslant\text{len }L.

Here
\text{len }M
and
\text{len }L
are the number of
M
-operators and
L
-operators in the inner product
\langle A;N,a|A;N,b\rangle
i.e.,

\text{len }M=\sum_{j=1}^{N}(k_{j}+k_{j}^{\prime}),\quad\text{len }L=\sum_{j=1}^{N}(N_{j}+N_{j}^{\prime}-k_{j}-k_{j}^{\prime}).

(163)

We observed that in the large
\xi_{A}
limit with
\xi_{A}\gg\Delta_{A}
, non-zero
K^{ab}_{(N)}
will be of the order of
\mathcal{O}(\frac{1}{{\xi_{A}}^{\text{len }M}})
.
Next, we note that in the one point matrix

\displaystyle\langle A|(\prod_{j=N}^{1}M_{j}^{k_{j}^{\prime}})(\prod_{j=N}^{1}L_{j}^{N_{j}^{\prime}-k_{j}^{\prime}})\phi_{p}(0,1)(\prod_{j=1}^{N}L_{-j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}})\prod_{j=1}^{N}(M_{-j}^{k_{j}})|A\rangle

(164)

the ‘uncontracted’
M
-operators give rise to factors of
\xi_{p}
through the commutator with
\phi_{p}(0,1)
.

As an example let us look at
\langle A|L_{1}L_{2}\phi_{p}(0,1)M_{-1}M_{-1}M_{-1}|A\rangle
. After a bit of calculation, we have

\displaystyle\langle A|L_{2}L_{1}\phi_{p}(0,1)M_{-1}M_{-1}M_{-1}|A\rangle=

\displaystyle 3\xi_{A}[M_{1},[\phi_{p}(0,1),M_{-1}]]

(165)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 9\langle A|[M_{1},[L_{1}[[\phi_{p}(0,1),M_{-1}],M_{-1}]]]|A\rangle

(166)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 3\xi_{A}\langle A|[L_{2},[[\phi_{p}(0,1),M_{-1}],M_{-1}]]|A\rangle

(167)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 3\langle A|[M_{2},[[\phi_{p}(0,1),M_{-1}],M_{-1}]]|A\rangle

(168)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\langle A|[L_{2},[L_{1},[[[\phi_{p}(0,1),M_{-1}],M_{-1}],M_{-1}]]]|A\rangle.

The factor of
\xi_{A}
is due to
M_{0}
which we get due to contraction of
L
’s with
M
’s. The commutator of the operators with
\phi_{p}
are due to all the uncontracted operators. Each
[\phi_{p},M]
and
[\phi_{p},L]
will give a factor of
\xi_{p}
and
\Delta_{p}
respectively.
Hence, the generic element
\langle A;N,a|\phi_{p}(0,1)|A;N,b\rangle

is a polynomial in both
\xi_{A}
and
\xi_{p}
(and also in
\Delta_{A}^{\prime}
and
\Delta_{p}
), with each monomial’s
\xi
-dependence being of the form
~{\xi_{A}^{\prime}}^{r}{\xi_{p}}^{s}
, such that
r+s=\text{len }M
. Clearly,
r
is the number of ‘contracted’
M
-operators while
s
denotes the number of ‘uncontracted’
M
-operators for each monomial, with no two monomials having the same
r
,
s
. This is because these monomials have varying number of available
L
-operators for contracting
M
-operators as some
L
-operators are used in commutation with the
\phi_{p}(0,1)
-field but the total number of
M
-operators in each of them is same(
=\text{len }M
).

Note that

\langle A|L_{1}L_{2}M_{-1}M_{-1}M_{-1}|A\rangle=0

and we can see from (
165
) that the number of uncontracted M-operators is equal to 2 or more. We gave a proof in Appendix
E
that this is true for any
\langle A;N,a|\phi_{p}(0,1)|A;N,b\rangle
for which the Gram matrix vanish, i.e.

\langle A;N,a|\phi_{p}(0,1)|A;N,b\rangle=0\quad\text{if}\,\langle A;N,a|A;N,b\rangle=0\,\text{and $\#$ of uncontracted}\,M_{n}>2.

So, the one point matrix elements corresponding to the vanishing Gram matrix will be a polynomial in which the exponents of
\xi_{p}
for each term is greater than or equal to 2.

To this end, we note that the generic matrix element at level
N
can be rewritten using (
362
) as below

\displaystyle\langle A|(\prod_{j=N}^{1}M_{j}^{k_{j}^{\prime}})(\prod_{j=N}^{1}L_{j}^{N_{j}^{\prime}-k_{j}^{\prime}})\phi_{p}(0,1)y^{M_{0}}(\prod_{j=1}^{N}L_{-j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}})(\prod_{j=1}^{N}M_{-j}^{k_{j}})|A\rangle y^{-\xi_{A}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle A|(\prod_{j=N}^{1}M_{j}^{k_{j}^{\prime}})(\prod_{j=N}^{1}L_{j}^{N_{j}^{\prime}-k_{j}^{\prime}})\phi_{p}(0,1)(\prod_{j=1}^{N}(L_{-j}+2\pi i\rho jM_{-j})^{N_{j}-k_{j}})\prod_{j=1}^{N}(M_{-j}^{k_{j}})|A\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle A|(\prod_{j=N}^{1}M_{j}^{k_{j}^{\prime}})(\prod_{j=N}^{1}L_{j}^{N_{j}^{\prime}-k_{j}^{\prime}})\phi_{p}(0,1)(\prod_{j=1}^{N}L_{-j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}})\prod_{j=1}^{N}(M_{-j}^{k_{j}})|A\rangle

\displaystyle+(\text{terms with less right-$L$-string length and more right-$M$-string length}).

(169)

Clearly, for the matrix elements contributing to
\mathcal{F}_{N}(\Delta_{p},\xi_{p};\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|c_{L},c_{M}|\rho)
, the first term is of the highest order(
\leqslant\text{len }L
) in
\xi_{A}
among all the terms (due to maximum number of
L
-operators and minimum number of
M
-operators, under the restriction
\text{len }M\geqslant\text{len }L
), though other terms may be of this same order. Thus, the contribution of a generic matrix element that corresponds to a vanishing Gram-matrix element lying below the anti-diagonal is
\mathcal{O}(\frac{1}{{\xi_{A}}^{s}})
in the said limit, where
s
is the number of uncontracted
M
-operators in the first term (clearly, other terms have
s^{\prime}\geqslant s
). We already mentioned that
s\geqslant 2
for vanishing Gram-matrix elements. Thus, in this limit,
K^{ab}_{(N)}\langle A;N,a|\phi_{p}(0,1)y^{M_{0}}|A;N,b\rangle
corresponding to any vanishing Gram-matrix element, do not contribute to the leading and sub-leading pieces of interest in
\mathcal{F}_{N}
.
Thus, only the matrix elements corresponding to the non-vanishing anti-diagonal Gram-elements or squared-norms of the self-conjugate states contribute to the said pieces in this limit.

The generic matrix element corresponding to a non-vanishing anti-diagonal Gram-element is given by:

\displaystyle\langle A|(\prod_{j=N}^{1}M_{j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}})(\prod_{j=N}^{1}L_{j}^{k_{j}})\phi_{p}(0,1)y^{M_{0}}(\prod_{j=1}^{N}L_{-j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}})(\prod_{j=1}^{N}M_{-j}^{k_{j}})|A\rangle y^{-\xi_{A}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle A|(\prod_{j=N}^{1}M_{j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}})(\prod_{j=N}^{1}L_{j}^{k_{j}})\phi_{p}(0,1)(\prod_{j=1}^{N}(L_{-j}+2\pi i\rho jM_{-j})^{N_{j}-k_{j}})(\prod_{j=1}^{N}M_{-j}^{k_{j}})|A\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle A|(\prod_{j=N}^{1}M_{j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}})(\prod_{j=N}^{1}L_{j}^{k_{j}})\phi_{p}(0,1)(\prod_{j=1}^{N}L_{-j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}})(\prod_{j=1}^{N}M_{-j}^{k_{j}})|A\rangle+\sum_{r=1}^{N}r(N_{r}-k_{r})

\displaystyle\times(2\pi i\rho)\langle A|(\prod_{j=N}^{1}M_{j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}})(\prod_{j=N}^{1}L_{j}^{k_{j}})\phi_{p}(0,1)(\prod_{j=1}^{N}L_{-j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}-\delta_{j,r}}(\prod_{j=1}^{N}M_{-j}^{k_{j}+\delta_{j,r}})|A\rangle

\displaystyle+(\text{terms with less right-$L$-string length and more right-$M$-string length})

(170)

The corresponding element in the inverse Gram-matrix is
\mathcal{O}({\xi_{A}^{-\sum_{j=1}^{N}N_{j}}})
. It is simply the inverse of the corresponding anti-diagonal Gram-element. As the terms not explicitly written above are
\mathcal{O}(\xi_{A}^{\text{len }L})
, with
\text{len }L=\sum_{j=1}^{N}N_{j}-r
, with
r\geqslant 2
, their contribution to
\mathcal{F}_{N}(\Delta_{p},\xi_{p};\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|c_{L},c_{M}|\rho)
is
\mathcal{O}(\frac{1}{\xi_{A}^{r}})
; thus they don’t contribute to the leading or sub-leading pieces in this limit. On the other hand, the first term has
r=0
and the second one has
r=1
; hence they contribute.

6.2.3
Leading term

Now we will construct the leading term in the BMS torus block in the large
\xi_{A}
expansion. For this, let us look at the first term in (
170
)

\langle A|(\prod_{j=N}^{1}M_{j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}})(\prod_{j=N}^{1}L_{j}^{k_{j}})\phi_{p}(0,1)(\prod_{j=1}^{N}L_{-j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}})(\prod_{j=1}^{N}M_{-j}^{k_{j}})|A\rangle.

(171)

The leading term in
\xi_{A}
will be the one in which all the
L
-operators and
M
-operators are contracted with each other

\displaystyle\langle A|(\prod_{j=N}^{1}M_{j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}})(\prod_{j=N}^{1}L_{j}^{k_{j}})(\prod_{j=1}^{N}L_{-j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}})(\prod_{j=1}^{N}M_{-j}^{k_{j}})\phi_{p}(1,0)|A\rangle

(172)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle A|(\prod_{j=N}^{1}M_{j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}})(\prod_{j=N}^{1}L_{j}^{k_{j}})(\prod_{j=1}^{N}L_{-j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}})(\prod_{j=1}^{N}M_{-j}^{k_{j}})|A\rangle\langle A|\phi_{p}(1,0)|A\rangle

(173)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{ApA}\langle A|(\prod_{j=N}^{1}M_{j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}})(\prod_{j=N}^{1}L_{j}^{k_{j}})(\prod_{j=1}^{N}L_{-j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}})(\prod_{j=1}^{N}M_{-j}^{k_{j}})|A\rangle.

(174)

The inverse Gram matrix elements
K_{(N)}^{ab}
for

K_{ab}^{(N)}=\langle A|(\prod_{j=N}^{1}M_{j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}})(\prod_{j=N}^{1}L_{j}^{k_{j}})(\prod_{j=1}^{N}L_{-j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}})(\prod_{j=1}^{N}M_{-j}^{k_{j}})|A\rangle

(175)

are simply given by

K_{(N)}^{ab}=\left[{\langle A|(\prod_{j=N}^{1}M_{j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}})(\prod_{j=N}^{1}L_{j}^{k_{j}})(\prod_{j=1}^{N}L_{-j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}})(\prod_{j=1}^{N}M_{-j}^{k_{j}})|A\rangle}\right]^{-1}.

(176)

Thus we have

\displaystyle\text{Leading order in }K^{ab}_{(N)}\langle A;N,a|\phi_{p}(0,1)y^{M_{0}}|A;N,b\rangle

(177)

\displaystyle=y^{\xi_{A}}\frac{\langle A|(\prod_{j=N}^{1}M_{j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}})(\prod_{j=N}^{1}L_{j}^{k_{j}})(\prod_{j=1}^{N}L_{-j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}})(\prod_{j=1}^{N}M_{-j}^{k_{j}})|A\rangle\langle A|\phi_{p}(1,0)|A\rangle}{\langle A|(\prod_{j=N}^{1}M_{j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}})(\prod_{j=N}^{1}L_{j}^{k_{j}})(\prod_{j=1}^{N}L_{-j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}})(\prod_{j=1}^{N}M_{-j}^{k_{j}})|A\rangle}

(178)

\displaystyle=y^{\xi_{A}}\langle A|\phi_{p}(0,1)|A\rangle=y^{\xi_{A}}C_{ApA}.

(179)

Each non-zero anti-diagonal terms in the Gram matrix and diagonal elements corresponding to self-conjugate states contributes the same factor as above.
Since we have
\widetilde{\text{dim}}_{N}
number of such elements, the leading term in
\mathcal{F}_{N}
is given by

\text{Leading term}=\frac{1}{y^{\xi_{A}}C_{ApA}}y^{\xi_{A}}C_{ApA}\widetilde{\text{dim}}_{N}=\widetilde{\text{dim}}_{N}.

(180)

So, we have

\mathcal{F}_{N}=\widetilde{\text{dim}}_{N}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\xi_{A}}\right).

(181)

Using this, we can see from (
148
) that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\Delta_{A},\xi_{A},c_{L},c_{M}}^{\Delta_{p},\xi_{p}}(\sigma,\rho)=\sum_{N}q^{N}\widetilde{\text{dim}}_{N}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\xi_{A}}\right)=\frac{q^{\frac{1}{12}}}{\eta(\sigma)^{2}}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\xi_{A}}\right),

(182)

where

\eta(\sigma)=e^{\frac{2\pi i\sigma}{24}}\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}(1-e^{2\pi i\sigma n})=q^{\frac{1}{24}}\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}(1-q^{n}).

It is of interest to note here that if we modify the definition of the BMS torus block (
143
) to keep the exponential factors
q^{\Delta_{A}-c_{L}/2}
and
y^{\xi_{A}-c_{M}/2}
, the expression of the leading term BMS torus block is exactly the expression for the BMS character found in
Bagchi:2019unf
;
Oblak:2015sea
;
Barnich:2015mui
;
Garbarz:2015lua
.

6.2.4
Subleading term

Now, we focus our attention on the sub-leading pieces in the BMS torus block in our large
\xi_{A}
expansion. We shall see that there are actually two sources for the sub-leading correction. We being our descriptions with an example.

The sub-leading contribution to (
171
) is due to the terms in which all the
L
-operators and
M
-operators contract to produce
M_{0}
except for one pair of
L
and
M
. This will have one less
\xi_{A}
as compared to the leading term. The un-contracted pair of
L
and
M
appear in the commutator with
\phi_{p}
in the form
[L_{k},[\phi_{p}(0,1),M_{-k}]]
or
[M_{k},[\phi_{p}(0,1),L_{-k}]]
. Let us consider an example:

\displaystyle\text{Sub-leading term in }\langle A|M_{1}^{3}L_{2}^{2}\phi_{p}(0,1)L_{-1}^{3}M_{-2}^{3}|A\rangle

(183)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 9\langle A|M_{1}^{2}L_{2}^{2}L_{-1}^{2}M_{-2}^{3}[M_{1},[\phi_{p}(0,1),L_{-1}]|A\rangle+6\langle A|M_{1}^{3}L_{2}^{1}L_{-1}^{3}M_{-2}^{2}[L_{2},[\phi_{p}(0,1),M_{-2}]|A\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 9\langle A|M_{1}^{2}L_{2}^{2}L_{-1}^{2}M_{-2}^{3}|A\rangle\langle A|[M_{1},[\phi_{p}(0,1),L_{-1}]|A\rangle+6\langle A|M_{1}^{3}L_{2}^{1}L_{-1}^{3}M_{-2}^{2}|A\rangle\langle A|[L_{2},[\phi_{p}(0,1),M_{-2}]|A\rangle.

The factor of 9 is the number of choosing a pair of
M_{1}
and
L_{-1}
that appear in the commutator
\langle A|[M_{1},[\phi_{p}(0,1),L_{-1}]|A\rangle
. Since we have 3
M_{1}^{3}
and 3
L_{-1}^{3}
, the total of pairs is given by
3\times 3=9
. Similarly the factor of 6 is the number of choosing a pair of
L_{2}
and
M_{-2}
that appear in the commutator
\langle A|[L_{2},[\phi_{p}(0,1),M_{-2}]|A\rangle
.

As mentioned, we will have two types of terms contributing at the next-to-leading order to the BMS torus block. Following the example above, we can deduce the first type of term, which arises from the sub-leading term for a generic anti-diagonal element (
171
). This first type consist of terms with

\langle A|[M_{j},[\phi_{p}(0,1),L_{-j}]|A\rangle\quad\text{or}\quad\langle A|[L_{j},[\phi_{p}(0,1),M_{-j}]|A\rangle.

We can see that there are
(N_{j}-k_{j})^{2}
ways of choosing a pair of
M_{j}
and
L_{-j}
and
k_{j}^{2}
number of ways of choosing a pair of
L_{j}
and
M_{-j}
. Thus the subleading term is given by

\displaystyle\text{Type 1 Sub-leading term (SLT${}_{1}$)}=

\displaystyle\quad\sum_{r=1}^{N}(N_{r}-k_{r})^{2}\langle A|\prod_{j=N}^{1}M_{j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}-\delta_{j,r}}\prod_{j=N}^{1}L_{j}^{k_{j}}\prod_{j=1}^{N}L_{-j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}-\delta_{j,r}}\prod_{j=1}^{N}M_{-j}^{k_{j}}|A\rangle\langle A|[M_{r},[\phi_{p}(0,1),L_{-r}]]|A\rangle

(184)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{r=1}^{N}k_{r}^{2}\langle A|(\prod_{j=N}^{1}M_{j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}})(\prod_{j=N}^{1}L_{j}^{k_{j}-\delta_{j,r}})(\prod_{j=1}^{N}L_{-j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}})(\prod_{j=1}^{N}M_{-j}^{k_{j}-\delta_{j,r}})|A\rangle\langle A|[L_{r},[\phi_{p}(0,1),M_{-r}]]|A\rangle.

Now, we have

\langle A|[M_{r},[\phi_{p}(0,1),L_{-r}]]|A\rangle=\langle A|[L_{r},[\phi_{p}(0,1),M_{-r}]]|A\rangle=C_{ApA}\,r^{2}\xi_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1).

(186)

Substituting this in the previous equation we get

SLT
1

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{ApA}\sum_{r=1}^{N}(N_{r}-k_{r})^{2}r^{2}\xi_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1)\langle A|\prod_{j=N}^{1}M_{j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}-\delta_{j,r}}\prod_{j=N}^{1}L_{j}^{k_{j}}\prod_{j=1}^{N}L_{-j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}-\delta_{j,r}}\prod_{j=1}^{N}M_{-j}^{k_{j}}|A\rangle

(188)

\displaystyle+C_{ApA}\sum_{r=1}^{N}k_{r}^{2}r^{2}\xi_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1)\langle A|\prod_{j=N}^{1}M_{j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}}\prod_{j=N}^{1}L_{j}^{k_{j}-\delta_{j,r}}\prod_{j=1}^{N}L_{-j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}}\prod_{j=1}^{N}M_{-j}^{k_{j}-\delta_{j,r}}|A\rangle.

We have to multiply this by the corresponding inverse Gram matrix
K^{ab}_{(N)}
. As we have already pointed out, for the one point matrices of interest, these are simply given by
K^{ab}_{(N)}=\frac{1}{K_{ab}^{(N)}}
.
So, we get

SLT
1

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{ApA}\sum_{r=1}^{N}\frac{(N_{r}-k_{r})^{2}r^{2}\xi_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1)\prod_{j=1}^{N}\langle A|M_{j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}-\delta_{j,r}}L_{-j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}-\delta_{j,r}}|A\rangle\langle A|L_{j}^{k_{j}}M_{-j}^{k_{j}}|A\rangle}{\prod_{j=1}^{N}\langle A|M_{j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}}L_{-j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}}|A\rangle\langle A|L_{j}^{k_{j}}M_{-j}^{k_{j}}|A\rangle}

(189)

\displaystyle+C_{ApA}\sum_{r=1}^{N}\frac{k_{r}^{2}r^{2}\xi_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1)\prod_{j=1}^{N}\langle A|M_{j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}}L_{-j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}}|A\rangle\langle A|L_{j}^{k_{j}-\delta_{j,r}}M_{-j}^{k_{j}-\delta_{j,r}}|A\rangle}{{\prod_{j=1}^{N}\langle A|M_{j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}}L_{-j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}}|A\rangle\langle A|L_{j}^{k_{j}}M_{-j}^{k_{j}}|A\rangle}},

Using (
126
) and (
127
) the above expression is given by

SLT
1

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{ApA}\sum_{r=1}^{N}\frac{(N_{r}-k_{r})r^{2}\xi_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1)}{\langle A|M_{r}L{-r}|A\rangle}+C_{ApA}\sum_{r=1}^{N}\frac{k_{r}r^{2}\xi_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1)}{\langle A|L_{r}M{-r}|A\rangle}.

(190)

In the large
\xi_{A}
limit we have

\langle A|L_{r}M{-r}|A\rangle=2r\xi_{A}-c_{M}(r^{3}-r)\approx 2r\xi_{A}.

(191)

Substituting this in the previous equation, we get

SLT
1

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{ApA}\sum_{r=1}^{N}\frac{(N_{r}-k_{r})r\xi_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1)}{2\xi_{A}}+C_{ApA}\sum_{r=1}^{N}\frac{k_{r}r\xi_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1)}{2\xi_{A}}

(193)

\displaystyle\quad=C_{ApA}\frac{\xi_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1)}{2\xi_{A}}\sum_{r=1}^{N}rN_{r}=C_{ApA}\frac{\xi_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1)}{2\xi_{A}}N

This is only for one term. Thus the total subleading contribution of these Type 1 subleading terms in
\mathcal{F}_{N}
due to (
171
), after taking care of the factors of
C_{ApA}
and
y^{\xi_{A}}
is

\text{Total contribution from all SLT${}_{1}$}=N\frac{\xi_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1)}{2\xi_{A}}\widetilde{\text{dim}}_{N}.

(194)

The other sub-leading piece in the BMS torus block comes from the second term in (
170
)

\displaystyle\text{Type 2 Sub-leading term (SLT${}_{2}$)}=

\displaystyle\quad 2\pi i\rho\sum_{r=1}^{N}r(N_{r}-k_{r})\langle A|\prod_{j=N}^{1}M_{j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}}\prod_{j=N}^{1}L_{j}^{k_{j}}\ \phi_{p}(0,1)\ \prod_{j=1}^{N}L_{-j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}-\delta_{j,r}}\prod_{j=1}^{N}M_{-j}^{k_{j}+\delta_{j,r}}|A\rangle

(195)

(196)

The leading term in this is will be subleading in
\mathcal{F}_{N}
. These are the term in which one
M_{j}
from
M_{j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}}
and an
M_{-j}
from
M_{-j}^{k_{j}+\delta_{j,r}}
are used in the commutator with
\phi_{p}
i,e.,
[M_{j},[\phi_{p},M_{-j}]]
. With this, the remaining
M
-operators is equal to the number of
L
-operators and thus can be contracted with no uncontracted
M
-operators. More explicitly, this is given by

\displaystyle\text{SLT${}_{2}$}=

\displaystyle 2\pi i\rho\sum_{r=1}^{N}r(N_{r}-k_{r})\langle A|\prod_{j=N}^{1}M_{j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}-\delta_{j,r}}\prod_{j=N}^{1}L_{j}^{k_{j}}\prod_{j=1}^{N}L_{-j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}-\delta_{j,r}}\prod_{j=1}^{N}M_{-j}^{k_{j}}|A\rangle

(197)

\displaystyle\quad\times\langle A|\phi(0,1)|A\rangle(N_{r}-k_{r})(k_{r}+1)\langle A|[M_{r},[\phi_{p}(0,1),M_{-r}]]|A\rangle,

where
(N_{r}-k_{r})(k_{r}+1)
is the number of ways of choosing a pair of
M_{r}
and
M_{-r}
.
We have to multiply the above by the inverse Gram matrix elements. From our previous calculation we have

\displaystyle\frac{\langle A|(\prod_{j=N}^{1}M_{j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}-\delta_{j,r}})(\prod_{j=N}^{1}L_{j}^{k_{j}})(\prod_{j=1}^{N}L_{-j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}-\delta_{j,r}}(\prod_{j=1}^{N}M_{-j}^{k_{j}})|A\rangle}{\langle A|(\prod_{j=N}^{1}M_{j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}})(\prod_{j=N}^{1}L_{j}^{k_{j}})(\prod_{j=1}^{N}L_{-j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}}(\prod_{j=1}^{N}M_{-j}^{k_{j}})|A\rangle}

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{(N_{r}-k_{r})\langle A|[M_{r},L_{-r}]|A\rangle}\approx\frac{1}{(N_{r}-k_{r})2r\xi_{A}}.

(198)

Using
\langle A|[M_{r},[\phi_{p}(0,1),M_{-r}]]|A\rangle=r^{2}\xi_{p}^{2}
, the type 2 sub-leading term due to (
196
) is given by

\displaystyle\text{SLT${}_{2}$}=2\pi i\rho\sum_{r=1}^{N}\frac{\,r^{3}(N_{r}-k_{r})^{2}(k_{r}+1)}{(N_{r}-k_{r})2r\xi_{A}}=(2\pi i\rho)\frac{\xi_{p}^{2}}{2\xi_{A}}\sum_{r=1}^{N}r^{2}(N_{r}-k_{r})(k_{r}+1).

(199)

Let us change our notation a bit, we will replace
N_{r}-k_{r}
by
l_{r}
and
k_{r}
by
m_{r}
. With this new notation, we have

\displaystyle\text{SLT${}_{2}$}=(2\pi i\rho)\frac{\xi_{p}^{2}}{2\xi_{A}}\sum_{r=1}^{N}r^{2}l_{r}(m_{r}+1).

(200)

This is the contribution due to only one matrix elements. We have to sum up for all the sets
\{\{l_{r}\},\{m_{r}\}\}
for which

\sum_{r=1}^{N}r(l_{r}+m_{r})=N,

(201)

Let us denote this set by
\mathcal{A}
. Thus the subleading term due to (
196
) is

\text{Total contribution from all SLT${}_{2}$}=(2\pi i\rho)\frac{\xi_{p}^{2}}{2\xi_{A}}\sum_{\{\{l_{r}\},\{m_{r}\}\}\in\mathcal{A}}\sum_{r=1}^{N}r^{2}l_{r}(m_{r}+1).

(202)

Combining all our results, we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{N}(\Delta_{p},\xi_{p};\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|c_{L},c_{M}|\rho)=

\displaystyle\left(1+\frac{\xi_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1)}{2\xi_{A}}N\right)\widetilde{\text{dim}}_{N}

\displaystyle+(2\pi i\rho)\frac{\xi_{p}^{2}}{2\xi_{A}}\sum_{\{\{l_{r}\},\{m_{r}\}\}\in A}\sum_{r=1}^{N}r^{2}l_{r}(m_{r}+1)+\mathcal{O}(\frac{1}{\xi_{A}^{2}}).

(203)

A conjecture and a rewriting

We will now conjecture an identity involving the partition of integers using two colours which will enable us rewrite our earlier equation (
203
). Consider the partition of an integer
N
using two colours in the form:

\sum_{j=1}^{N}j(l_{j}+m_{j})=N,\quad\forall\,l_{j},m_{j}\geqslant 0.

Let us denote the set of all such different partitions as
A
and denote an element in
A
as
\{\{l_{j}\},\{m_{j}\}\}
. Now, we conjecture the following relation:

\displaystyle\sum_{\{\{l_{j}\},\{m_{j}\}\}\in A}\,\,\sum_{j=1}^{N}j^{2}l_{j}(m_{j}+1)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=0}^{N}p(N-k)p(k)(N-2k)^{2}

(204)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{N}p(N-k)p(k)(N-k)(N-2k).

(205)

Although we don’t have a proof of this conjecture, we have checked for the validity of (
205
) upto level 5. Some of the details are described in appendix
L
. Substituting (
205
) in (
203
), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{N}(\Delta_{p},\xi_{p};\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|c_{L},c_{M}|\rho)=

\displaystyle\left(1+\frac{\xi_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1)}{2\xi_{A}}N\right)\widetilde{\text{dim}}_{N}

(206)

\displaystyle+\pi i\rho\frac{\xi_{p}^{2}}{\xi_{A}}\sum_{k=0}^{N}p(N-k)p(k)(N-k)(N-2k)+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\xi_{A}^{2}}\right).

6.2.5
The special case of
\xi_{p}=0

As noted previously, the uncontracted
M
-operators in the relevant matrix elements of (
149
) give rise to factors of
\xi_{p}
. So, when
\xi_{p}=0
, only the matrix elements corresponding to the non-vanishing anti-diagonal Gram-elements or squared-norms of the self-conjugate states give non-vanishing contribution to
\mathcal{F}_{N}(\Delta_{p},\xi_{p};\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|c_{L},c_{M}|\rho)
. From (
170
) and (
174
), it is evident that for
\xi_{p}=0
:

\displaystyle\langle A|(\prod_{j=N}^{1}M_{j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}})(\prod_{j=N}^{1}L_{j}^{k_{j}})\phi_{p}(0,1)y^{M_{0}}(\prod_{j=1}^{N}L_{-j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}})(\prod_{j=1}^{N}M_{-j}^{k_{j}})|A\rangle y^{-\xi_{A}}

\displaystyle=\langle A|(\prod_{j=N}^{1}M_{j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}})(\prod_{j=N}^{1}L_{j}^{k_{j}})\phi_{p}(0,1)(\prod_{j=1}^{N}L_{-j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}})(\prod_{j=1}^{N}M_{-j}^{k_{j}})|A\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+(\text{terms with atleast 1 uncontracted $M$-operators, hence giving 0})

\displaystyle=\langle A|(\prod_{j=N}^{1}M_{j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}})(\prod_{j=N}^{1}L_{j}^{k_{j}})(\prod_{j=1}^{N}L_{-j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}})(\prod_{j=1}^{N}M_{-j}^{k_{j}})|A\rangle y^{-\xi_{A}}\langle A|\phi_{p}(0,1)|A\rangle+0.

(207)

Thus the leading term in
\xi_{A}
is the exact result in a closed-form for
\xi_{p}=0
. Consequently, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{N}(\Delta_{p},\xi_{p}=0;\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|c_{L},c_{M}|\rho)_{A\neq Id.}=\widetilde{\text{dim}}_{N}=\sum_{n=0}^{N}p(n)p(N-n).

(208)

Thus the ‘torus block’ for a primary with
\xi_{p}=0
is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\Delta_{p},\xi_{p}=0}_{\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}:A\neq Id.,c_{L},c_{M}}(\sigma,\rho)=\sum_{N=0}^{\infty}q^{N}\widetilde{\text{dim}}_{N}=\frac{q^{\frac{1}{12}}}{\eta(\sigma)^{2}}.

(209)

So, for
\xi_{p}=0
, the torus block doesn’t depend on
\Delta_{p}
. Hence, the one-point function for a primary-field
7
7

7

We restrict the BMSFT to only contain primary-fields with
\Delta_{p}>0
, with the exception of the Identity being the only primary-field with
\Delta_{p}=0
.
with
\Delta_{p}>0
and
\xi_{p}=0
is as follows (the vacuum block doesn’t contribute for
\Delta_{p}>0
, as
\langle 0|\phi_{p}(1,0)|0\rangle=\delta_{\Delta_{p},0}
)

\displaystyle\langle\phi_{\Delta_{p}>0,\xi_{p}=0}\rangle_{(\sigma,\rho)}=\frac{q^{\frac{1}{12}-\frac{c_{L}}{2}}y^{-\frac{c_{M}}{2}}}{\eta(\sigma)^{2}}\sum_{A\neq Id.}D(\Delta_{A},\xi_{A})\langle A|\phi_{p}(0,1)|A\rangle q^{\Delta_{A}}y^{\xi_{A}}.

(210)

6.3
Minkowski torus block from Casimir equation

We will now digress a bit from the main theme of the paper to discuss an important point, which we will not use later in the paper. In this section we will find the global BMS torus block or the Minkowski torus block, using differential equation from the Casimir operators. Of course, the name Minkowski torus block is because the BMS
3
algebra, restricted to the “global” part
n=0,\pm 1
is just
iso(2,1)
, the Poincare algebra in three bulk dimensions. We are interested, as always, in a 2d field theory with
iso(2,1)
as its symmetries.

We will adopt the ideas and methods of
Kraus:2017ezw
with suitable modifications to obtain the BMS versions of the analogous 2d CFT results. These methods are also similar to the ones in
Bagchi:2016geg
used to obtain the BMS four-point blocks.

We begin by rewriting the block a bit as shown below

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\Delta_{A},\xi_{A},c_{L},c_{M}}^{\Delta_{p},\xi_{p}}(q,y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm Tr}_{\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}}\left(\phi_{p}(u,\phi)q^{L_{0}-\frac{c_{L}}{2}}y^{M_{0}-\frac{c_{M}}{2}}\right)}{q^{\Delta_{A}-\frac{c_{L}}{2}}y^{\xi_{A}-\frac{c_{M}}{2}}C_{ApA}}

(211)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{q^{-\Delta_{A}}y^{-\xi_{A}}}{C_{ApA}}{\rm Tr}_{\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}}\left(q^{L_{0}}y^{M_{0}}\phi_{p}(u,\phi)\right)

(212)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\frac{q^{-\Delta_{A}}y^{-\xi_{A}}}{C_{ApA}}\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\Delta_{A},\xi_{A},c_{L},c_{M}}^{\Delta_{p},\xi_{p}}(q,y).

(213)

Note that
\phi_{p}(u,\phi)
is the primary field on the cylinder and instead of expressing the blocks as a function of
\sigma
and
\rho
, we are using the variables
q=e^{2\pi i\sigma}
and
y=e^{2\pi i\rho}
.
We also use the cyclicity property of trace to move
q^{L_{0}}y^{M_{0}}
to the front (also remember that
L_{0}
and
M_{0}
commute).

Let
P_{A}
be the projector operator for the BMS module of the highest weight state
|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle
i.e., it acts as an identity operator for any state in the module and annihilate any other states which does not belong to the module. It is given by

P_{A}=\sum_{N=0}^{\infty}K_{(N)}^{ij}|A;N,i\rangle\langle A;N,j|.

(214)

We can use
P_{A}
to project out the contribution of the BMS module of
|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle
to the one-point function. So, the blocks
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\Delta_{A},\xi_{A},c_{L},c_{M}}^{\Delta_{p},\xi_{p}}(q,y)
is given by

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\Delta_{A},\xi_{A},c_{L},c_{M}}^{\Delta_{p},\xi_{p}}(q,y)={\rm{Tr}}_{\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}}\left(q^{L_{0}}y^{M_{0}}\phi_{p}(u,\phi)\right)={\rm{Tr}}\left(P_{A}\,q^{L_{0}}y^{M_{0}}\phi_{p}(u,\phi)\right),

(215)

where
\rm{Tr}
, without the subscript
\Delta_{A}
and
\xi_{A}
, is trace over the basis of the whole Hilbert space of the theory.

Just like what we have in the case of four-point conformal block, in the large central charge limit, the leading term of the torus block in (
215
) is given by tracing over states of the form
L_{-1}^{k}M_{-1}^{q}|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle
. So, at level
N
, we have to consider only states of the form
\sum_{k=0}^{N}(L_{-1})^{N-k}(M_{-1})^{k}|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle
. Likewise, the states
|A;N,i\rangle
appearing in the projector in (
214
) will only be of this form in the large central charge limit. Thus, the global part of
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\Delta_{A},\xi_{A},c_{L},c_{M}}^{\Delta_{p},\xi_{p}}(q,y)
is schematically given by

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{\Delta_{A},\xi_{A},c_{L},c_{M}}^{\Delta_{p},\xi_{p}}(q,y)=\text{Tr}_{g}\left(P_{A}^{g}\,q^{L_{0}}y^{M_{0}}\phi_{p}(u,\phi)\right)=\text{Tr}_{g,A}\left(q^{L_{0}}y^{M_{0}}\phi_{p}(u,\phi)\right),

(216)

where the index
g
in
\text{Tr}_{g}
and
P_{A}^{g}
means that we are tracing only over states of the form
L_{-1}^{k}M_{-1}^{q}|\Delta,\xi\rangle
and the projector also consists only of states of this form.
Likewise,
\text{Tr}_{g,A}
is over states of the form
L_{-1}^{k}M_{-1}^{q}|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle
.

The global subgroup of BMS is generated by the set of generators
\{L_{0,\pm 1}1,M_{0,\pm 1}\}
which from a closed sub-algebra. The quadratic Casimir’s of these generators are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle M_{0}^{2}-M_{-1}M_{1}

(217)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2L_{0}M_{0}-\frac{1}{2}(L_{-1}M_{1}+L_{1}M_{-1}+M_{1}L_{-1}+M_{-1}L_{1})

(218)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2L_{0}M_{0}-L_{-1}M_{1}-M_{-1}L_{1}-2M_{0}.

(219)

Since
L_{-1}
and
M_{-1}
commutes with
\mathcal{C}_{1}
and
\mathcal{C}_{2}
, the states
L_{-1}^{k}M_{-1}^{q}|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle
are eigenstates of these operators

\mathcal{C}_{1,2}L_{-1}^{k}M_{-1}^{q}|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle=\lambda_{1,2}L_{-1}^{k}M_{-1}^{q}|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle,

(220)

with eigenvalues given by

\lambda_{1}=\xi_{A}^{2},\quad\lambda_{2}=2\xi_{A}(\Delta_{A}-1).

(221)

Thus we have

\mathcal{C}_{1,2}P_{A}^{g}=\lambda_{1,2}P_{A}^{g},\quad P_{A}^{g}\mathcal{C}_{1,2}=\lambda_{1,2}P_{A}^{g}.

(222)

Using the above equation in (
216
), we obtain

\displaystyle\text{Tr}_{g}\left(P_{A}^{g}\,\mathcal{C}_{1,2}\,q^{L_{0}}y^{M_{0}}\phi_{p}(u,\phi)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda_{1,2}\tilde{f}(q,y)

(223)

\displaystyle\implies\text{Tr}_{g,A}\left(\mathcal{C}_{1,2}\,q^{L_{0}}y^{M_{0}}\phi_{p}(u,\phi)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda_{1,2}\tilde{f}(q,y),

(224)

where we denote
\tilde{f}_{\Delta_{A},\xi_{A},c_{L},c_{M}}^{\Delta_{p},\xi_{p}}(q,y)
by
\tilde{f}(q,y)
to reduce cluttering of indices.
In the LHS, we can move
\mathcal{C}_{1,2}
toward the right by commuting with
q^{L_{0}}
and
y^{M_{0}}
. For this, we will make use of the identities given below

\displaystyle L_{n}q^{L_{0}}=q^{n+L_{0}}L_{n},\quad L_{n}y^{M_{0}}=y^{M_{0}}L_{n}+n\log y\,y^{M_{0}}M_{n},\quad M_{n}q^{L_{0}}=q^{L_{0}+n}M_{n}.

(225)

(226)

Using the fact that

L_{0}q^{L_{0}}=q\partial_{q}q^{L_{0}},\quad M_{0}y^{M_{0}}=y\partial_{y}y^{M_{0}},

(227)

the insertion of
L_{0}
and
M_{0}
is same as acting with differential operator
q\partial_{q}
and
y\partial_{y}

\displaystyle\text{Tr}_{g,A}\left(L_{0}\,q^{L_{0}}y^{M_{0}}\phi_{p}(u,\phi)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q\partial_{q}\text{Tr}_{g,A}\left(q^{L_{0}}y^{M_{0}}\phi_{p}(u,\phi)\right),

(228)

\displaystyle\text{Tr}_{g,A}\left(M_{0}\,q^{L_{0}}y^{M_{0}}\phi_{p}(u,\phi)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle y\partial_{y}\text{Tr}_{g,A}\left(q^{L_{0}}y^{M_{0}}\phi_{p}(u,\phi)\right).

(229)

For the insertion of
M_{n}
, we use (
226
) as well as the cyclicity property of trace to obtain

\displaystyle\text{Tr}_{g,A}

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\left(M_{n}\,q^{L_{0}}y^{M_{0}}\phi_{p}(u,\phi)\right)=q^{n}\text{Tr}_{g,A}\left(\,q^{L_{0}}y^{M_{0}}M_{n}\phi_{p}(u,\phi)\right)

(230)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q^{n}\text{Tr}_{g,A}\left(\,q^{L_{0}}y^{M_{0}}\phi_{p}(u,\phi)M_{n}\right)+q^{n}\text{Tr}_{g,A}\left(\,q^{L_{0}}y^{M_{0}}[M_{n},\phi_{p}(u,\phi)]\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q^{n}\text{Tr}_{g,A}\left(M_{n}\,q^{L_{0}}y^{M_{0}}\phi_{p}(u,\phi)M_{n}\right)+q^{n}\text{Tr}_{g,A}\left(\,q^{L_{0}}y^{M_{0}}\mathcal{M}_{n}\phi_{p}(u,\phi)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q^{n}\text{Tr}_{g,A}\left(M_{n}\,q^{L_{0}}y^{M_{0}}\phi_{p}(u,\phi)M_{n}\right)+q^{n}\mathcal{M}_{n}\text{Tr}_{g,A}\left(\,q^{L_{0}}y^{M_{0}}\phi_{p}(u,\phi)\right),

where the differential operator
\mathcal{M}_{n}
is defined as

[M_{n},\phi_{p}(u,\phi)]\equiv\mathcal{M}_{n}\phi_{p}(u,\phi).

(231)

Similarly, we define the differential operator
\mathscr{L}_{n}
by

[L_{n},\phi_{p}(u,\phi)]\equiv\mathscr{L}_{n}\phi_{p}(u,\phi).

(232)

Rearranging the above equation we get

\text{Tr}_{g,A}\left(M_{n}\,q^{L_{0}}y^{M_{0}}\phi_{p}(u,\phi)\right)=\frac{q^{n}}{1-q^{n}}\mathcal{M}_{n}\tilde{f}(q,y).

(233)

We also have

\displaystyle\text{Tr}_{g,A}\left(L_{n}\,q^{L_{0}}y^{M_{0}}\phi_{p}(u,\phi)\right)=\frac{q^{n}}{1-q^{n}}\mathscr{L}_{n}\tilde{f}(q,y)+\frac{q^{n}n\log y}{(1-q^{n})^{2}}\mathcal{M}_{n}\tilde{f}(q,y).

(234)

We could insert an additional
M
or
L
in the above two equations to obtain the following identities (note that
n=1,-1
)

\text{Tr}_{g,A}\left(M_{-n}M_{n}\,q^{L_{0}}y^{M_{0}}\phi_{p}(u,\phi)\right)=\frac{1}{(1-q^{n})(1-q^{-n})}\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathcal{M}_{-n}\tilde{f}(q,y),

(235)

\displaystyle\text{Tr}_{g,A}\left(M_{-n}L_{n}\,q^{L_{0}}y^{M_{0}}\phi_{p}(u,\phi)\right)=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(1-q^{n})(1-q^{-n})}\mathscr{L}_{n}\mathcal{M}_{-n}\tilde{f}(q,y)

(236)

\displaystyle+\frac{2nq^{n}y}{1-q^{n}}\partial_{y}\tilde{f}(q,y)+\frac{n\log y}{(1-q^{n})^{2}(1-q^{-n})}\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathcal{M}_{-n}\tilde{f}(q,y),

\displaystyle\text{Tr}_{g,A}\left(L_{-n}M_{n}\,q^{L_{0}}y^{M_{0}}\phi_{p}(u,\phi)\right)=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(1-q^{n})(1-q^{-n})}\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathscr{L}_{-n}\tilde{f}(q,y)

(237)

\displaystyle+\frac{2nq^{n}y}{1-q^{n}}\partial_{y}\tilde{f}(q,y)-\frac{n\log y}{(1-q^{n})(1-q^{-n})^{2}}\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathcal{M}_{-n}\tilde{f}(q,y).

Now that we have the required identities, let us find the differential equation for
\tilde{f}(q,y)
using the quadratic Casimir
\mathcal{C}_{1}
. For this we have

\displaystyle\text{Tr}_{g,A}\left(\mathcal{C}_{1}\,q^{L_{0}}y^{M_{0}}\phi_{p}(u,\phi)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{Tr}_{g,A}\left(M_{0}^{2}\,q^{L_{0}}y^{M_{0}}\phi_{p}(u,\phi)\right)-\text{Tr}_{g,A}\left(M_{-1}M_{1}\,q^{L_{0}}y^{M_{0}}\phi_{p}(u,\phi)\right),

(238)

which gives us

\displaystyle\xi_{A}^{2}f(q,y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle y\partial y\left(y\partial yf(q,y)\right)-\frac{1}{(1-q)(1-q^{-1})}\mathcal{M}_{1}\mathcal{M}_{-1}f(q,y).

(240)

We know that
\tilde{f}(q,y)
is invariant under translation. Thus

\mathcal{M}_{0}\tilde{f}(q,t)=\partial_{u}\tilde{f}(q,t)=0.

(241)

So, we may add
\mathcal{M}_{0}^{2}\tilde{f}(q,y)=0
in the above equation

\displaystyle\xi_{A}^{2}\tilde{f}(q,y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle y\partial y\left(y\partial y\tilde{f}(q,y)\right)+\frac{1}{(1-q)(1-q^{-1})}(\mathcal{M}_{0}^{2}-\mathcal{M}_{1}\mathcal{M}_{-1})\tilde{f}(q,y).

(242)

Then using

[\mathcal{C}_{1},\phi_{p}(u,\phi)]=(\mathcal{M}_{0}^{2}-\mathcal{M}_{1}\mathcal{M}_{-1})\phi_{p}(u,\phi)=\xi_{p}^{2}\phi_{p}(u,\phi),

(243)

in the above differential equation, and substituting

Y=\log y,\quad\tilde{f}(q,Y)=\frac{q}{(1-q)^{2}}f(q,Y)

we obtain
8
8

8

Interestingly, the differential equation stays the same in
\tilde{f}
as well.
:

\partial_{Y}^{2}f(q,Y)-\left(\xi_{p}^{2}\frac{q}{(1-q)^{2}}+\xi_{A}^{2}\right)f(q,Y)=0.

(244)

The general solution of this equation is given by

f(q,y)=A(q)y^{\xi_{A}\sqrt{1+\frac{\xi_{p}^{2}}{\xi_{A}^{2}}\frac{q}{(1-q)^{2}}}}+B(q)y^{-\xi_{A}\sqrt{1+\frac{\xi_{p}^{2}}{\xi_{A}^{2}}\frac{q}{(1-q)^{2}}}}

(245)

In the above, we have resubistuted
Y
in terms of
y
. We know that
f(q,y)
varies like
y^{\xi_{A}}
. Thus, we have to drop the second term in the above equation which is not of this form. Thus, we have

f(q,Y)=A(q)e^{Y\sqrt{\xi_{A}^{2}+\xi_{p}^{2}\frac{q}{(1-q)^{2}}}}=A(q)y^{\sqrt{\xi_{A}^{2}+\xi_{p}^{2}\frac{q}{(1-q)^{2}}}}.

(246)

Next, let us look at the differential equation obtained from the Casimir
\mathcal{C}_{2}

\displaystyle 2\xi_{A}(\Delta_{A}-1)\tilde{f}(q,y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{Tr}_{g,A}\left((2L_{0}M_{0}-L_{-1}M_{1}-M_{-1}L_{1}-2M_{0})\,q^{L_{0}}y^{M_{0}}\phi_{p}(u,\phi)\right)

(247)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2q\partial_{q}\partial_{Y}\tilde{f}(q,Y)-2\frac{1+q}{1-q}\partial_{Y}\tilde{f}(q,Y)+\frac{q}{(1-q)^{2}}(\mathcal{M}_{1}\mathscr{L}_{-1}+\mathscr{L}_{1}\mathcal{M}_{-1})\tilde{f}(q,Y)

(249)

\displaystyle+Y\frac{q(1+q)}{(1-q)^{3}}\mathcal{M}_{1}\mathcal{M}_{-1}\tilde{f}(q,Y).

Translational invariance of
\tilde{f}(q,Y)
gives us
\mathcal{M}_{0}\tilde{f}(q,Y)=\mathcal{M}_{0}^{2}\tilde{f}(q,Y)=\mathcal{M}_{0}\mathscr{L}_{0}\tilde{f}(q,Y)=0
. Thus, the above equation is same as

\displaystyle 2q\partial_{q}\partial_{Y}\tilde{f}(q,Y)-2\frac{1+q}{1-q}\partial_{Y}\tilde{f}(q,Y)+\frac{q}{(1-q)^{2}}(-2\mathcal{M}_{0}\mathscr{L}_{0}+\mathcal{M}_{1}\mathscr{L}_{-1}+\mathscr{L}_{1}\mathcal{M}_{-1}+2\mathcal{M}_{0})\tilde{f}(q,Y)

(250)

\displaystyle+Y\frac{q(1+q)}{(1-q)^{3}}(-\mathcal{M}_{0}^{2}+\mathcal{M}_{1}\mathcal{M}_{-1})f(q,Y)=2\xi_{A}(\Delta_{A}-1)f(q,y).

(251)

Then we substitute

\displaystyle(\mathcal{M}_{0}^{2}-\mathcal{M}_{1}\mathcal{M}_{-1})\tilde{f}(q,Y)=\xi_{p}^{2}\tilde{f}(q,Y)

(252)

\displaystyle(2\mathcal{M}_{0}\mathscr{L}_{0}-\mathcal{M}_{1}\mathscr{L}_{-1}-\mathscr{L}_{1}\mathcal{M}_{-1}-2\mathcal{M}_{0})\tilde{f}(q,Y)=2\xi_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1)\tilde{f}(q,Y),

(253)

in the above equation to obtain

q\partial_{q}\partial_{Y}\tilde{f}(q,Y)-\frac{1+q}{1-q}\partial_{Y}\tilde{f}(q,Y)-\left(\frac{q}{(1-q)^{2}}\xi_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1)+Y\frac{q(1+q)}{(1-q)^{3}}\frac{\xi_{p}^{2}}{2}\right)\tilde{f}(q,y)=\xi_{A}(\Delta_{A}-1)\tilde{f}(q,Y).

(254)

Substituting
\tilde{f}(q,Y)=\frac{q}{(1-q)^{2}}f(q,Y)
, the differential equation simplifies to

q\partial_{q}\partial_{Y}f(q,Y)-\left(\frac{q}{(1-q)^{2}}\xi_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1)+Y\frac{q(1+q)}{(1-q)^{3}}\frac{\xi_{p}^{2}}{2}\right)f(q,y)=\xi_{A}(\Delta_{A}-1)f(q,Y).

(255)

Now

\partial_{Y}f(q,Y)=\sqrt{\xi_{A}^{2}+\xi_{p}^{2}\frac{q}{(1-q)^{2}}}f(q,Y)\equiv\tilde{A}(q)e^{Y\sqrt{\xi_{A}^{2}+\xi_{p}^{2}\frac{q}{(1-q)^{2}}}},

(256)

where

\tilde{A}(q)=\sqrt{\xi_{A}^{2}+\xi_{p}^{2}\frac{q}{(1-q)^{2}}}A(q).

(257)

Then we have

q\partial_{q}\partial_{Y}f(q,Y)=\frac{Yq\left(1+q\right)\xi_{p}^{2}e^{Y\sqrt{\xi_{A}^{2}+\frac{q\xi_{p}^{2}}{\left(1-q\right)^{2}}}}}{\left(1-q\right)^{3}\left(2\sqrt{\xi_{A}^{2}+\frac{q\xi_{p}^{2}}{\left(1-q\right)^{2}}}\right)}\tilde{A}(q)+e^{Y\sqrt{\xi_{A}^{2}+\xi_{p}^{2}\frac{q}{(1-q)^{2}}}}\,q\partial_{q}\tilde{A}(q)

(258)

Using this, the differential equation (
255
) further simplifies to

q\partial_{q}\tilde{A}(q)-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\xi_{A}^{2}+\xi_{p}^{2}\frac{q}{(1-q)^{2}}}}\left(\frac{q}{(1-q)^{2}}\xi_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1)+\xi_{A}(\Delta_{A}-1)\right)\tilde{A}(q)=0.

(259)

Let
\tilde{A}(q)=G(q)\tilde{B}(q)
such that

q\partial_{q}G(q)=\frac{q}{(1-q)^{2}\sqrt{\xi_{p}^{2}+\xi_{p}^{2}\frac{q}{(1-q)^{2}}}}\xi_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1)G(q).

(260)

We can solve this to find
G(q)
which is given by

\displaystyle G(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K_{1}\left(2\sqrt{\xi_{A}^{2}+\frac{q\xi_{p}^{2}}{(1-q)^{2}}}+\xi_{p}\frac{1+q}{1-q}\right)^{\Delta_{p}-1},

(261)

where
K_{1}
is a constant.
Substituting
\tilde{A}(q)=G(q)\tilde{B}(q)\tilde{B}(q)
in (
259
), we get the differential equation for
\tilde{B}(q)

q\partial_{q}\tilde{B}(q)-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\xi_{A}^{2}+\xi_{p}^{2}\frac{q}{(1-q)^{2}}}}\xi_{A}(\Delta_{A}-1)\tilde{B}(q)=0,

(262)

whose solution is

\tilde{B}(q)=K_{2}\xi_{p}^{2(-\Delta_{A}+1)}\left(\sqrt{(1-q)^{2}\xi_{A}^{2}+q\xi_{p}^{2}}+(q+1)\xi_{A}\right)^{2(-\Delta_{A}+1)}q^{\Delta_{A}-1}

(263)

Combining all our results we have

\displaystyle\tilde{f}(q,y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{Kq}{(1-q)^{2}\sqrt{\xi_{A}^{2}+\xi_{p}^{2}\frac{q}{(1-q)^{2}}}}y^{\sqrt{\xi_{A}^{2}+\xi_{p}^{2}\frac{q}{(1-q)^{2}}}}\left(2\sqrt{\xi_{A}^{2}+\frac{q\xi_{p}^{2}}{(1-q)^{2}}}+\xi_{p}\frac{1+q}{1-q}\right)^{\Delta_{p}-1}

(265)

\displaystyle\times\,\left(\sqrt{(1-q)^{2}\xi_{A}^{2}+q\xi_{p}^{2}}+(q+1)\xi_{A}\right)^{-2(\Delta_{A}-1)}q^{\Delta_{A}-1}.

Finally we would like to find the integration constant
K
. We will do this by expanding the above solution around
q=0
and compare the first few terms with the contribution from level
0
and
1
to
\tilde{f}(q,y)
. The expansion of (
) up to order in
q^{1}
(mod
q^{\Delta_{A}}
) is given by

K\,2^{2-2\Delta_{A}}\xi_{A}^{1-2\Delta_{A}}\left(2\xi_{A}+\xi_{p}\right){}^{\Delta_{p}-1}q^{\Delta_{A}}y^{\xi_{A}}\left(1+q\left(2+\frac{\xi_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1)}{\xi_{A}}+\pi i\rho\frac{\xi_{p}^{2}}{\xi_{A}}-\frac{\Delta_{A}}{2\xi_{A}^{2}}\xi_{p}^{2}\right)+\mathcal{O}(q^{2})\right).

(267)

Each level comes with a factor of
q^{N+\Delta_{A}}
and the contribution of level 0 and level 1 to the global block
\tilde{f}(q,y)
is

C_{ApA}\,q^{0}q^{\Delta_{A}}y^{\xi_{A}}+C_{ApA}\,q^{\Delta_{A}+1}y^{\xi_{A}}\left(2+\frac{\xi_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1)}{\xi_{A}}+\pi i\rho\frac{\xi_{p}^{2}}{\xi_{A}}-\frac{\Delta_{A}}{2\xi_{A}^{2}}\xi_{p}^{2}\right).

(268)

Comparing this with the above equation we can see that

K=C_{ApA}\,4^{\Delta_{A}-1}\xi_{A}^{2\Delta_{A}-1}\left(2\xi_{A}+\xi_{p}\right){}^{1-\Delta_{p}}.

(269)

Substituting this back in (
), the form of the global torus block is given by

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{\Delta_{A},\xi_{A},c_{L},c_{M}}^{\Delta_{p},\xi_{p}}(q,y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{ApA}\,4^{\Delta_{A}-1}\xi_{A}^{2\Delta_{A}-1}\left(2\xi_{A}+\xi_{p}\right){}^{1-\Delta_{p}}\left(\sqrt{(1-q)^{2}\xi_{A}^{2}+q\xi_{p}^{2}}+(q+1)\xi_{A}\right)^{-2(\Delta_{A}-1)}

(271)

\displaystyle\times\frac{q^{\Delta_{A}}}{(1-q)^{2}\sqrt{\xi_{A}^{2}+\xi_{p}^{2}\frac{q}{(1-q)^{2}}}}y^{\sqrt{\xi_{A}^{2}+\xi_{p}^{2}\frac{q}{(1-q)^{2}}}}\left(2\sqrt{\xi_{A}^{2}+\frac{q\xi_{p}^{2}}{(1-q)^{2}}}+\xi_{p}\frac{1+q}{1-q}\right)^{\Delta_{p}-1}

This is our final expression for the global BMS torus block, or the Minkowski torus block.

Before ending this subsection, we would like to point out that there is some potential subtlety between the BMS global blocks and the large
c_{M}
limit of the BMS blocks. The expectation from 2d CFT on general grounds is that in the large central charge limit, since the norm of the states at level 2 and higher depend inversely on the central charge, the Virasoro blocks would reduce to the global blocks. However, as shown in
Alkalaev:2016fok
, this is not true for the torus CFT in contrast to the CFT on the sphere. One may introduce the so-called “light blocks” which are defined as the leading approximation in
1/c
expansion. On the other hand, by truncating Virasoro algebra to its global
sl(2)
sub-algebra one is led to global block. On the sphere, these two functions are equal, while on torus and higher genus surfaces these are different. It is likely that BMS torus blocks share the same property, i.e. one generally distinguishes between BMS global and BMS light blocks for field theories defined on higher genus surfaces. We thank Konstantin Alkalaev for pointing this out to us. We hope to come back to this issue in the future and resolve it.

7
Structure constants for BMS highest weight primaries

We derived expressions for the BMS torus blocks in the previous section. Armed with this knowledge, we now move on to calculating the asymptotic behaviour of three-point structure constants
C_{ApA}
for primary fields in the highest weight representations. We will evaluate this in the large
\xi_{A}
limit, which was also the limit we used to derive the expressions of the BMS torus blocks.

We note that
C_{ApA}
is the coordinate independent constant in the three point function
\langle\phi_{\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}}(t_{1},x_{1})\phi_{p}(t_{2},x_{2})\phi_{\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}}(t_{3},x_{3})\rangle
. The analysis in this section will closely follow to the one used in Section
4.2
. We will find the asymptotic formula for a generic primary field
\phi_{p}
. First of all let us rewrite the S-modular transformation (
53
) as

\displaystyle\langle\phi_{\Delta,\xi}\rangle_{(\sigma,\rho)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma^{-\Delta}e^{\frac{\xi\rho}{\sigma}}\left<\phi_{\Delta,\xi}\right>_{(-\frac{1}{\sigma},\frac{\rho}{\sigma^{2}})}.

(273)

We expand the left hand side (LHS) of (
273
) in terms of the torus blocks of the primaries, where as the right hand side (RHS) of (
273
) is expanded using the eigenbasis of
L_{0}
and
M_{0}
as in Section
4.2
. This leads to

\displaystyle e^{-2\pi i(\sigma c_{L}/2+\rho c_{M}/2)}\sum_{A}D(\Delta_{A},\xi_{A})e^{2\pi i\sigma\Delta_{A}}e^{2\pi i\rho\xi_{A}}C_{ApA}\,\mathcal{F}_{\Delta_{A},\xi_{A},c_{L},c_{M}}^{\Delta_{p},\xi_{p}}(\sigma,\rho)

\displaystyle\quad=\sigma^{-\Delta_{p}}e^{\xi_{p}\frac{\rho}{\sigma}}\sum_{i}D(\Delta_{i},\xi_{i})C_{ipi}e^{-\frac{2\pi i}{\sigma}(\Delta_{i}-\frac{c_{L}}{2})}e^{2\pi i\frac{\rho}{\sigma^{2}}(\xi_{i}-\frac{c_{M}}{2})}.

(274)

We will choose
\sigma=i\beta
and consider the limit
\beta\rightarrow 0^{+}
and
|\rho|\rightarrow 0^{+}
. In this limit, as before, we replace the LHS by a double integral while the RHS is dominated by contribution from the lightest field
\chi
such that
C_{\chi p\chi}\neq 0
:

\displaystyle e^{2\pi\beta\frac{c_{L}}{2}}e^{-2\pi i\rho c_{M}/2}\int d\Delta_{A}\int d\xi_{A}T_{p}(\Delta_{A},\xi_{A})e^{-2\pi\beta\Delta_{A}}e^{2\pi i\rho\xi_{A}}\,\mathcal{F}_{\Delta_{A},\xi_{A},c_{L},c_{M}}^{\Delta_{p},\xi_{p}}(i\beta,\rho)

\displaystyle\quad\approx(i\beta)^{-\Delta_{p}}e^{-i\xi_{p}\frac{\rho}{\beta}}T_{p}(\Delta_{\chi},\xi_{\chi})e^{-\frac{2\pi}{\beta}(\Delta_{\chi}-\frac{c_{L}}{2})}e^{-2\pi i\frac{\rho}{\beta^{2}}(\xi_{\chi}-\frac{c_{M}}{2})}.

(275)

Here
T_{i}(\Delta_{A},\xi_{A})\equiv D(\Delta_{i},\xi_{i})C_{ipi}
. For small
\beta
, the LHS is dominated by torus blocks with large
\xi_{A}
. So, to a good approximation, we may replace the integral by integral over only large
\xi_{A}
. From (
182
), for large
\xi_{A}
we have

\mathcal{F}_{\Delta_{A},\xi_{A},c_{L},c_{M}}^{\Delta_{p},\xi_{p}}(\sigma,\rho)=\frac{q^{\frac{1}{12}}}{\eta(\sigma)^{2}}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\xi_{A}}\right)+\ldots\Rightarrow\mathcal{F}_{\Delta_{A},\xi_{A},c_{L},c_{M}}^{\Delta_{p},\xi_{p}}(i\beta,\rho)=\frac{e^{-\frac{2\pi\beta}{12}}}{\eta(i\beta)^{2}}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\xi_{A}}\right)+\ldots

(276)

For the special case of
\xi_{p}=0
, as described in Sec
6.2.5
, there are no subleading corrections to the block. For a generic
\xi_{p}\neq 0
, we will work also in the leading
\xi_{A}
approximation. So we will keep only the first term in the above equation and neglect the
\mathcal{O}(\frac{1}{\xi_{A}})
term and other higher order terms in
\xi_{A}
. For this to be possible i.e., for the subleading and other higher order terms to be suppressed in comparison to the leading term, we need the additional requirement that
\beta^{2}\xi_{A}\gg 1
. This is shown in Appendix
K
.

Next, we use the small
\beta
asymptotic of the Dedekind eta-function to obtain

\mathcal{F}_{\Delta_{A},\xi_{A},c_{L},c_{M}}^{\Delta_{p},\xi_{p}}(i\beta,\rho)\approx 2\pi\beta\exp\left\{-\frac{\pi}{6}\left(\beta-\frac{1}{\beta}\right)\right\}+...

(277)

Substituting the above expression in (
275
), we have

\displaystyle e^{2\pi\beta\frac{c_{L}}{2}}e^{-2\pi i\rho\frac{c_{M}}{2}}2\pi\beta e^{\frac{\pi}{6}\left(-\beta+\frac{1}{\beta}\right)}\int d\Delta_{A}\int d\xi_{A}T_{p}(\Delta_{A},\xi_{A})e^{-2\pi\beta\Delta_{A}}e^{2\pi i\rho\xi_{A}}

\displaystyle\quad\approx(i\beta)^{-\Delta_{p}}e^{-i\xi_{p}\frac{\rho}{\beta}}\ T_{p}(\Delta_{\chi},\xi_{\chi})e^{-\frac{2\pi}{\beta}(\Delta_{\chi}-\frac{c_{L}}{2})}e^{-2\pi i\frac{\rho}{\beta^{2}}(\xi_{\chi}-\frac{c_{M}}{2})}

This leads to

\displaystyle\int d\Delta_{A}^{\prime}\int d\xi_{A}^{\prime}\ T_{p}(\Delta_{A}^{\prime},\xi_{A}^{\prime})e^{-2\pi\beta\Delta_{A}^{\prime}}e^{2\pi i\rho\xi_{A}^{\prime}}\approx

\displaystyle\frac{\beta^{-\Delta_{p}-1}}{2\pi i^{\Delta_{p}}}T_{p}(\Delta_{\chi},\xi_{\chi})e^{2\pi\beta(-\frac{c_{L}}{2}+\frac{1}{12})}

(278)

\displaystyle\times e^{-2\pi i\rho(\frac{1}{\beta^{2}}(\xi_{\chi}-\frac{c_{M}}{2})-\frac{c_{M}}{2}+\frac{\xi_{p}}{2\pi\beta})}e^{-\frac{2\pi}{\beta}(\Delta_{\chi}-\frac{c_{L}}{2}+\frac{1}{12})}.

We multiply both sides with
e^{-2\pi i\xi_{A}(\text{Re}\,\rho)}
and integrate over real part of
\rho
. On the LHS we have a
\delta(\xi_{A}^{\prime}-\xi_{A})
which when integrated over
\xi_{A}^{\prime}
will give us

{\text{LHS}}=\int d\Delta_{A}^{\prime}T_{p}(\Delta_{A}^{\prime},\xi_{A})e^{-2\pi\beta\Delta_{A}^{\prime}}e^{-2\pi(\text{Im}\,\rho)\xi_{A}}.

On the RHS, the integral over
(\text{Re}\,\rho)
will give us a delta-function. Specifically, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}d\Delta_{A}^{\prime}T_{p}(\Delta_{A}^{\prime},\xi_{A})e^{-2\pi\beta\Delta_{A}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\frac{\beta^{-\Delta_{p}-1}}{2\pi i^{\Delta_{p}}}e^{2\pi\beta(-\frac{c_{L}}{2}+\frac{1}{12})}e^{-\frac{2\pi}{\beta}(\Delta_{\chi}-\frac{c_{L}}{2}+\frac{1}{12})}\ T_{p}(\Delta_{\chi},\xi_{\chi})

(279)

\displaystyle{\hskip-56.9055pt}\times e^{2\pi(\text{Im}\,\rho)(\frac{1}{\beta^{2}}(\xi_{\chi}-\frac{c_{M}}{2})-\frac{c_{M}}{2}+\frac{\xi_{p}}{2\pi\beta}+\xi_{A})}\delta\left\{\frac{1}{\beta^{2}}(\xi_{\chi}-\frac{c_{M}}{2})-\frac{c_{M}}{2}+\frac{\xi_{p}}{2\pi\beta}+\xi_{A}\right\}.

The two roots of the argument of the
\delta
-function are

\beta_{\pm}=\frac{-\frac{\xi_{p}}{2\pi}\pm\sqrt{\left(\frac{\xi_{p}}{2\pi}\right)^{2}+4\left(\frac{c_{M}}{2}-\xi_{\chi}\right)\left(\xi_{A}-\frac{c_{M}}{2}\right)}}{2\left(\xi_{A}-\frac{c_{M}}{2}\right)}.

(280)

Using the property of the
\delta
-function we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}d\Delta_{A}^{\prime}T_{p}(\Delta_{A}^{\prime},\xi_{A})e^{-2\pi\beta\Delta_{A}^{\prime}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{*=+,-}\frac{\beta^{-\Delta_{p}-1}}{2\pi i^{\Delta_{p}}}e^{2\pi\beta_{*}(-\frac{c_{L}}{2}+\frac{1}{12})-\frac{2\pi}{\beta_{*}}(\Delta_{\chi}-\frac{c_{L}}{2}+\frac{1}{12})}T_{p}(\Delta_{\chi},\xi_{\chi})

(281)

\displaystyle\qquad\times e^{2\pi(\text{Im}\,\rho)}\frac{\delta(\beta-\beta_{\star})}{|\frac{c_{M}-2\xi_{\chi}}{\beta_{\star}^{3}}-\frac{\xi_{p}}{2\pi\beta_{\star}^{2}}|}.

We then invert the above Laplace-transformation using the analytic continuation of the
\delta
-function (
94
) to obtain

\displaystyle\frac{T_{p}(\Delta_{A},\xi_{A})}{T_{p}(\Delta_{\chi},\xi_{\chi})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{*=+,-}\frac{\beta^{-\Delta_{p}-1}}{2\pi i^{\Delta_{p}}}{\frac{e^{2\pi\beta_{*}(-\frac{c_{L}}{2}+\frac{1}{12})}e^{-\frac{2\pi}{\beta_{*}}(\Delta_{\chi}-\frac{c_{L}}{2}+\frac{1}{12})}}{|\frac{c_{M}-2\xi_{\chi}}{\beta_{\star}^{3}}-\frac{\xi_{p}}{2\pi\beta_{\star}^{2}}|}}\int_{b-i\infty}^{b+i\infty}\frac{d\beta}{i}\frac{e^{2\pi\beta\Delta_{A}^{\prime}}}{2\pi i(\beta-\beta^{*})}

(282)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{*=+,-}\frac{1}{2\pi}(i\beta_{*})^{-\Delta_{p}-1}e^{2\pi\beta_{*}(-\frac{c_{L}}{2}+\frac{1}{12})}e^{-\frac{2\pi}{\beta_{*}}(\Delta_{\chi}-\frac{c_{L}}{2}+\frac{1}{12})}\frac{e^{2\pi\beta_{*}\Delta_{A}}}{|\frac{c_{M}-2\xi_{\chi}}{\beta_{\star}^{3}}-\frac{\xi_{p}}{2\pi\beta_{\star}^{2}}|}.

(283)

Note that we start out with the condition that
\beta
is small and
\xi_{A}
is large. Indeed, from (
280
) we see that for
\xi_{A}\gg\frac{\xi_{p}}{2\pi}\pm\frac{c_{M}}{2}
,
\beta_{\pm}
is small. We also assume
\beta
to be real for which we require
\xi_{\chi}<\frac{c_{M}}{2}
. In these limit

\beta_{\pm}\approx-\frac{\xi_{p}}{4\pi\xi_{A}}\pm\sqrt{\frac{{c_{M}}-2\xi_{\chi}}{2\xi_{A}}}.

We also require the contribution from
\beta_{+}
to dominate and the contribution from
\beta_{-}
to be negligible. This is achieved by the condition that

\Delta_{\chi}<\frac{c_{L}}{2}-\frac{1}{12}.

So, we have

\displaystyle\frac{T_{p}(\Delta_{A},\xi_{A})}{T_{p}(\Delta_{\chi},\xi_{\chi})}

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\frac{(-i)^{\Delta_{p}+1}}{\beta_{+}^{\Delta_{P}-1}}e^{2\pi\beta_{+}(\Delta_{A}-\frac{c_{L}}{2}+\frac{1}{12})}e^{\frac{2\pi}{\beta_{+}}(\frac{c_{L}}{2}-\Delta_{\chi}-\frac{1}{12})}\frac{1}{\frac{c_{M}-2\xi_{\chi}}{\beta_{+}}-\frac{\xi_{p}}{2\pi}}.

(285)

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\frac{(-i)^{\Delta_{p}+1}}{2\xi_{A}}\left(\frac{\xi_{A}}{\frac{c_{M}}{2}-\xi_{\chi}}\right)^{\frac{\Delta_{p}}{2}}e^{\frac{\xi_{p}}{2}\left(-\frac{\Delta_{A}-\frac{c_{L}}{2}+\frac{1}{12}}{\xi_{A}}-\frac{\frac{c_{L}}{2}-\Delta_{\chi}-\frac{1}{12}}{\frac{c_{M}}{2}-\xi_{\chi}}\right)}

(287)

\displaystyle\times e^{2\pi\left(\sqrt{\frac{\frac{c_{M}}{2}-\xi_{\chi}}{\xi_{A}}}(\Delta_{A}-\frac{c_{L}}{2}+\frac{1}{12})+\sqrt{\frac{\xi_{A}}{\frac{c_{M}}{2}-\xi_{\chi}}})(\frac{c_{L}}{2}-\Delta_{\chi}-\frac{1}{12})\right)}.

To find the asymptotic formula for density of states in the large
\xi_{A}
limit, we put
\Delta_{p}=0=\xi_{p}
directly in (
287
) and also replace the primary field
\chi
by the lightest primary field
\mathcal{O}
present in the theory

\displaystyle\frac{D(\Delta_{A},\xi_{A})}{D(\Delta_{\mathcal{O}},\xi_{\mathcal{O}})}

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle-\frac{i}{4\pi\xi_{A}}e^{2\pi\left[(\Delta_{A}-\frac{c_{L}}{2}+\frac{1}{12})\sqrt{\frac{\frac{c_{M}}{2}-\xi_{\mathcal{O}}}{\xi_{A}}}+(\frac{c_{L}}{2}-\Delta_{\mathcal{O}}-\frac{1}{12})\sqrt{\frac{\xi_{A}}{\frac{c_{M}}{2}-\xi_{\mathcal{O}}}}\right]}.

(288)

It is satisfying to note that in the limit
\Delta_{A}\gg c_{L}
and with
\mathcal{O}
as the vacuum (so
\Delta_{\mathcal{O}}=\xi_{\mathcal{O}}=0
), taking the logarithm of the above equation (
288
), we reproduce the Cardy formula for BMS primaries, which was worked out in the saddle point method in
Bagchi:2019unf
.
Substituting (
288
) back in (
287
) we obtain the asymptotic formula of three-point coefficient for primary fields for large
\xi_{A}

\displaystyle C_{ApA}

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle(-i)^{\Delta_{p}}C_{\chi A\chi}\frac{D(\Delta_{\chi},\xi_{\chi})}{D(\Delta_{\mathcal{O}},\xi_{\mathcal{O}})}\left(\frac{\xi_{A}}{\frac{c_{M}}{2}-\xi_{\chi}}\right)^{\frac{\Delta_{p}}{2}}e^{\frac{\xi_{p}}{2}\left(-\frac{\Delta_{A}-\frac{c_{L}}{2}+\frac{1}{12}}{\xi_{A}}-\frac{\frac{c_{L}}{2}-\Delta_{\chi}-\frac{1}{12}}{\frac{c_{M}}{2}-\xi_{\chi}}\right)}\times

(289)

\displaystyle e^{2\pi\left(\sqrt{\frac{\xi_{A}}{\frac{c_{M}}{2}-\xi_{\chi}}}(\frac{c_{L}}{2}-\frac{1}{12}-\Delta_{\chi})-\sqrt{\frac{\xi_{A}}{\frac{c_{M}}{2}-\xi_{\mathcal{O}}}}(\frac{c_{L}}{2}-\frac{1}{12}-\Delta_{\mathcal{O}})+(\sqrt{\frac{\frac{c_{M}}{2}-\xi_{\chi}}{\xi_{A}}}-\sqrt{\frac{\frac{c_{M}}{2}-\xi_{\mathcal{O}}}{\xi_{A}}})(\Delta_{A}+\frac{1}{12}-\frac{c_{L}}{2})\right)}.

If we consider the limit where
c_{M}\gg\xi_{\chi},\xi_{\mathcal{O}}
, then we see that the asymptotic structure constants for the BMS primaries matches the general analysis we have considered in earlier sections (e.g. comparing (
289
) and (
101
)), with the identification

c_{L}\to c_{L}-\frac{1}{6}.

(290)

The shift in central charge due to descendants is similar to that which has been observed in 2d CFTs. This shift can be viewed as a one-loop renormalisation of the bulk central charge due to the presence of BMS descendants. It is interesting to see that the central term
c_{M}
does not get shifted. This is reminiscent of the recent analysis of
Merbis:2019wgk
, where the authors also found a one-loop shift of only
c_{L}
and not
c_{M}
. In fact the shift of the central charge
c_{L}
found in
Merbis:2019wgk
exactly matches with our result above (
290
) (after accounting for the difference in normalisations between our work and
Merbis:2019wgk
).
9
9

9

The authors in
Merbis:2019wgk
speak about two different shifts in the central extension
c_{L}
corresponding to two different classes of co-adjoint orbits of the BMS group, viz. the vacuum orbit and a generic orbit. The case we are interested in is the generic orbit since on the boundary side we are interested in thermal states and in the bulk dual we deal with the FSC solution, and not the vacuum Minkowski solution.

8
A Quick Bulk analysis

8.1
Flatspace Cosmologies

In a holographic duality, the thermal states on the field theory are equivalent to geometries with horizons in the bulk dual. For a 2d CFT, thermal states are dual to BTZ black holes in AdS
3
. For non-extremal BTZ solutions, the metric is given by

ds^{2}_{\text{\tiny BTZ}}=-\frac{(r^{2}-r_{+}^{2})(r^{2}-r_{-}^{2})}{r^{2}\ell^{2}}dt^{2}+\frac{r^{2}\ell^{2}}{(r^{2}-r_{+}^{2})(r^{2}-r_{-}^{2})}dr^{2}+r^{2}\left(d\phi-\frac{r_{+}r_{-}}{\ell r^{2}}dt\right)^{2}.

(291)

Here
r_{\pm}
are the outer and inner horizons, which are given in terms of the mass (
M
) and angular momentum (
J
) of the BTZ black hole by

r_{\pm}=\sqrt{2G\ell(\ell M+J)}\pm\sqrt{2G\ell(\ell M-J)}.

(292)

In the above,
\ell
is the radius of AdS
3
. BTZ black hole solutions are locally AdS
3
and can be viewed as orbifolds of AdS
3
.

The holographic duals of thermal states in 2d BMSFTs are the so-called Flat Space Cosmologies (FSC). The metric for these cosmological solutions are given by

ds^{2}_{\text{\tiny FSC${}_{1}$}}=Mdu^{2}-2dudr+Jdud\psi+r^{2}d\psi^{2}

(293)

Here
u
is the retarded time and
M
and
J
label the mass and angular momentum of these solutions. These are the zero modes of the most generic metric

ds^{2}_{\text{\tiny FSC${}_{1}$}}=\Theta(\phi)du^{2}-2dudr+\left[\Xi(\phi)+\partial_{\phi}\Theta(\phi)\right]dud\psi+r^{2}d\psi^{2}

(294)

for asymptotically flat boundary conditions for which one gets the BMS
3
algebra as the asymptotic symmetry algebra on the null boundary.

\Theta(\phi)
and
\Xi(\phi)
are functions called the mass and angular momentum aspects which reduce to
M
and
J/2
for the FSC solution. FSC solutions are locally flat and can also be obtained by quotienting 3d Minkowski spacetime by a boost and a translation. For this reason, they are also known as shifted-boost orbifolds
Cornalba:2003kd
. The Penrose diagram of FSC spacetimes is given in Figure
3
.

Figure 3:
Penrose diagram for Flat Space Cosmologies.

Minkowski spacetimes can be viewed as an infinite radius of AdS. One can similarly obtain the FSC solutions by starting with (
291
) and taking
\ell\to\infty
. The metric here, written in
(t,r,\phi)
coordinates, takes the form

ds^{2}_{\text{\tiny FSC${}_{2}$}}=\hat{r}^{2}_{+}dt^{2}-\frac{r^{2}}{\hat{r}^{2}_{+}(r^{2}-r_{0}^{2})}dr^{2}+2\hat{r}_{+}r_{0}d\phi dt+r^{2}d\phi^{2},

(295)

where

\hat{r}_{+}=\sqrt{8GM},\,\,\,r_{0}=\sqrt{\frac{2G}{M}}J.

(296)

Going from (
293
) to (
295
) is done by the following change of variables:

d\psi=d\phi+\frac{r_{0}dr}{\hat{r}_{+}(r^{2}-r_{0}^{2})},\quad du=dt+\frac{r^{2}dr}{\hat{r}_{+}^{2}(r^{2}-r_{0}^{2})}

(297)

The cosmological nature of the metric (
295
) manifests itself in the limit. When
\ell\to\infty
, the outer horizon of the original BTZ black hole goes out to infinity leaving behind just the interior of the black hole as the whole solution. Here thus the roles of the radial and temporal directions flip. The solution (
295
) is dependent on
r
, which in this case is the temporal direction.
r=r_{0}
is the remnant of the inner BTZ horizon that turns into a cosmological horizon in this singular limit.

To understand the cosmological nature of the FSC intrinsically without the aid of a limiting procedure, it is best to look at another coordinate system where the metric can be rewritten as

ds^{2}_{\text{\tiny FSC${}_{3}$}}=-d\tau^{2}+\frac{(E\tau)^{2}}{1+(E\tau)^{2}}dx^{2}+\left(1+(E\tau)^{2}\right)\left(dy+\frac{(E\tau)^{2}}{1+(E\tau)^{2}}dx\right)^{2}

(298)

In the above,
y
is identified as
y\sim y+2\pi r_{0}
. It can be checked that the above is a solution to vacuum Einstein’s equation in three dimensions with a vanishing cosmological constant. The obvious temporal dependence makes this a cosmological solution. For positive
\tau
, (
298
) describes an expanding universe from a cosmological horizon at
\tau=0
. The map between (
298
) and (
295
) is the following

\hat{r}_{+}t=x,\quad r_{0}\phi=y+x,\quad\left(\frac{r}{r_{0}}\right)^{2}=1+(E\tau)^{2},\,\text{with}\,E=\frac{\hat{r}_{+}}{r_{0}}.

(299)

For our calculations in the next subsection, we will focus on using (
295
) as our metric of choice.

8.2
One point functions in FSC background

We now wish to calculate
\langle E|O|E\rangle
, for high energy
E
, from the bulk side. The dual to the finite temperature BMSFT thermal state is a FSC solution. The BMS weights of the FSC are

\xi_{\text{\tiny FSC}}=M+\frac{c_{M}}{2},\,\,\,\Delta_{\text{\tiny FSC}}=J+\frac{c_{L}}{2}.

(300)

We also know that for 3d Einstein gravity
c_{L}=0,\,\,c_{M}=\frac{1}{4G}
. For large
M
and
J
, we have

\xi_{\text{\tiny FSC}}\approx M,\,\,\,r_{0}\approx\frac{\Delta_{\text{\tiny FSC}}}{\sqrt{2c_{M}\xi_{\text{\tiny FSC}}}},\,\,\,\hat{r}_{+}\approx\sqrt{\frac{2\xi_{\text{\tiny FSC}}}{c_{M}}}.

(301)

It is of interest to note here that with these identifications of the BMS weights of the FSC, the Bekenstein-Hawking entropy associated with the cosmological horizon matches precisely with the BMS-Cardy formula (
28
) presented in Sec. (
2
)
Bagchi:2012xr
:

S_{\text{\tiny{Bekenstein-Hawking}}}=\frac{\text{Area of horizon}}{4G}=\frac{2\pi r_{0}}{4G}=S_{\mbox{\tiny{BMS-Cardy}}}.

(302)

We now wish to take this correspondence one step further and match the asymptotic formula for the structure constants that we have obtained to a bulk analysis.

Our object of interest,
\langle E|O|E\rangle
, in the bulk side denotes the calculation of a one-point function of a light operator
O
, or a probe, in the background of a heavy state
|E\rangle
, which is given by a FSC solution. Of course, we don’t expect a single microstate to have a geometric description, so in a sense the FSC geometry arises on course graining over a family of such microstates. We are working in the probe limit and hence we will discount the backreaction of
O
on the FSC geometry.

Figure 4:
One-loop contribution to
\langle E|O|E\rangle

We are interested in the contribution to
\langle E|O|E\rangle
that comes from the diagram shown in Figure
4
. The field
\phi_{O}
comes from boundary at infinity and splits into a pair of
\phi_{\chi}
which wraps around the cosmological horizon
r_{0}
. We are working in the probe limit where
\xi_{O},\xi_{\chi}\gg 1
but these fields are light in the sense that
\xi_{O},\xi_{\chi}\ll c_{M}
. We also have
\xi_{\text{\tiny FSC}}\gg c_{M}
. Even in the case of asymptotically flat spacetimes, for bulk scalar field with mass
m
, the two point function is given by
e^{-mL}
, where
L
is the length of the geodesic connecting the two points
Hijano:2017eii
. So, for
\phi_{\chi}
, the contribution to the one loop diagram is given by

\exp({-\xi_{\chi}2\pi r_{0}})\approx\exp\left({-\frac{2\pi\xi_{\chi}\Delta_{\text{\tiny FSC}}}{\sqrt{2\xi_{\text{\tiny FSC}}c_{M}}}}\right).

(303)

In the above equation we make use of the fact that for a primary field
(\Delta_{b},\xi_{b})
on the boundary, the corresponding field in the bulk has a mass
\xi_{b}
. This follows from a matching of Casimirs from the point of the bulk and the boundary.

Now let us calculate the contribution from the geodesic along the
r
direction
r=r_{0}
to
r=\infty
. In general, the length of this geodesic from
r=0
to
r=\Lambda
is given by

\displaystyle L=\int_{r_{0}}^{\Lambda}\frac{rdr}{\hat{r}_{+}\sqrt{r^{2}-r_{0}^{2}}}=\frac{\sqrt{\Lambda^{2}-r_{0}^{2}}}{\hat{r}_{+}^{2}}\,\Rightarrow\log{L}=\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{\Lambda^{2}}{r_{0}^{2}}-1\right)-\log\left(\frac{\hat{r}_{+}}{r_{0}}\right).

(304)

In the above expression, we can see that
\log L
diverges when
\Lambda
and hence
\log\left(\frac{\Lambda}{r_{0}}-1\right)
goes to infinity. We regulate this it by removing the first term. The renormalized geodesic length is thus given by

{}\log L=-\log\left(\frac{\hat{r}_{+}}{r_{0}}\right)\implies L=\frac{r_{0}}{\hat{r}_{+}}\approx\frac{\Delta_{\text{\tiny FSC}}}{2\xi_{\text{\tiny FSC}}}.

(305)

So, the contribution of this geodesic to to the one-point function is given by

\exp(-\xi_{O}L)\approx\exp\left(-{\xi_{O}\frac{r_{0}}{\hat{r}_{+}}}\right)\approx\exp\left(-\frac{\xi_{O}\Delta_{\text{\tiny FSC}}}{2\xi_{\text{\tiny FSC}}}\right).

(306)

Combining the above calculations, we have

\boxed{\langle E|O|E\rangle\approx\langle\chi|O|\chi\rangle\exp\left(-{\frac{\xi_{O}\Delta_{\text{\tiny FSC}}}{2\xi_{\text{\tiny FSC}}}}-{\frac{2\pi\xi_{\chi}\Delta_{\text{\tiny FSC}}}{\sqrt{2\xi_{\text{\tiny FSC}}c_{M}}}}\right).}

(307)

This matches with the field theory calculation for asymptotic formula for three-point coefficient given in (
83
).

From the other field theory calculation in (
101
), we have

\displaystyle{\langle E|O|E}\rangle

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\frac{\rho(\chi)}{\rho(O)}C_{\chi O\chi}\left(\frac{\xi}{\frac{c_{M}}{2}-\xi_{\chi}}\right)^{\frac{\Delta_{O}-1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\xi}{\frac{c_{M}}{2}-\xi_{l}}}\,\,e^{\frac{\xi_{O}}{2}\left(-\frac{\Delta-\frac{c_{L}}{2}}{\xi}+\frac{\frac{c_{L}}{2}-\Delta_{\chi}}{\frac{c_{M}}{2}-\xi_{\chi}}\right)}

(308)

\displaystyle\times e^{2\pi\left(\sqrt{\frac{\xi}{\frac{c_{M}}{2}-\xi_{\chi}}}\left(\frac{c_{L}}{2}-\Delta_{\chi}\right)-\sqrt{\frac{\xi}{\frac{c_{M}}{2}-\xi_{l}}}\left(\frac{c_{L}}{2}-\Delta_{l}\right)+\left(\sqrt{\frac{\frac{c_{M}}{2}-\xi_{\chi}}{\xi}}-\sqrt{\frac{\frac{c_{M}}{2}-\xi_{l}}{\xi}}\right)\left(\Delta-\frac{c_{L}}{2}\right)\right)}.

Here
l
is the lightest field in the theory with weights
(\xi_{l},\Delta_{l})
. We will take this field to be the vacuum, so we have
\xi_{l}=\Delta_{l}=0
. Since we consider the case where the corresponding bulk fields
\phi_{O}
and
\phi_{\chi}
are scalars, we have
\Delta_{O}=\Delta_{\chi}=0
. For 3d Einstein gravity, we also have
c_{L}=0
. So, for these cases, (
308
) reduces to

\displaystyle\langle E|O|E\rangle

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\frac{\rho(\chi)}{\rho(O)}C_{\chi O\chi}\,\exp\left(-{\frac{\xi_{O}\Delta_{\text{\tiny FSC}}}{2\xi_{\text{\tiny FSC}}}}{-\frac{2\pi\xi_{\chi}\Delta_{\text{\tiny FSC}}}{\sqrt{2\xi_{\text{\tiny FSC}}c_{M}}}}\right),

(309)

matching with the bulk analysis answer (
307
).

9
Conclusions

9.1
Summary

Let us summarise for the reader what we have achieved in the current paper. We have been looking 2d field theories invariant under the BMS
3
algebra. These are theories which are putative duals to 3d asymptotically flat spacetimes.

We concentrated on modular properties of these 2d BMSFTs and specifically those of the torus one-point function. Using the BMS modular transformations of the torus one-point function, we computed an asymptotic formula for the three-point structure constants. To begin with, we used two distinct methods, one a saddle-point analysis and the other which hinged on inverting integral transformations, to obtain expressions for the structure constants. We found that the second method worked for a wider range of parameters than the saddle point method. Both methods were however blind to the exact states that the trace was performed on.

We then computed the asymptotic form of structure constants when the states were in the BMS highest weight representation. In order to do this, we needed to develop quite a lot of new machinery for these BMSFTs. A particularly important development was the derivation of the BMS torus blocks. We found expressions for the leading and subleading terms in the BMS torus blocks in a particular limit of large weights. These developments were put in use when we recomputed the torus one-point function for the BMS primaries.

Finally, we used a bulk analysis that depended on a geodesic approximation in terms of a probe scalar in the background of a flat space cosmological solution to reproduce the field theory results. Additionally, we have a large number of appendices with a lot of more technical details.

9.2
Discussions and Future Directions

We now turn to discuss various aspects and future directions of our findings in this paper.

More flat space holography

One of our principal objectives in this work has been to further the advancement of holography for 3d asymptotically flat spacetimes. We have added the asymptotic three-point structure constants to the list of quantities that now match between the bulk and the putative boundary theory. Our bulk analysis can be improved by looking at a Witten diagram like procedure instead of the geodesic approximation
Kraus:2016nwo
;
Kraus:2017ezw
. One can also look at the Chern-Simons formulation to understand this in a different way, following e.g.
Alkalaev:2020yvq
.

There are obvious generalisations to our work in this paper to theories with higher spin
Afshar:2013vka
;
Gonzalez:2013oaa
, and supersymmetric BMS theories (see e.g.
Barnich:2014cwa
;
Lodato:2016alv
;
Banerjee:2018hbl
), and other extensions of BMS e.g. with extra currents
Basu:2017aqn
.

An important direction is the generalisation to higher point functions on the torus. For the torus two point function, there are important applications. These would be connected to quasinormal modes in the dual bulk spacetime and to the eigenstate thermalisation hypothesis
Brehm:2018ipf
;
Romero-Bermudez:2018dim
;
Hikida:2018khg
in the context of BMSFTs
10
10

10

For further discussions of higher point torus functions for 2d CFTs and their holographic interpretation, the reader is pointed to
Alkalaev:2017bzx
.
.

The modular bootstrap programme in 2d CFTs initiated in
Hellerman:2009bu
helps constrain theories further. The S-modular transformation is particularly useful in this regard. A review of current developments is summarised in
Brehm:2019pcx
. A similar programme is currently being attempted currently for 2d BMSFTs. This is something we hope to report on in the near future.

It is of interest to see how the one-point torus amplitude calculation generalises in the case of Flatspace Chiral gravity (F
\chi
G)
Bagchi:2012yk
(and its supersymmetric cousin
Bagchi:2018ryy
). F
\chi
G relates a theory of Chern-Simons gravity with asymptotically flat boundary conditions to a chiral 2d conformal field theory. This theory admits FSC solutions. So it is natural to ponder what sort of bulk construction would reproduce what should be a chiral half of the Kraus-Maloney CFT answer.

Black holes in general dimensions

In
Carlip:2017xne
;
Carlip:2019dbu
, Carlip has argued that the algebra of horizon preserving diffeomorphisms are enhanced to form BMS
3
for generic black holes and has used the BMS-Cardy formula
Bagchi:2012xr
to derive the Bekenstein-Hawking entropy for these black holes. Along the same lines, it could thus be argued that the considerations of this paper would also be applicable to black holes in general dimensions. In particular, higher dimensional black holes will also get their “spots”
Kraus:2016nwo
through the mechanism elucidated in this paper.

Tensionless string theory and BMS modular invariance

BMS
3
algebra arises as residual gauge symmetries on the worldsheet of the tensionless bosonic closed string, in the equivalent of the conformal gauge
Isberg:1993av
;
Bagchi:2013bga
. There has been recent efforts in trying to formulate the theory of tensionless strings from the point of BMS symmetries, mirroring the development of usual string theory by methods of conformal symmetry. Our modular explorations are hence of importance when one attempts to understand tensionless string theory on higher genus surfaces. One of the principal aims of the programme initiated in
Bagchi:2013bga
;
Bagchi:2015nca
is the understanding of string amplitudes in this very high energy limit by purely worldsheet methods and comparing it to the seminal analysis of Gross and Mende
Gross:1987kza
;
Gross:1987ar
;
Gross:1988ue
. Modular properties of 2d BMSFTs would be central to understanding one loop amplitudes in this tensionless regime.

Relatedly, ambitwistor strings have been shown to be tensionless strings in disguise
Casali:2016atr
. Efforts in attempting to construct partition functions for these ambitwistor strings have been made
Casali:2017zkz
using the modular transformations we have worked extensively with in this work. It is conceivable that more can be understood for ambitwistor string theory using techniques developed in this paper.

Non-relativistic conformal symmetry

As remarked earlier, BMS
3
is isomorphic to the 2d Galilean conformal algebra (GCA)
Bagchi:2010zz
, which can be obtained by a non-relativistic limit on the parent relativistic conformal theory. This remarkable isomorphism means that all our computations in this paper are equally valid for non-relativistic conformal systems in 2d.

There has been recent interesting progress in the understanding of non-relativistic strong gravity
Hansen:2020pqs
, where non-relativistic black hole solutions have been discussed. Building on the initial proposal of a non-relativistic limit of AdS/CFT, one could put our results in context. Starting with AdS
3
/CFT
2
, following
Bagchi:2009my
, one would end up with a non-relativistic Newton-Cartan like AdS
{}_{2}\times{\rm I\!R}
which would be dual to a 2d Galilean CFT. There would presumably be solutions in the bulk corresponding to the BTZ black hole, which like the Schwarzschild solutions in
Hansen:2020pqs
would have horizons. The modular properties described in this paper would help reproduce the entropy and probe reactions to these solutions. It would be interesting to investigate this in further detail.

The 2d GCA also appears on non-relativistic string worldsheets corresponding to strings moving on target spaces with Newton-Cartan geometries
Harmark:2018cdl
. Again, properties of 2d field theories with these symmetries, particularly modular properties discussed in this paper, would be important there. Construction of partition functions and modular invariance in the context of these string theories would rely heavily on the things we have developed here.

Higher dimensional explorations

It would be very useful to try and generalise these considerations to higher dimensions. The general idea of holography stays the same, viz. we propose that the
d
-dimensional field theory would inherit the asymptotic symmetries of the
(d+1)
dimensional asymptotically flat bulk, which is the BMS
d+1
group
Bagchi:2016bcd
;
Bagchi:2019xfx

11
11

11

For other recent proposals, see e.g.
Ball:2019atb
;
Laddha:2020kvp
. See
Banerjee:2020kaa
;
Banerjee:2018gce
for interesting work in trying to understand scattering in four dimensional asymptotically flat spacetimes with a potential three dimensional dual building on the work relating scattering with a celestial 2d CFT in
Pasterski:2016qvg
;
Pasterski:2017kqt
.
. In particular, if some of these methods can be applied to 3d field theories with BMS
4
symmetries, we would be able to connect with quantities of very significant physical interest, S-matrix elements.
BMS
4
symmetries however are more involved and the structure of
\mathscr{I}^{\pm}
which is given by
{\rm I\!R}_{u}\times S^{2}
, now admits two copies of the (non-centrally extended) Virasoro algebra along with supertranslations
12
12

12

This is one of the proposed infinite dimensional extensions of BMS
4

Barnich:2010eb
. For another proposal, involving the group Diff
(S^{2})
, see
Campiglia:2014yka
.
. It is not entirely obvious how the methods described in this paper would generalise.

As we mentioned, the isomorphism between the BMS
3
and the GCA
2
means that the results we derived here are equally applicable to non-relativistic conformal systems. In higher dimensions, the structure of GCA
d
remains very similar to the
d=2
case, with only additional vectorial labels on the
M
generators and the existence of rotations. These symmetries have been shown to exist in non-relativistic versions of electrodynamics
Bagchi:2014ysa
;
Festuccia:2016caf
and Yang-Mills
Bagchi:2015qcw
, with arbitrary massless matter couplings as well
Bagchi:2017yvj
. Our methods developed in this paper may be more amenable to generalisation to higher dimensions in this context given the similar structures of the
d=2
case with the general case.

APPENDICES

Appendix A
Review of torus one-point function in 2d CFT

In this appendix, we are giving a review of large
h_{A}
limit of torus one point function for relativistic CFT
2
using mode expansion. Only for this section, we use the notation
L_{n},\bar{L}_{n}
to define the Virasoro generators giving the corresponding algebra.

{}\begin{split}[L_{n},L_{m}]=&(n-m)L_{n+m}+\frac{c}{12}n(n^{2}-1)\delta_{m+n,0}\\
[\bar{L}_{n},\bar{L}_{m}]=&(n-m)\bar{L}_{n+m}+\frac{\bar{c}}{12}n(n^{2}-1)\delta_{m+n,0}\end{split}

(310)

Mode expansion

It is well known that a conformal field
\phi_{p}(z,\bar{z})
of dimension
(h,\bar{h})
on the 2-d Euclidean plane may be mode-expanded as below:

\displaystyle\phi_{p}(z,\bar{z})=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}z^{-m-h}\bar{z}^{-n-\bar{h}}\phi_{m,n}.

(311)

The quasi-primary modes satisfy the usual Hermitian-conjugation relation (on the real surface):
