\displaystyle\phi^{\dagger}_{m,n}=\phi_{-m,-n}.

(312)

As the holomorphic and anti-holomorphic degrees of freedom of a CFT on a 2-d Euclidean plane decouples from each other, we define the mode-expansion for the holomorphic part (and exactly similarly, for the anti-holomorphic part as well) as:

\displaystyle\phi_{p}(z)=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}z^{-m-h}\phi_{m},

(313)

with the Hermitian-conjugation:
\phi^{\dagger}_{m}=\phi_{-m}
, for quasi-primary modes. From the ‘plane’-representation theory of
CFT_{2}
, we know that for a local (holomorphic) primary field
\phi_{p}(z)
, we have (for
n\geqslant-1
):

\displaystyle[L_{n},\phi_{p}(z)]=[h(n+1)z^{n}+z^{n+1}\partial_{z}]\phi_{p}(z).

(314)

Now, using the mode-expansion (
313
) into (
314
) and comparing the coefficients of
z^{-m-h}
on both sides of the equation, we conclude that (for
n\geqslant-1
):

\displaystyle[L_{n},\phi_{m}]=[n(h-1)-m]\phi_{m+n}.

(315)

A noteworthy point is that while the mode-expansion (
311
) or (
313
) is valid for any local fields, relation (
315
) is only applicable to the primary fields.

Torus one-point function

Exploiting the translation invariance of the torus one-point function of a local primary field, i.e. using the fact that it is independent of the ‘elliptic coordinate’
(w,\bar{w})
on the torus, the torus one-point function is a further generalization of the partition function in the way it is defined (for a primary field
\phi_{p}
on the plane, with dimensions
(h_{p},\bar{h}_{p})
):

\displaystyle\langle\phi\rangle_{(\tau,\bar{\tau})}

\displaystyle=\rm{Tr}\left[\phi_{p}(1,1)e^{2\pi i\tau(L_{0}-\frac{c}{24})}e^{-2\pi i\bar{\tau}(\bar{L}_{0}-\frac{\bar{c}}{24})}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{A}D(h_{A},\bar{h}_{A})q^{h_{A}-\frac{c}{24}}\bar{q}^{\bar{h}_{A}-\frac{\bar{c}}{24}}\langle h_{A},\bar{h}_{A}|\phi_{p}(1,1)|h_{A},\bar{h}_{A}\rangle F^{h_{p},\bar{h}_{p}}_{h_{A},\bar{h}_{A},c,\bar{c}}(q,\bar{q}),

(316)

where
\{A\}
denotes the collection of all the primary fields in the theory and
q=e^{2\pi i\tau}
.
D(h_{A},\bar{h}_{A})
is the number of the primary fields with dimension
(h_{A},\bar{h}_{A})
, in the theory. The ‘F’ functions, called ‘torus conformal blocks’, which are completely determined by the symmetry algebra alone, are clearly defined (in the highest-weight representation of the Virasoro algebra) to be holomorphically factorizable as
F^{h_{p},\bar{h}_{p}}_{h_{A},\bar{h}_{A},c,\bar{c}}(q,\bar{q})=F^{h_{p}}_{h_{A},c}(q)F^{\bar{h}_{p}}_{\bar{h}_{A},\bar{c}}(\bar{q})
, where:

\displaystyle F^{h_{p}}_{h_{A},c}(q)

\displaystyle=q^{-h_{A}+\frac{c}{24}}\frac{Tr_{\mathcal{M}(h_{A},c)}(\phi_{p}(1)q^{L_{0}-\frac{c}{24}})}{\langle h_{A}|\phi_{p}(1)|h_{A}\rangle}

\displaystyle=\sum_{N=0}^{\infty}\sum_{i,j\in\{N\}}q^{N}G^{ij}_{(N)}\frac{\langle h_{A};N,i|\phi_{p}(1)|h_{A};N,j\rangle}{\langle h_{A}|\phi_{p}(1)|h_{A}\rangle}:=\sum_{N=0}^{\infty}q^{N}F_{N}(h_{p},h_{A},c),

(317)

along with a similar definition for the anti-holomorphic part. In the above,
\mathcal{M}(h_{A},c)
denotes the holomorphic sector of the Verma module of the primary field with dimensions
(h_{A},\bar{h}_{A})
, while
[G^{ij}_{(N)}]
is the inverse Gram-matrix at level N of this holomorphic module, with
|h_{A};N,i\rangle
being a state at level N in the highest-weight representation.
\{N\}
is the collection of all such states in the module.

To explicitly calculate the matrix elements as in (
A
), let us consider the mode-expansion (2.3) at
z=1
:

\displaystyle\phi_{p}(z=1)=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\phi_{m}.

(318)

Now, let’s consider the following stream of calculations that will lead to a useful identity (for
N\geqslant 0
and
M\geqslant 0
):

\displaystyle\langle h_{A};N,i|L_{0}\phi_{p}(1)|h_{A};M,j\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle h_{A};N,i|[L_{0},\phi_{p}(1)]|h_{A};M,j\rangle+\langle h_{A};N,i|\phi_{p}(1)L_{0}|h_{A};M,j\rangle

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}(h_{A}+N)\langle h_{A};N,i|\phi_{m}|h_{A};M,j\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\langle h_{A};N,i|(-m)\phi_{m}|h_{A};M,j\rangle+\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}(h_{A}+M)\langle h_{A};N,i|\phi_{m}|h_{A};M,j\rangle

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}(m+N-M)\langle h_{A};N,i|\phi_{m}|h_{A};M,j\rangle=0

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}m\langle h_{A};N,i|\phi_{m-N+M}|h_{A};M,j\rangle=0.

(319)

Using (
318
) and (
319
), we may now proceed to calculate the relevant matrix elements in (
A
). Firstly, we look at level 1 matrix element:

{}\begin{split}&\langle h_{A}|L_{1}\phi_{p}(1)L_{-1}|h_{A}\rangle\\
&=\langle h_{A}|[L_{1},\phi_{p}(1)]L_{-1}|h_{A}\rangle+\langle h_{A}|\phi_{p}(1)L_{1}L_{-1}|h_{A}\rangle\\
&=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\langle h_{A}|(h_{p}-1-m)\phi_{m+1}L_{-1}|h_{A}\rangle+2h_{A}\langle h_{A}|\phi_{p}(1)|h_{A}\rangle\\
&=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}(h_{p}-1){\langle h_{A}|L_{1}\phi_{-m-1}|h_{A}\rangle}^{*}+2h_{A}\langle h_{A}|\phi_{p}(1)|h_{A}\rangle\\
\end{split}

{}\begin{split}&=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}(h_{p}-1){\langle h_{A}|(h_{p}+m)\phi_{-m}|h_{A}\rangle}^{*}+2h_{A}\langle h_{A}|\phi_{p}(1)|h_{A}\rangle\\
&=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}(h_{p}-1)(h_{p}+m){\langle h_{A}|\phi_{m}|h_{A}\rangle}+2h_{A}\langle h_{A}|\phi_{p}(1)|h_{A}\rangle\\
&=[h_{p}(h_{p}-1)+2h_{A}]\langle h_{A}|\phi_{p}(1)|h_{A}\rangle.\end{split}

(320)

where, to go from 2nd to 3rd step and from 5th to 6th step, (
319
) was in use. As level 1 has only one state, we get
G^{11}_{(1)}=\frac{1}{2h_{A}}
and hence

\displaystyle F_{1}(h_{p},h_{A},c)=1+\frac{h_{p}(h_{p}-1)}{2h_{A}}.

(321)

Of course we may just blindly copy the above calculation and conclude that for
n\geqslant 1
:

\displaystyle\langle h_{A}|L_{n}\phi_{p}(1)L_{-n}|h_{A}\rangle=[n^{2}h_{p}(h_{p}-1)+2nh_{A}+(n^{3}-n)\frac{c}{12}]\langle h_{A}|\phi_{p}(1)|h_{A}\rangle.

(322)

A class of repeatedly occurring matrix elements that can be easily calculated recursively and have a neat ‘series-summation’ form is of the general form
\langle h_{A}|L_{n}^{k}\phi_{p}(1)L_{-n}^{k}|h_{A}\rangle
for
k>0
and
n\geqslant 1
. To evaluate this, we need the following commutation relation:

\displaystyle[L_{n},L_{-n}^{k}]=\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}L_{-n}^{k-1-l}(2nL_{0}+(n^{3}-n)\frac{c}{12})L_{-n}^{l}.

(323)

The calculation is as below (with
h_{A}^{\prime}=h_{A}+(n^{2}-1)\frac{c}{24}
):

{}\begin{split}&\langle h_{A}|L_{n}^{k}\phi_{p}(1)L_{-n}^{k}|h_{A}\rangle\\
&\\
&~=\langle h_{A}|L_{n}^{k-1}[L_{n},\phi_{p}(1)]L_{-n}^{k}|h_{A}\rangle+\langle h_{A}|L_{n}^{k-1}\phi_{p}(1)L_{n}L_{-n}^{k}|h_{A}\rangle\\
&\\
&~=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\langle h_{A}|L_{n}^{k-1}[n(h_{p}-1)-m]\phi_{m+n}L_{-n}^{k}|h_{A}\rangle+2n\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}(h_{A}^{\prime}+nl)\langle h_{A}|L_{n}^{k-1}\phi_{p}(1)L_{-n}^{k-1}|h_{A}\rangle\\
&\\
&~=[n(h_{p}-1)]\langle h_{A}|L_{n}^{k-1}\phi_{p}(1)L_{-n}^{k}|h_{A}\rangle\ +nk[2h_{A}^{\prime}+n(k-1)]\langle h_{A}|L_{n}^{k-1}\phi_{p}(1)L_{-n}^{k-1}|h_{A}\rangle\\
&\\
&~=n\sum_{l=0}^{1}\frac{1}{(1-l)!}\binom{k}{l}n^{l}(\frac{2h_{A}^{\prime}}{n}+k-l)_{l}(h_{p}-1+l)_{1-l}\langle h_{A}|L_{n}^{k-1}\phi_{p}(1)L_{-n}^{k-l}|h_{A}\rangle\\
\end{split}

{}\begin{split}&\\
&~=[n(h_{p}-1)]\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\langle h_{A}|L_{n}^{k-2}[n(h_{p}-1)-m]\phi_{m+n}L_{-n}^{k}|h_{A}\rangle+2nk[2h_{A}^{\prime}+n(k-1)][n(h_{p}-1)]\\
&~~~\quad\langle h_{A}|L_{n}^{k-2}\phi_{p}(1)L_{-n}^{k-1}|h_{A}\rangle+n^{2}k(k-1)[2h_{A}^{\prime}+n(k-1)][2h_{A}^{\prime}+n(k-2)]\langle h_{A}|L_{n}^{k-2}\phi_{p}(1)L_{-n}^{k-2}|h_{A}\rangle\\
\end{split}

{}\begin{split}&\\
&~=[n(h_{p}-1)][n(h_{p}-2)]\langle h_{A}|L_{n}^{k-2}\phi_{p}(1)L_{-n}^{k}|h_{A}\rangle+2nk[2h_{A}^{\prime}+n(k-1)][n(h_{p}-1)]\\
&~~~~~\langle h_{A}|L_{n}^{k-2}\phi_{p}(1)L_{-n}^{k-1}|h_{A}\rangle+n^{2}k(k-1)[2h_{A}^{\prime}+n(k-1)][2h_{A}^{\prime}+n(k-2)]\langle h_{A}|L_{n}^{k-2}\phi_{p}(1)L_{-n}^{k-2}|h_{A}\rangle\\
&\\
&~=n^{2}\sum_{l=0}^{2}\frac{2}{(2-l)!}\binom{k}{l}n^{l}(\frac{2h_{A}^{\prime}}{n}+k-l)_{l}(h_{p}-2+l)_{2-l}\langle h_{A}|L_{n}^{k-2}\phi_{p}(1)L_{-n}^{k-l}|h_{A}\rangle\\
&\\
&\text{=~~~~~~~~~~~$\ldots\ldots\ldots\ldots$ recursive reduction of the power of $L_{n}$ $\ldots\ldots\ldots\ldots\ldots$}\\
&\\
&~=n^{k}\sum_{l=0}^{k}(k-l)!{\binom{k}{l}}^{2}n^{k-l}(\frac{2h_{A}^{\prime}}{n}+l)_{k-l}(h_{p}-l)_{l}\langle h_{A}|\phi_{p}(1)L_{-n}^{l}|h_{A}\rangle.\end{split}

Now, we are left with the undetermined
\langle h_{A}|\phi_{p}(1)L_{-n}^{l}|h_{A}\rangle
which can be evaluated recursively (we note that
\phi_{p}^{\dagger}(1)=\phi_{p}(1)
):

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\langle h_{A}|\phi_{p}(1)L_{-n}^{l}|h_{A}\rangle={\langle h_{A}|L_{n}^{l}\phi_{p}(1)|h_{A}\rangle}^{*}

\displaystyle\quad\quad=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}{\langle h_{A}|L_{n}^{l-1}[n(h_{p}-1)-m]\phi_{m+n}|h_{A}\rangle}^{*}

\displaystyle\quad\quad=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}{\langle h_{A}|L_{n}^{l-1}[nh_{p}-m]\phi_{m}|h_{A}\rangle}^{*}={\langle h_{A}|L_{n}^{l-1}[nh_{p}+n(l-1)]\phi_{p}(1)|h_{A}\rangle}^{*}

\displaystyle\quad\quad=n(h_{p}+l-1){\langle h_{A}|\phi_{p}(1)L_{-n}^{l-1}|h_{A}\rangle}=n^{l}(h_{p})_{l}\langle h_{A}|\phi_{p}(1)|h_{A}\rangle.

(324)

Thus finally we get:

\displaystyle\frac{\langle h_{A}|L_{n}^{k}\phi_{p}(1)L_{-n}^{k}|h_{A}\rangle}{\langle h_{A}|\phi_{p}(1)|h_{A}\rangle}=n^{2k}\sum_{l=0}^{k}(k-l)!{\binom{k}{l}}^{2}(\frac{2h_{A}^{\prime}}{n}+l)_{k-l}(h_{p}-l)_{2l}

(325)

We see that (
322
) is a special case of (
324
). Now, for the purpose of book-keeping, let’s find the relevant matrix elements at level 2. Using the above general formulae, we instantly get:

\displaystyle\frac{\langle h_{A}|L_{1}^{2}\phi_{p}(1)L_{-1}^{2}|h_{A}\rangle}{\langle h_{A}|\phi_{p}(1)|h_{A}\rangle}=4h_{A}(2h_{A}+1)+4h_{p}(h_{p}-1)(2h_{A}+1)+(h_{p}-2)_{4}

\displaystyle\frac{\langle h_{A}|L_{2}\phi_{p}(1)L_{-2}|h_{A}\rangle}{\langle h_{A}|\phi_{p}(1)|h_{A}\rangle}=4h_{p}(h_{p}-1)+4h_{A}+\frac{c}{2}.

(326)

while a little calculation reveals that:

\displaystyle{\langle h_{A}|L_{2}\phi_{p}(1)L_{-1}^{2}|h_{A}\rangle}

\displaystyle={\langle h_{A}|L_{1}^{2}\phi_{p}(1)L_{-2}|h_{A}\rangle}

\displaystyle=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}[2(h_{p}-1)-m]\langle h_{A}|\phi_{m+2}L_{-1}^{2}|h_{A}\rangle+6h_{A}\langle h_{A}|\phi_{p}(1)|h_{A}\rangle

\displaystyle=2(h_{p}-1)\langle h_{A}|\phi_{p}(1)L_{-1}^{2}|h_{A}\rangle+6h_{A}\langle h_{A}|\phi_{p}(1)|h_{A}\rangle

\displaystyle=[2(h_{p}-1)h_{p}(h_{p}+1)+6h_{A}]\langle h_{A}|\phi_{p}(1)|h_{A}\rangle.

(327)

where, to go to the last step from the penultimate one, we used (
A
) directly. Now, we are ready to note down the level 2 contribution to the torus block:

{}F_{2}(h_{p},h_{A},c)=2+\frac{4[(8h_{A}+\frac{c}{2})(2h_{A}+1)-6h_{A}(h_{p}+1)](h_{p}-1)_{2}+(4h_{A}+\frac{c}{2})(h_{p}-2)_{4}}{4h_{A}[(2h_{A}+1)(4h_{A}+\frac{c}{2})-9h_{A}]}

(328)

As an aside, noticing the utility of (
A
), we may further generalize it into the following:

{}\frac{\langle h_{A}|\phi_{p}(1)\prod_{i=1}^{n}L_{-i}^{l_{i}}|h_{A}\rangle}{\langle h_{A}|\phi_{p}(1)|h_{A}\rangle}=\frac{\langle h_{A}|(\prod_{i=n}^{1}L_{i}^{l_{i}})\phi_{p}(1)|h_{A}\rangle}{\langle h_{A}|\phi_{p}(1)|h_{A}\rangle}^{\star}=\prod_{k=1}^{n}k^{l_{k}}(h_{p}+\frac{1}{k}\sum_{i=k+1}^{n}il_{i})_{l_{k}}.

(329)

In principle, following our derivation of (
324
), one should be able to evaluate the most general matrix element
\langle h_{A}|(\prod_{j=m}^{1}L_{j}^{l_{j}})\phi_{p}(1)\prod_{i=1}^{n}L_{-i}^{k_{i}}|h_{A}\rangle
, by recursively reducing the powers of all the
\{L_{j}\}
to 0 and then applying Virasoro commutation relation and finally (
329
).

Global torus block

In the highest-weight representation of the global Virasoro algebra, the descendant state at level N in the holomorphic module of the primary field with dimension
h_{A}
is given by
L_{-1}^{N}|h_{A}\rangle
. So, the global torus block can be computed very easily using (
324
) with
n=1
. Also, we have:

\displaystyle\langle h_{A}|L_{1}^{N}L_{-1}^{N}|h_{A}\rangle=N!(2h_{A})_{N}.

(330)

These lead to (for
n=1
,
h^{\prime}_{A}=h_{A}
):

\displaystyle F_{N}^{global}(h_{p},h_{A})=\sum_{l=0}^{N}\frac{1}{l!}\binom{N}{l}\frac{(h_{p}-l)_{2l}}{(2h_{A})_{l}}.

(331)

And, finally, we evaluate the holomorphic global torus block, by evaluating the global version of (
A
) to obtain, for
h_{A}>0
:

\displaystyle F^{global}_{h_{p},h_{A}}(q)

\displaystyle=\sum_{N=0}^{\infty}q^{N}\sum_{l=0}^{N}\frac{1}{l!}\binom{N}{l}\frac{(h_{p}-l)_{2l}}{(2h_{A})_{l}}=\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\sum_{N=0}^{\infty}q^{N+l}\frac{1}{l!}\binom{N+l}{l}\frac{(h_{p}-l)_{2l}}{(2h_{A})_{l}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\frac{q^{l}}{l!}[\sum_{N=0}^{\infty}q^{N}\binom{N+l}{l}]\frac{(h_{p}-l)_{2l}}{(2h_{A})_{l}}=\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}q^{l}\frac{{(1-q)}^{-l-1}}{l!}\frac{(h_{p}-l)_{2l}}{(2h_{A})_{l}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-q}\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\frac{(1-h_{p})_{l}(h_{p})_{l}}{l!(2h_{A})_{l}}\Big(\frac{q}{q-1}\Big)^{l}

\displaystyle={(1-q)^{-1}}{}_{2}F_{1}\Big(h_{p},1-h_{p};2h_{A};\frac{q}{q-1}\Big).

(332)

We note that as
|q|<1
, the convergence issue is automatically taken care of.

Large
h_{A}
limit of
F_{N}(h_{p},h_{A},c)

While deriving an asymptotic, Cardy-like formula for structure constant, we need to consider the torus block of a heavy exchanged operator with dimension
h_{A}\gg c,h_{p}
. We would like to find the leading and the sub-leading terms in the asymptotic expansion in
\frac{1}{h_{A}}
of the torus block. The general matrix element at level
N
,
\langle h_{A}|(\prod_{j=N}^{1}L_{j}^{l_{j}})\phi_{p}(1)\prod_{i=1}^{N}L_{-i}^{k_{i}}|h_{A}\rangle
, with
\sum_{j=1}^{N}jl_{j}=N=\sum_{i=1}^{N}ik_{i}
, is evaluated algorithmically as below that in essence is the recursive reduction of the Virasoro operator string at the left of
\phi_{p}(1)
. Being real quantities, these matrix elements is indifferent to the swapping of the left and right string according to the Hermittian conjugation. Among these two strings, the one which has less length than the other, is reduced recursively (for faster computation). In the following, without the loss of generality, we assume that
\sum_{j=1}^{N}l_{j}\leqslant\sum_{i=1}^{N}k_{i}
.

\displaystyle\langle h_{A}|(\prod_{j=N}^{1}L_{j}^{l_{j}})\phi_{p}(1)\prod_{i=1}^{N}L_{-i}^{k_{i}}|h_{A}\rangle=\langle h_{A}|(\prod_{i=N}^{1}L_{i}^{k_{i}})\phi_{p}(1)\prod_{j=1}^{N}L_{-j}^{l_{j}}|h_{A}\rangle^{*}

\displaystyle=(h_{p}-1)\langle h_{A}|(\prod_{j=N}^{2}L_{j}^{l_{j}})L_{1}^{l_{1}-1}\phi_{p}(1)\prod_{i=1}^{N}L_{-i}^{k_{i}}|h_{A}\rangle+\langle h_{A}|(\prod_{j=N}^{2}L_{j}^{l_{j}})L_{1}^{l_{1}-1}\phi_{p}(1)L_{1}\prod_{i=1}^{N}L_{-i}^{k_{i}}|h_{A}\rangle

\displaystyle=(h_{p}-1)(h_{p}-2)\langle h_{A}|(\prod_{j=N}^{2}L_{j}^{l_{j}})L_{1}^{l_{1}-2}\phi_{p}(1)\prod_{i=1}^{N}L_{-i}^{k_{i}}|h_{A}\rangle

\displaystyle+2(h_{p}-1)\langle h_{A}|(\prod_{j=N}^{2}L_{j}^{l_{j}})L_{1}^{l_{1}-2}\phi_{p}(1)L_{1}\prod_{i=1}^{N}L_{-i}^{k_{i}}|h_{A}\rangle+\langle h_{A}|(\prod_{j=N}^{2}L_{j}^{l_{j}})L_{1}^{l_{1}-2}\phi_{p}(1)L_{1}^{2}\prod_{i=1}^{N}L_{-i}^{k_{i}}|h_{A}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{a_{1}=0}^{l_{1}}\binom{l_{1}}{a_{1}}(h_{p}-a_{1})_{a_{1}}\langle h_{A}|(\prod_{j=N}^{2}L_{j}^{l_{j}})\phi_{p}(1)L_{1}^{l_{1}-a_{1}}\prod_{i=1}^{N}L_{-i}^{k_{i}}|h_{A}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{a_{2}=0}^{l_{2}}\binom{l_{2}}{a_{2}}2^{a_{2}}(h_{p}-\frac{a_{1}}{2}-a_{2})_{a_{2}}\sum_{a_{1}=0}^{l_{1}}\binom{l_{1}}{a_{1}}(h_{p}-a_{1})_{a_{1}}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\langle h_{A}|(\prod_{j=N}^{3}L_{j}^{l_{j}})\phi_{p}(1)L_{2}^{l_{2}-a_{2}}L_{1}^{l_{1}-a_{1}}\prod_{i=1}^{N}L_{-i}^{k_{i}}|h_{A}\rangle

\displaystyle=...\text{recursive reduction of the left string of $\{L_{j}\}$}...

\displaystyle=\underset{(\sum_{m=1}^{N}a_{m})>0}{\sum_{a_{1}=0}^{l_{1}}\sum_{a_{2}=0}^{l_{2}}...\sum_{a_{N}=0}^{l_{N}}}\langle h_{A}|\phi_{p}(1)[\prod_{j=N}^{1}\binom{l_{j}}{a_{j}}j^{a_{j}}(h_{p}-\frac{1}{j}\sum_{p=1}^{j}pa_{p})_{a_{j}}L_{j}^{l_{j}-a_{j}}]\prod_{i=1}^{N}L_{-i}^{k_{i}}|h_{A}\rangle

\displaystyle+\langle h_{A}|\phi_{p}(1)|h_{A}\rangle\langle h_{A}|(\prod_{j=N}^{1}L_{j}^{l_{j}})\prod_{i=1}^{N}L_{-i}^{k_{i}}|h_{A}\rangle.

(333)

From the first term of (
332
), to make the
[\prod_{j=N}^{1}L_{j}^{l_{j}-a_{j}}]\prod_{i=1}^{N}L_{-i}^{k_{i}}|h_{A}\rangle
part a proper Virasoro highest-weight descendant state at level
\sum_{j=1}^{N}ja_{j}
, we use the Virasoro commutation relation and then using (
325
), we obtain a final form as
g(h_{A},h_{p})\langle h_{A}|\phi_{p}(1)|h_{A}\rangle
, where
g(h_{A},h_{p})
is a polynomial in both
h_{A}
and
h_{p}
.
Clearly, the second term in the above identity is an element of the Gram-matrix
[G_{(N)ij}]
, upto the
\langle h_{A}|\phi_{p}(1)|h_{A}\rangle
factor, while the
h_{p}
-dependence comes solely from the first term. Hence, the total contribution of this generic second term to
F_{N}(h_{p},h_{A},c)
in (
A
) is simply
{\rm dim}_{N}=p(N)
.

As we are interested in the large
h_{A}
limit, we would like to find the leading order term in
h_{A}
from the first term of (
332
). We note that the generic level
N
Gram-matrix element,
\langle h_{A}|(\prod_{j=N}^{1}L_{j}^{l_{j}})\prod_{i=1}^{N}L_{-i}^{k_{i}}|h_{A}\rangle
, is of the order
\mathcal{O}(h_{A}^{\alpha})
, with
1\leqslant\alpha\leqslant min\{\sum_{j=1}^{N}l_{j},\sum_{i=1}^{N}k_{i}\}
, while the corresponding element of the inverse Gram-matrix, (
\frac{\text{the co-factor of the mentioned element}}{\text{Kac determinant at level $N$}}
) is of the order
\mathcal{O}(h_{A}^{-\beta})
, with
\beta\geqslant max\{\sum_{j=1}^{N}l_{j},\sum_{i=1}^{N}k_{i}\}
. Hence, the generic summation term in (
332
) is at most of the order
\mathcal{O}(h_{A}^{\sum_{j=1}^{N}(l_{j}-a_{j})})
in
h_{A}
, while the remaining descendant state at level
\sum_{j=1}^{N}ja_{j}
gives a factor dependent on
h_{p}
according to (
325
). Clearly, the maximum possible order in
h_{A}
for such a term is
\mathcal{O}(h_{A}^{(\sum_{j=1}^{N}l_{j})-1})
and we simplify this term as below:

\displaystyle\underset{(\sum_{m=1}^{N}a_{m})=1}{\sum_{a_{1}=0}^{l_{1}}\sum_{a_{2}=0}^{l_{2}}...\sum_{a_{N}=0}^{l_{N}}}\langle h_{A}|\phi_{p}(1)[\prod_{j=N}^{1}\binom{l_{j}}{a_{j}}j^{a_{j}}(h_{p}-\frac{1}{j}\sum_{p=1}^{j}pa_{p})_{a_{j}}L_{j}^{l_{j}-a_{j}}]\prod_{i=1}^{N}L_{-i}^{k_{i}}|h_{A}\rangle

\displaystyle=(h_{p}-1){\sum_{j=1}^{N}}jl_{j}\langle h_{A}|\phi_{p}(1)(\prod_{p=N}^{j+1}L_{p}^{l_{p}})L_{j}^{l_{j}-1}(\prod_{q=j-1}^{1}L_{q}^{l_{q}})\prod_{i=1}^{N}L_{-i}^{k_{i}}|h_{A}\rangle.

(334)

Now, in any row or column of both the
[G_{(N)ij}]
and its inverse, the diagonal element is the highest order term in
h_{A}
, though other terms may be of equal or lower order. Clearly, the diagonal element of the Gram matrix is of the order
\mathcal{O}(h_{A}^{\sum_{i=1}^{N}k_{i}})
, while the corresponding diagonal element of the inverse Gram matrix is of the order
\mathcal{O}(h_{A}^{-\sum_{i=1}^{N}k_{i}})
, i.e. only a diagonal element of
[G_{(N)ij}]
and the corresponding one of
[G_{(N)}^{ij}]
are of the same order in
h_{A}
and
\frac{1}{h_{A}}
respectively. By definition
G_{(N)}^{ij}G_{(N)jk}=\delta^{i}_{k}
and if we naively evaluate the large
h_{A}
limit of
[G_{(N)}^{ij}]
and
[G_{(N)ij}]
by keeping only the leading term in
h_{A}
of each element and then do matrix multiplication, we see that the non-diagonal elements of the resulting matrix is at most
\mathcal{O}(h_{A}^{-1})
while diagonal elements are
1+\mathcal{O}(h_{A}^{-1})
.

Thus if we multiply
[G_{(N)}^{ij}]
and
[\langle h_{A};N,i|\phi_{p}(1)|h_{A};N,j\rangle]-\langle h_{A}|\phi_{p}(1)|h_{A}\rangle[G_{(N)ij}]
, by keeping only the highest order term in
h_{A}
for each element, it is easy to deduce from the above arguments that the non-diagonal terms in the resultant matrix are at most
\mathcal{O}(h_{A}^{-2})
while the diagonal terms are
\mathcal{O}(h_{A}^{-1})
. So, in the large
h_{A}
limit, to find the
h_{p}
-dependent coefficient of the
\mathcal{O}(h_{A}^{-1})
term in the
\frac{1}{h_{A}}
-expansion of the torus block, it suffices to consider only the diagonal elements:
\langle h_{A}|(\prod_{j=N}^{1}L_{j}^{k_{j}})\phi_{p}(1)\prod_{i=1}^{N}L_{-i}^{k_{i}}|h_{A}\rangle
. Even for each of these elements also, we only need the leading order term in
h_{A}
, that also depends on
h_{p}
(l.o.t.
\Rightarrow
lower order terms in
h_{A}
):

{}\begin{split}&\text{$h_{p}$-dependent term in }\langle h_{A}|(\prod_{j=N}^{1}L_{j}^{k_{j}})\phi_{p}(1)\prod_{i=1}^{N}L_{-i}^{k_{i}}|h_{A}\rangle\\
&=(h_{p}-1){\sum_{j=1}^{N}}jk_{j}\langle h_{A}|\phi_{p}(1)(\prod_{p=N}^{j+1}L_{p}^{k_{p}})L_{j}^{k_{j}-1}(\prod_{q=j-1}^{1}L_{q}^{k_{q}})\prod_{i=1}^{N}L_{-i}^{k_{i}}|h_{A}\rangle+{l.o.t.}^{\prime\prime}\\
&=(h_{p}-1){\sum_{j=1}^{N}}[jk_{j}\langle h_{A}|\phi_{p}(1)k_{j}L_{-j}|h_{A}\rangle{\langle h_{A}|L_{j}L_{-j}|h_{A}\rangle}^{k_{j}-1}\\
&\qquad\times\prod_{i=1,i\neq j}^{N}{\langle h_{A}|L_{i}L_{-i}|h_{A}\rangle}^{k_{i}}]+{l.o.t.}^{\prime}\\
&=(h_{p}-1)h_{p}{\sum_{j=1}^{N}}[{(jk_{j})}^{2}{(2jh_{A})}^{k_{j}-1}{(k_{j}-1)}!\prod_{i=1,i\neq j}^{N}{(2ih_{A})}^{k_{i}}{k_{i}}!]\langle h_{A}|\phi_{p}(1)|h_{A}\rangle+{l.o.t.}\end{split}

(335)

Clearly, for our purpose, we only need to explicitly know only the leading order term in
h_{A}
of only the diagonal elements of the inverse Gram-matrix. In the large
h_{A}
limit, it is approximately given by:

\displaystyle\langle h_{A}|(\prod_{j=N}^{1}L_{j}^{k_{j}})\prod_{i=1}^{N}L_{-i}^{k_{i}}|h_{A}\rangle^{-1}=[\prod_{i=1}^{N}{(2ih_{A})}^{k_{i}}{k_{i}}!]^{-1}+{l.o.t.}

Thus, each diagonal element of the
\langle h_{A};N,i|\phi_{p}(1)|h_{A};N,i\rangle
matrix gives the following
h_{p}
-dependent contribution to
F_{N}(h_{p},h_{A},c)
in the large
h_{A}
limit:

\displaystyle\text{$h_{p}$-dependent term in }\langle h_{A}|(\prod_{j=N}^{1}L_{j}^{k_{j}})\phi_{p}(1)\prod_{i=1}^{N}L_{-i}^{k_{i}}|h_{A}\rangle\times\langle h_{A}|(\prod_{j=N}^{1}L_{j}^{k_{j}})\prod_{i=1}^{N}L_{-i}^{k_{i}}|h_{A}\rangle^{-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{h_{p}(h_{p}-1)}{2h_{A}}\sum_{j=1}^{N}jk_{j}+\mathcal{O}(h_{A}^{-2})=N\frac{h_{p}(h_{p}-1)}{2h_{A}}+\mathcal{O}(h_{A}^{-2}).

(336)

So, each diagonal element gives equal contribution to the
\frac{1}{h_{A}}
-term and there are
p(N)
such elements. Hence, collecting all the contributions in large
h_{A}
limit:

\displaystyle F_{N}(h_{p},h_{A},c)=p(N)\left(1+N\frac{h_{p}(h_{p}-1)}{2h_{A}}\right)+\mathcal{O}(h_{A}^{-2}).

(337)

Special cases

1.
h_{p}=1
: From (
332
), it is evident that the first term which involves at least one commutation of the
\phi_{p}(1)
with the Virasoro generators is proportional to
(h_{p}-1)
, so the first term is 0. So, the generic matrix element satisfies
\frac{\langle h_{A}|(\prod_{j=N}^{1}L_{j}^{l_{j}})\phi_{p}(1)\prod_{i=1}^{N}L_{-i}^{k_{i}}|h_{A}\rangle}{\langle h_{A}|\phi_{p}(1)|h_{A}\rangle}=\langle h_{A}|(\prod_{j=N}^{1}L_{j}^{l_{j}})\prod_{i=1}^{N}L_{-i}^{k_{i}}|h_{A}\rangle
, i.e. it is proportional to the Gram-matrix elements. Thus, clearly, we have an exact result:
F_{N}(h_{p}=1,h_{A},c)=p(N)
.

2.
h_{p}=0
: To finally evaluate the first term of (
332
), we must finally use (
325
) as described in the previous section. If
h_{p}=0
, the R.H.S. of (
325
) is 0. So, the generic matrix element is again proportional to the corresponding Gram-matrix element. Thus, here also, we have the same result:
F_{N}(h_{p}=0,h_{A},c)=p(N)
.

Asymptotic structure constant for torus one point function:

After having the expression for CFT torus conformal block we will now briefly summarise the analysis of
Kraus:2016nwo
to calculate torus one point function, more specifically the asymptotic form of the three point co-efficient. We will be exploiting the modular transformation property of CFT torus one point function. Under S modular transformation, the one point function transforms as,

{}\langle\phi\rangle_{(\tau,\bar{\tau})}=\tau^{-h}\bar{\tau}^{-\bar{h}}\langle\phi\rangle_{(-\frac{1}{\tau},-\frac{1}{\bar{\tau}})}.

(338)

Following (
A
) we can write,

{}\begin{split}\langle\phi\rangle_{(\tau,\bar{\tau})}&=\sum_{A}D(h_{A},\bar{h}_{A})e^{2\pi i\tau(h_{A}-\frac{c}{24})}e^{-2\pi i\bar{\tau}(\bar{h}_{A}-\frac{\bar{c}}{24})}C_{ApA}F^{h_{p},\bar{h}_{p}}_{h_{A},\bar{h}_{A},c,\bar{c}}(\tau,\bar{\tau})\\
&=\tau^{-h_{p}}\bar{\tau}^{-\bar{h_{p}}}\sum_{A}D(h_{i},\bar{h}_{i})C_{ipi}\>e^{\big(-\frac{2\pi i}{\tau}(h_{i}-\frac{c}{24})\big)}\>e^{\big(\frac{2\pi i}{\bar{\tau}}(\bar{h}_{i}-\frac{\bar{c}}{24})\big)}.\end{split}

(339)

Here we have used
q=e^{2\pi i\tau},\>\bar{q}=e^{-2\pi i\bar{\tau}}
. The LHS of the expression is written in terms of the torus block of the primaries, where as the RHS is expanded in arbitrary eigenstates of virasoro zero mode
L_{0},\bar{L}_{0}
. We have also used the notation for the three-point coefficients
C_{ApA}=\langle h_{A},\bar{h}_{A}|\phi_{p}(1,1)|h_{A},\bar{h}_{A}\rangle,\;C_{ipi}=\langle h_{i},\bar{h}_{i}|\phi_{p}(1,1)|h_{i},\bar{h}_{i}\rangle
. (
339
) relates the high temperature limit of the theory to the low temperature one.

Next we take the limit
\tau=\frac{i\beta}{2\pi}\to i0^{+},\;\bar{\tau}=\frac{-i\beta}{2\pi}\to-i0^{+}
or
\beta\to 0^{+}
, and write the RHS in terms of the lightest state
\chi
for which
\langle\chi|\phi|\chi\rangle\neq 0
. Then the RHS is given by,

{}\text{RHS}\approx i^{h_{p}-\bar{h}_{p}}\left(\frac{2\pi}{\beta}\right)^{h_{p}+\bar{h}_{p}}D(h_{\chi},\bar{h}_{\chi})C_{\chi p\chi}e^{-\frac{4\pi^{2}}{\beta}(h_{\chi}+\bar{h}_{\chi}-\frac{c}{12})}.

(340)

We will rewrite the sum of the scaling dimension of the state
\chi
as
E_{\chi}=h_{\chi}+\bar{h}_{\chi}
and also use
c=\bar{c}
. Accordingly, we also write the contributions from the scaling dimension of the operator
\phi
as
E_{p}=h_{p}+\bar{h}_{p}
and
s_{p}=h_{p}-\bar{h}_{p}
. We also write
T_{p}(h_{A},\bar{h}_{A})\equiv D(h_{A},\bar{h}_{A})C_{ApA}
and
T_{p}(h_{\chi},\bar{h}_{\chi})\equiv D(h_{A},\bar{h}_{A})C_{\chi p\chi}
.

Now, the small
\beta
limit implies the torus blocks in the LHS to be approximated in the large
h_{A},\bar{h}_{A}
limit. Following
Kraus:2016nwo
, the torus blocks can be written as,

{}F^{h_{p},\bar{h}_{p}}_{h_{A},\bar{h}_{A},c}(\beta)\approx\beta e^{\frac{1}{12}\left(-\beta+\frac{4\pi^{2}}{\beta}\right)}

(341)

This expression is approximated in small
\beta
limit only. Finally, we can perform an inverse Laplace transform on (
339
) to find the form of
T_{p}(h_{A},\bar{h}_{A})
as,

{}T_{p}(h_{A},\bar{h}_{A})=\frac{i^{s_{p}}}{2\pi}\int dh_{A}d{\bar{h}_{A}}T_{p}({h_{\chi},\bar{h}_{\chi}})\left(\frac{2\pi}{\beta}\right)^{E_{p}+1}\text{Exp}\left[(h_{A}+\bar{h}_{A}-\frac{c-1}{12})\beta-(E_{\chi}-\frac{c-1}{12})\frac{4\pi^{2}}{\beta}\right]

(342)

At large
h_{A}
limit, this integral can be approximated by the contribution from the saddle point at

{}\beta\approx 2\pi\sqrt{\frac{\frac{c-1}{12}-E_{\chi}}{E-\frac{c-1}{12}}}+\frac{E_{p}+1}{2(E-\frac{c-1}{12})}+\ldots.

(343)

Here, we have used
E=h_{A}+\bar{h}_{A}
. The leading terms dominate the suppressed terms in the large
h_{A},\bar{h}_{A}
limit or
E\to\infty
limit. The above equation is also valid as long as the operator is light that is
E_{\chi}<<\frac{c}{12}
. Putting this value back into (
342
), we obtain

{}\begin{split}\frac{T_{p}(h_{A},\bar{h}_{A})}{T_{p}(h_{\chi},\bar{h}_{\chi})}\approx&\frac{i^{s_{p}}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\left(\frac{c-1}{12}-E_{\chi}\right)^{-\frac{E_{p}}{2}-\frac{1}{4}}\left(E-\frac{c-1}{12}\right)^{\frac{E_{p}}{2}-\frac{1}{4}}\\
&~~~~~~\text{Exp}\left(4\pi\sqrt{\left(\frac{c-1}{12}-E_{\chi}\right)\left(E-\frac{c-1}{12}\right)}+\ldots\right)\end{split}

(344)

At this point, to find the asymptotic formula for density of states in the large
h_{A}
limit, we put
E_{p}=0=s_{p}
directly in the above equation and also replace the primary field
\chi
by the lightest primary field
\mathcal{O}
present in the theory.

{}\begin{split}\frac{D_{p}(h_{A},\bar{h}_{A})}{D_{p}(h_{\mathcal{O}},\bar{h}_{\mathcal{O}})}\approx&\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\left(\frac{c-1}{12}-E_{\mathcal{O}}\right)^{-\frac{1}{4}}\left(E-\frac{c-1}{12}\right)^{-\frac{1}{4}}\\
&~~~~~~\text{Exp}\left(4\pi\sqrt{\left(\frac{c-1}{12}-E_{\mathcal{O}}\right)\left(E-\frac{c-1}{12}\right)}+\ldots\right)\end{split}

(345)

We can see that in the large
E\to\infty
limit and taking
\mathcal{O}
to be the vacuum
E_{\mathcal{O}}=0
, we match this expression with that of
Kraus:2016nwo
. Substituting this back to (
344
), we finally obtain the asymptotic formula of three-point coefficient for primary fields for large
h_{A},\bar{h}_{A}
or
E\to\infty
,

{}\begin{split}\text{C}_{ApA}\approx&i^{s_{p}}\frac{D{(h_{\chi},\bar{h}_{\chi})}}{D{(h_{\mathcal{O}},\bar{h}_{\mathcal{O})}}}C_{\chi p\chi}\left(\frac{c-1}{12}-E_{\mathcal{O}}\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}\left(E-\frac{c-1}{12}\right)^{\frac{E_{p}}{2}}\left(\frac{c-1}{12}-E_{\chi}\right)^{-\frac{E_{p}}{2}-\frac{1}{4}}\\
&~~~~~\text{Exp}\left[-\frac{\pi(c-1)}{12}\left(\sqrt{1-\frac{12E_{\mathcal{O}}}{c-1}}-\sqrt{1-\frac{12E_{\chi}}{c-1}}\right)\sqrt{\frac{12E}{c-1}-1}\right]\end{split}

(346)

If we assume there is no degeneracy in the system along with
\mathcal{O}
to be the vacuum state, we get back the same result as
Kraus:2016nwo
.

Appendix B
Finite BMS transformations

In this section, our main goal is to derive the transformation rule for the
BMS_{3}
primary fields under finite
BMS_{3}
transformation. We will do this by deriving the finite
GCA_{2}
transformation of primary fields by taking non-relativistic contraction of the corresponding rule in
CFT_{2}
. The result for
BMS
is obtained form that of
GCA
by flipping the coordinates.

Under a local conformal transformation
z\rightarrow w(z)
and
\bar{z}\rightarrow\bar{w}(\bar{z})
, the
CFT_{2}
primary field with conformal weights (
h
,
\bar{h}
) transforms like:

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}(w,\bar{w})=(\frac{dw}{dz})^{-h}(\frac{d\bar{w}}{d\bar{z}})^{-\bar{h}}{\phi}(z,\bar{z})

(347)

In order to look at non-relativsitic contraction, we write the complex co-ordinates as
z=u+v
and
\bar{z}=u-v
, where
u
is the Euclidean time and
v
is the space coordinate. Now, we have defined our conformal transformation in such a way that
w(z)
is a holomorphic function of
z
and
\bar{w}(\bar{z})
is an anti-holomorphic function of
\bar{z}
. Let

\displaystyle w(z)={u}^{\prime}(u,v)+{v}^{\prime}(u,v)

\displaystyle\bar{w}(\bar{z})={u}^{\prime}(u,v)-{u}^{\prime}(u,v)

(348)

By using the fact that
\frac{\partial w(z)}{\partial\bar{z}}=\frac{\partial\bar{w}(\bar{z})}{\partial z}=0
, we get the Cauchy-Riemann equation

\partial_{u}u^{\prime}=\partial_{v}v^{\prime},\quad\quad\partial_{v}u^{\prime}=\partial_{u}v^{\prime}.

(349)

Using this, we can deduce that

\displaystyle\frac{dw}{dz}=\partial_{u}u^{\prime}+\partial_{u}v^{\prime},\quad\quad\frac{d\bar{w}}{d\bar{z}}=\partial_{u}u^{\prime}-\partial_{u}v^{\prime}.

(350)

Now, let us consider the non-relativistic contraction of the Virasoro symmetry algebra of the relativistic CFT, by the scaling
u\rightarrow u
and
v\rightarrow\epsilon v
with
\epsilon\rightarrow 0
and similar scaling is applied to the primed coordinates. Also, conformal weights are scaled as
h\rightarrow\frac{1}{2}(\Delta-\frac{\xi}{\epsilon})
and
\bar{h}\rightarrow\frac{1}{2}(\Delta+\frac{\xi}{\epsilon})
. Thus, we proceed to apply the contraction to (
347
):

\displaystyle(\frac{dw}{dz})^{-h}(\frac{d\bar{w}}{d\bar{z}})^{-\bar{h}}

\displaystyle=\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}(\partial_{u}u^{\prime}+\epsilon\partial_{u}v^{\prime})^{-\frac{1}{2}(\Delta-\frac{\xi}{\epsilon})}(\partial_{u}u^{\prime}-\epsilon\partial_{u}v^{\prime})^{-\frac{1}{2}(\Delta+\frac{\xi}{\epsilon})}

\displaystyle=(\partial_{u}u^{\prime})^{-\Delta}e^{\xi\frac{\partial_{u}v^{\prime}}{\partial_{u}u^{\prime}}}

(351)

If we apply the non-relativistic contraction to the Cauchy-Riemann equations (
349
), we get

\partial_{u}u^{\prime}=\partial_{v}v^{\prime},\quad\quad\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\frac{1}{\epsilon}\partial_{v}u^{\prime}=-\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\epsilon\partial_{u}v^{\prime}.

(352)

Clearly, the second condition tells us that
\partial_{v}u^{\prime}
must be 0, which in turn implies that
u^{\prime}=u^{\prime}(u)
. By solving the remaining equation, we get
v^{\prime}(u,v)=v\partial_{u}u^{\prime}+f(u)
, where
f(u)
is any arbitrary well-behaved function.
Thus, under the finite
GCA_{2}
transformation
u\rightarrow u^{\prime}=u^{\prime}(u)
and
x\rightarrow v^{\prime}=v\partial_{u}u^{\prime}+f(u)
, the primary field with
GCA_{2}
weights (
\Delta
,
\xi
) transforms as:

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})=(\partial_{u}u^{\prime})^{-\Delta}e^{\xi\frac{\partial_{u}v^{\prime}}{\partial_{u}u^{\prime}}}\phi(u,v).

(353)

The way to obtain the finite
BMS_{3}
transformation would be to use the commutation of the
BMS_{3}
generators with the primary field. We know that the difference in the commutation of the primary field with the generators in
GCA_{2}
and
BMS_{3}
is that
u
and
v
are exchanged.
Thus for finite
BMS_{3}
transformation, we just have to exchange
u
and
v
in the previous equation (
353
) giving us

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})=(\partial_{v}v^{\prime})^{-\Delta}e^{\xi\frac{\partial_{v}u^{\prime}}{\partial_{v}v^{\prime}}}\phi(u,v).

(354)

As for the coordinate, finite
BMS_{3}
transformation are given by

u\rightarrow u^{\prime}=u\partial_{v}f(v)+g(v),\quad\quad v\rightarrow v^{\prime}=f(v).

(355)

Let us consider the mapping from 2-d Euclidean plane (spanned by
(t,x)
) to cylinder (spanned by
(u,\phi)
) in
BMS_{3}
, given by:
t=-iue^{i\phi}
and
x=e^{i\phi}
. The primary field in the cylinder
\phi_{\rm cyl}(u,\phi)
with
(\Delta,\xi)
are related to the primary field on the plane
\phi(t,x)
as

\phi_{\rm cyl}(u,\phi)=Ae^{i\phi\Delta}e^{-iu\xi}\phi_{p}(t,x).

(356)

where p in subscript denotes ‘plane’ and
A
is constant which can be absorbed to
\phi_{p}(t,x)
. This is the same transformation rule as in (
37
).

Appendix C
Some useful identities and their derivations

C.1
Some useful identities

In this section we will list out useful identities for the computation of the matrix elements
\langle A;N,i|\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}|A;N,j\rangle
in (
149
). These identities are derived using the commutation relation (
138
) and their derivations are given in the next subsection. The primary field
\phi_{p}
is at
t=0,x=1
and the mode expansion (
130
) at this point is given by

\displaystyle\phi_{p}(0,1)=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\phi_{m,0}.

(357)

The first identity is given by

\displaystyle\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}(m+N-M)\langle A;N,i|\phi_{m,n}|A;M,j\rangle

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\implies\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}m\langle A;N,i|\phi_{m-N+M,n}|A;M,j\rangle

\displaystyle=0\text{ , (for any $n\in\mathbb{Z}-\mathbb{N}$)}.

(358)

This is for
N,M\geqslant 0
. In the following,
M_{-\overrightarrow{i}}
is any string of
M
-operators only (0-length string is also allowed), that acts on the highest-weight state
|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle\equiv|A\rangle
to produce a descendant state.
The second identity is given by

\displaystyle\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\langle A|M_{\overrightarrow{j}}\phi_{m,n}M_{-\overrightarrow{i}}|A\rangle

\displaystyle=0\text{ , (for any $n\in\mathbb{Z^{-}}$)},

(359)

or, as a consequence

\displaystyle\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\langle A|\phi_{m,n}|A\rangle=0\text{ , (for any $n\in\mathbb{Z^{-}}$)}.

(360)

Next, we note down a useful commutation relation

\displaystyle[M_{0},L_{-n}^{l_{n}}]=nl_{n}M_{-n}L_{-n}^{l_{n}-1}.

(361)

Using the above commutation relation and the BCH-lemma, we get the third useful identity given below

\displaystyle e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}\prod_{n=1}^{N}L_{-n}^{l_{n}}=(\prod_{n=1}^{N}e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}L_{-n}^{l_{n}}e^{-2\pi i\rho M_{0}})e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}

\displaystyle=\{\prod_{n=1}^{N}(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{{(2\pi i\rho)}^{k}}{k!}[\underbrace{M_{0},[M_{0},[M_{0},...[M_{0}}_{\text{$k$ times}},L_{-n}^{l_{n}}]]]...])\}e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}

\displaystyle=\{\prod_{n=1}^{N}(\sum_{k=0}^{l_{n}}\frac{{(2\pi in\rho)}^{k}}{k!}\frac{{l_{n}}!}{(l_{n}-k)!}L_{-n}^{l_{n}-k}M_{-n}^{k})\}e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}

\displaystyle=\left(\prod_{n=1}^{N}{(L_{-n}+2\pi in\rho M_{-n})}^{l_{n}}\right)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}.

(362)

These are all the necessary identities for calculating the matrix elements
\langle A,N,i|\phi_{p}(0,1)|A,N,j\rangle
.
We also note that
\phi_{p}^{\dagger}(0,1)=\phi_{p}(0,1)
. Let us we now show below how to use these identities to calculate the three matrix elements in level 1 using (
357
)-(
362
):

{}\begin{split}&\langle A|M_{1}\phi_{p}(0,1)y^{M_{0}}M_{-1}|A\rangle\cr&~=y^{\xi_{A}}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\langle A|M_{1}\phi_{m,0}M_{-1}|A\rangle\\
&~=y^{\xi_{A}}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\langle A|[M_{1},\phi_{m,0}]M_{-1}|A\rangle y^{\xi_{A}}+\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\langle A|\phi_{m,0}M_{1}M_{-1}|A\rangle\cr&~=y^{\xi_{A}}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\langle A|(\xi_{p}\phi_{m+1,0}+\phi_{m+1,-1})M_{-1}|A\rangle+0\cr&~=y^{\xi_{A}}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\langle A|\xi_{p}\phi_{m+1,0}M_{-1}|A\rangle+\langle A|\phi_{m+1,-1}M_{-1}|A\rangle\cr&~=y^{\xi_{A}}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\langle A|\xi_{p}[\phi_{m+1,0},M_{-1}]|A\rangle+y^{\xi_{A}}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\langle A|M_{-1}\xi_{p}\phi_{m+1,0}|A\rangle+0\cr&~=y^{\xi_{A}}\xi_{p}^{2}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\langle A|\phi_{m,0}|A\rangle+y^{\xi_{A}}\xi_{p}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\langle A|\phi_{m,-1}|A\rangle+0\cr&~=y^{\xi_{A}}{\xi_{p}}^{2}\langle A|\phi_{p}(0,1)|A\rangle,\end{split}

(363)

\displaystyle\langle A|L_{1}\phi_{p}(1,0)y^{M_{0}}M_{-1}|A\rangle

(364)

\displaystyle=y^{\xi_{A}}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\langle A|[L_{1},\phi_{m,0}]M_{-1}|A\rangle+y^{\xi_{A}}(2\xi_{A})\langle A|\phi_{p}(1,0)|A\rangle

\displaystyle=y^{\xi_{A}}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}(\Delta_{p}-1-m)\langle A|\phi_{m+1,0}M_{-1}|A\rangle+y^{\xi_{A}}(2\xi_{A})\langle A|\phi_{p}(1,0)|A\rangle

(365)

\displaystyle=y^{\xi_{A}}[\xi_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1)+2\xi_{A}]\langle A|\phi_{p}(1,0)|A\rangle

(366)

(367)

\displaystyle\langle A|M_{1}\phi_{p}(1,0)y^{M_{0}}L_{-1}|A\rangle

(368)

\displaystyle=y^{\xi_{A}}\langle A|M_{1}\phi_{p}(1,0)(L_{-1}+2\pi i\rho M_{-1})|A\rangle

(369)

\displaystyle=y^{\xi_{A}}[{\langle A|L_{1}\phi_{p}(1,0)M_{-1}|A\rangle}^{*}+\langle A|M_{1}2\pi i\rho\phi_{p}(1,0)M_{-1}|A\rangle]

(370)

\displaystyle=y^{\xi_{A}}[(2\pi i\rho){\xi_{p}}^{2}+\xi_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1)+2\xi_{A}]\langle A|\phi_{p}(1,0)|A\rangle

(371)

These results are summarised in (
158
).

Using the commutation relation (
138
) and the identities given above, we could deduce a very useful identity

{}\begin{split}&\hskip-34.14322pt\langle A|M_{n}^{j}L_{n}^{N-j}\phi_{p}(0,1)L_{-n}^{N-k}M_{-n}^{k}|A\rangle\\
&=n^{N-j}\sum_{m=0}^{N-j}\binom{N-j}{m}m!(\Delta_{p}+m-N+j)_{N-j-m}\\
&~~~~\times\sum_{l=0}^{m}\binom{k}{l}\binom{N-k}{m-l}{(2\xi_{A}^{\prime})}^{l}n^{N-k}(\frac{2\Delta_{A}^{\prime}}{n}+N+k-m)_{m-l}\\
&~~~~\times\sum_{r=0}^{N-k-m+l}{(2\xi_{A}^{\prime})}^{r}r!\binom{j}{r}\binom{N-k-m+l}{r}(\Delta_{p}+k-l-j+r)_{N-k-m+l-r}\\
&~~~~~~{(n\xi_{p})}^{k-l+j-r}\langle A|\phi_{p}(0,1)|A\rangle,\end{split}

(372)

where
\Delta_{A}^{\prime}=\Delta_{A}+(n^{2}-1)\frac{c_{L}}{2}
and
\xi_{A}^{\prime}=\xi_{A}+(n^{2}-1)\frac{c_{M}}{2}
. Here, we have also used the Pochhammer symbol

(A)_{i}=\Pi_{m=0}^{i-1}(A+m).

(373)

Using this, we could derive the following repeatedly occurring matrix element

\displaystyle\langle A|M_{n}^{j}L_{n}^{N-j}\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}L_{-n}^{N-k}M_{-n}^{k}|A\rangle

\displaystyle=e^{2\pi i\rho\xi_{A}}\langle A|M_{n}^{j}L_{n}^{N-j}\phi_{p}(0,1)(L_{-n}+2\pi i\rho nM_{-n})^{N-k}M_{-n}^{k}|A\rangle

\displaystyle=e^{2\pi i\rho\xi_{A}}\sum_{s=0}^{N-k}\binom{N-k}{s}(2\pi i\rho n)^{N-k-s}\langle A|M_{n}^{j}L_{n}^{N-j}\phi_{p}(0,1)L_{-n}^{s}M_{-n}^{N-s}|A\rangle.

(374)

and from there, we only require to substitute
k
by
N-s
in (
372
).
Hence,

\begin{split}&\langle A|M_{n}^{j}L_{n}^{N-j}\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}L_{-n}^{N-k}M_{-n}^{k}|A\rangle\\
&\\
&~~=e^{2\pi i\rho\xi_{A}}\sum_{m=0}^{N-j}\binom{N-k}{s}(2\pi i\rho n)^{N-k-s}\big[n^{N-j}\sum_{m=0}^{N-j}\binom{N-j}{m}m!(\Delta_{P}+m-N+j)_{N-j-m}\\
&\\
&~~~~\times\sum_{l=0}^{m}\binom{N-s}{l}\binom{s}{m-l}(2\xi_{A}^{\prime})^{l}n^{s}\left(\frac{2\Delta_{A}^{\prime}}{n}+2n-s-m\right)_{m-l}\\
&\\
&~~~~\times\sum_{r=0}^{s-m-l}(2\xi_{A}^{\prime})^{r}r!\binom{j}{r}\binom{s-m+l}{r}(\Delta_{P}+N-s-l-j+r)_{s-m+l-r}(n\xi_{p})^{N-s-l+j-r}\langle A|\phi_{p}(0,1)|A\rangle\big].\end{split}

(375)

Detail derivations of all these identities are given in below.

C.2
Derivations

In this appendix we will give a derivation of the identities listed above.
First of all, for
N,M\geqslant 0
, we have

\displaystyle\langle A;N,i|L_{0}\phi_{p}(0,1)|A;M,j\rangle=\langle A;N,i|[L_{0},\phi_{p}(0,1)]|A;M,j\rangle+\langle A;N,i|\phi_{p}(0,1)L_{0}|A;M,j\rangle

(376)

\displaystyle\implies\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}(\Delta_{A}+N)\langle A;N,i|\phi_{m,0}|A;M,j\rangle=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\langle A;N,i|(-m)\phi_{m,0}|A;M,j\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 199.16928pt~+\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}(\Delta_{A}+M)\langle A;N,i|\phi_{m,0}|A;M,j\rangle

(377)

\displaystyle\implies\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}(m+N-M)\langle A;N,i|\phi_{m,0}|A;M,j\rangle=0

(378)

\displaystyle\implies\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}m\langle A;N,i|\phi_{m-N+M,0}|A;M,j\rangle=0.

In the above, had we considered
\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\langle A;N,i|L_{0}\phi_{m,n}|A;M,j\rangle
as the starting point (for any
n\leqslant 0
), we would have been led to (
358
) (which is more general than the above)

\displaystyle\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}(m+N-M)\langle A;N,i|\phi_{m,n}|A;M,j\rangle=0

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}m\langle A;N,i|\phi_{m-N+M,n}|A;M,j\rangle=0\text{ , (for any $n\in\mathbb{Z}-\mathbb{N}$)}.

(379)

For any
n\in\mathbb{Z}-\mathbb{N}
, we have:

\displaystyle\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\langle A|M_{\overrightarrow{j}}M_{0}\phi_{m,n}M_{-\overrightarrow{i}}|A\rangle=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}[\langle A|M_{\overrightarrow{j}}[M_{0},\phi_{m,n}]M_{-\overrightarrow{i}}|A\rangle+\langle A|M_{\overrightarrow{j}}\phi_{m,n}M_{0}M_{-\overrightarrow{i}}|A\rangle]

(380)

\displaystyle\implies\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}(1-n)\langle A|M_{\overrightarrow{j}}\phi_{m,n-1}M_{-\overrightarrow{i}}|A\rangle=0.

(381)

This give us the second identity (
359
)

\displaystyle\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\langle A|M_{\overrightarrow{j}}\phi_{m,n}M_{-\overrightarrow{i}}|A\rangle

\displaystyle=0\text{ , (for any $n\in\mathbb{Z^{-}}$)}.

(382)

Now, let us describe how to obtain (
372
).
In the following, we use the notation
\Delta_{A}^{\prime}=\Delta_{A}+(n^{2}-1)\frac{c_{L}}{2}
and
\xi_{A}^{\prime}=\xi_{A}+(n^{2}-1)\frac{c_{M}}{2}
(without the loss of generality we take
j\geqslant k
, for faster evaluation and
n\geqslant 1
).
We will also use the identities below for the next calculation.

\begin{split}&[L_{0},M^{k}_{-n}]=nkM^{k}_{-n},\\
&[L_{0},L^{k}_{-n}]=nkL^{k}_{-n}\\
&[L_{n},L^{N-k}_{-n}]M^{k}_{-n}=n(N-k)\big[n(N+k-1)L^{N-k-1}_{-n}M^{k}_{-n}\\
&~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~+2L_{-n}^{N-k-1}M^{k}_{-n}L_{0}+(n^{2}-1)c_{L}L^{N-k-1}_{-n}M^{k}_{-n}\big]\end{split}

(383)

{}\begin{split}&\langle A|M_{n}^{j}L_{n}^{N-j}\phi_{p}(0,1)L_{-n}^{N-k}M_{-n}^{k}|A\rangle\\
&\\
&={\langle A|M_{n}^{k}L_{n}^{N-k}\phi_{p}(0,1)L_{-n}^{N-j}M_{-n}^{j}|A\rangle}^{*}\cr&\\
&=n(N-k)[2\Delta_{A}^{\prime}+n(N+k-1)]\langle A|M_{n}^{j}L_{n}^{N-j-1}\phi_{p}(0,1)L_{-n}^{N-k-1}M_{-n}^{k}|A\rangle+nk(2\xi_{A}^{\prime})\cr&\\
&\quad\langle A|M_{n}^{j}L_{n}^{N-j-1}\phi_{p}(0,1)L_{-n}^{N-k}M_{-n}^{k-1}|A\rangle+n(\Delta_{p}-1)\langle A|M_{n}^{j}L_{n}^{N-j-1}\phi_{p}(0,1)L_{-n}^{N-k}M_{-n}^{k}|A\rangle\end{split}

{}\begin{split}&=n\sum_{m=0}^{1}\binom{1}{m}m!(\Delta_{p}+m-1)_{1-m}\sum_{l=0}^{m}{(2\xi_{A}^{\prime})}^{l}\binom{k}{l}\binom{N-k}{m-l}n^{m-l}\cr&\qquad\qquad\times(\frac{2\Delta_{A}^{\prime}}{n}+N+k-m)_{m-l}\langle A|M_{n}^{j}L_{n}^{N-j-1}\phi_{p}(0,1)L_{-n}^{N-k-m+l}M_{-n}^{k-l}|A\rangle\cr&\cr&=\ldots\ldots\ldots\text{recursive reduction of the power of $L_{n}$}\ldots\ldots\ldots\cr&\cr&=n^{N-j}\sum_{m=0}^{N-j}\binom{N-j}{m}m!(\Delta_{p}+m-N+j)_{N-j-m}\sum_{l=0}^{m}\binom{k}{l}\binom{N-k}{m-l}\cr&\qquad\qquad\qquad\times{(2\xi_{A}^{\prime})}^{l}n^{m-l}(\frac{2\Delta_{A}^{\prime}}{n}+N+k-m)_{m-l}\langle A|M_{n}^{j}\phi_{p}(0,1)L_{-n}^{N-k-m+l}M_{-n}^{k-l}|A\rangle.\end{split}

(384)

Here, we have used the Pochhammer symbol

(A)_{i}=\prod_{m=0}^{i-1}(A+m).

(385)

By similar kind of calculations, for
p,q,r,\{s_{i}\},\{t_{j}\}\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
, we are led to the following two identities:

\displaystyle\langle A|M_{n}^{p}L_{n}^{q}\phi_{p}(0,1)M_{-n}^{r}|A\rangle

(386)

\displaystyle~={\langle A|M_{n}^{r}\phi_{p}(0,1)L_{-n}^{q}M_{-n}^{p}|A\rangle}^{*}

\displaystyle~=n(2\xi_{A}^{\prime})r\langle A|M_{n}^{p}L_{n}^{q-1}\phi_{p}(0,1)M_{-n}^{r-1}|A\rangle+n(\Delta_{p}+p+q-r-1)\langle A|M_{n}^{p}L_{n}^{q-1}\phi_{p}(0,1)M_{-n}^{r}|A\rangle

\displaystyle~=n\sum_{l=0}^{1}{(2\xi_{A}^{\prime})}^{l}l!\binom{r}{l}\binom{1}{l}(\Delta_{p}+p+q-r-1+l)_{1-l}\langle A|M_{n}^{p}L_{n}^{q-1}\phi_{p}(0,1)M_{-n}^{r-l}|A\rangle

\displaystyle~=\ldots\ldots\ldots\text{recursive reduction of the power of $L_{n}$}\ldots\ldots\ldots

\displaystyle~=n^{q}\sum_{l=0}^{q}{(2\xi_{A}^{\prime})}^{l}l!\binom{r}{l}\binom{q}{l}(\Delta_{p}+p-r+l)_{q-l}\langle A|M_{n}^{p}\phi_{p}(0,1)M_{-n}^{r-l}|A\rangle.

(387)

Also,

{}\begin{split}&\hskip-14.22636pt\langle A|(\prod_{i=1}^{P}M_{n_{i}}^{s_{i}})\phi_{p}(0,1)\prod_{j=1}^{Q}M_{-m_{j}}^{t_{j}}|A\rangle=\langle A|(\prod_{j=1}^{Q}M_{m_{j}}^{t_{j}})\phi_{p}(0,1)\prod_{i=1}^{P}M_{-n_{i}}^{s_{i}}|A\rangle^{*}\\
&=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\langle A|(\prod_{i=2}^{P}M_{n_{i}}^{s_{i}})M_{n_{1}}^{s_{1}-1}(n_{1}\xi_{p}\phi_{m+n_{1},0}+\phi_{m+n_{1},-1})\prod_{j=1}^{Q}M_{-m_{j}}^{t_{j}}|A\rangle\\
&=n_{1}\xi_{p}\langle A|(\prod_{i=2}^{P}M_{n_{i}}^{s_{i}})M_{n_{1}}^{s_{1}-1}\phi_{p}(0,1)\prod_{j=1}^{Q}M_{-m_{j}}^{t_{j}}|A\rangle+0\\
&={(n_{1}\xi_{p})}^{s_{1}}{\langle A|(\prod_{i=2}^{P}M_{n_{i}}^{s_{i}})\phi_{p}(0,1)\prod_{j=1}^{Q}M_{-m_{j}}^{t_{j}}|A\rangle}=(\prod_{i=1}^{P}{(n_{i}\xi_{p})}^{s_{i}})\langle A|(\prod_{j=1}^{Q}M_{m_{j}}^{t_{j}})\phi_{p}(0,1)|A\rangle^{*}\\
&=(\prod_{i=1}^{P}{(n_{i}\xi_{p})}^{s_{i}})(\prod_{j=1}^{Q}{(m_{j}\xi_{p})}^{t_{j}}){\langle A|\phi_{p}(0,1)|A\rangle},\end{split}

(388)

which imply that

\langle A|M_{n}^{p}L_{n}^{q}\phi_{p}(0,1)M_{-n}^{r}|A\rangle=n^{q}\sum_{l=0}^{q}{(2\xi_{A}^{\prime})}^{l}l!\binom{r}{l}\binom{q}{l}(\Delta_{p}+p-r+l)_{q-l}{(n\xi_{p})}^{p+r-l}\langle A|\phi_{p}(0,1)|A\rangle.

(389)

Thus finally we obtain (
372
)

\displaystyle\langle A|M_{n}^{j}L_{n}^{N-j}\phi_{p}(0,1)L_{-n}^{N-k}M_{-n}^{k}|A\rangle

(390)

\displaystyle~~=n^{N-j}\sum_{m=0}^{N-j}\binom{N-j}{m}m!(\Delta_{p}+m-N+j)_{N-j-m}\sum_{l=0}^{m}\binom{k}{l}\binom{N-k}{m-l}{(2\xi_{A}^{\prime})}^{l}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times n^{m-l}(\frac{2\Delta_{A}^{\prime}}{n}+N+k-m)_{m-l}{\langle A|M_{n}^{k-l}L_{n}^{N-k-m+l}\phi_{p}(0,1)M_{-n}^{j}|A\rangle}^{*}

\displaystyle~~=n^{N-j}\sum_{m=0}^{N-j}\binom{N-j}{m}m!(\Delta_{p}+m-N+j)_{N-j-m}\sum_{l=0}^{m}\binom{k}{l}\binom{N-k}{m-l}{(2\xi_{A}^{\prime})}^{l}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times n^{N-k}(\frac{2\Delta_{A}^{\prime}}{n}+N+k-m)_{m-l}\sum_{r=0}^{N-k-m+l}{(2\xi_{A}^{\prime})}^{r}r!\binom{j}{r}\binom{N-k-m+l}{r}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times(\Delta_{p}+k-l-j+r)_{N-k-m+l-r}{(n\xi_{p})}^{k-l+j-r}\langle A|\phi_{p}(0,1)|A\rangle.

(391)

Using this, we arrive at our desired, repeatedly occurring matrix element:

\displaystyle\langle A|M_{n}^{j}L_{n}^{N-j}\phi_{p}(1,0)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}L_{-n}^{N-k}M_{-n}^{k}|A\rangle

(392)

\displaystyle~~~=e^{2\pi i\rho\xi_{A}}\langle A|M_{n}^{j}L_{n}^{N-j}\phi_{p}(1,0)(L_{-n}+2\pi i\rho nM_{-n})^{N-k}M_{-n}^{k}|A\rangle

(393)

\displaystyle~~~=e^{2\pi i\rho\xi_{A}}\sum_{s=0}^{N-k}\binom{N-k}{s}(2\pi i\rho n)^{N-k-s}\langle A|M_{n}^{j}L_{n}^{N-j}\phi_{p}(1,0)L_{-n}^{s}M_{-n}^{N-s}|A\rangle

(394)

and from there, we only require to substitute
k
by
N-s
in the last line.
Finally we obtain (
375
),

\boxed{\begin{split}&\langle A|M_{n}^{j}L_{n}^{N-j}\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}L_{-n}^{N-k}M_{-n}^{k}|A\rangle\\
&=e^{2\pi i\rho\xi_{A}}\sum_{m=0}^{N-j}\binom{N-k}{s}(2\pi i\rho n)^{N-k-s}\big[n^{N-j}\sum_{m=0}^{N-j}\binom{N-j}{m}m!(\Delta_{P}+m-N+j)_{N-j-m}\\
&\sum_{l=0}^{m}\binom{N-s}{l}\binom{s}{m-l}(2\xi_{A}^{\prime})^{l}n^{s}\left(\frac{2\Delta_{A}^{\prime}}{n}+2n-s-m\right)_{m-l}\\
&\sum_{r=0}^{s-m-l}(2\xi_{A}^{\prime})^{r}r!\binom{j}{r}\binom{s-m+l}{r}(\Delta_{P}+N-s-l-j+r)_{s-m+l-r}\\
&(n\xi_{p})^{N-s-l+j-r}\langle A|\phi_{p}(0,1)|A\rangle\big]\end{split}}

(395)

Using the identities given above we calculate for level 2 the matrix elements that contribute to
\mathcal{F}_{2}(\Delta_{p},\xi_{p};\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|c_{L},c_{M}|\rho)
. These are listed below

\displaystyle\frac{\langle A|L_{1}^{2}\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}M_{-1}^{2}|A\rangle}{\langle A|\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}|A\rangle}=8{\xi_{A}}^{2}+8\xi_{A}\xi_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1)+{\xi_{p}}^{2}(\Delta_{p}-2)_{2},

(396)

\displaystyle\frac{\langle A|M_{1}^{2}\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}L_{-1}^{2}|A\rangle}{\langle A|\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}|A\rangle}=8{\xi_{A}}^{2}+8\xi_{A}\xi_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1)+{\xi_{p}}^{2}(\Delta_{p}-2)_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+2(2\pi i\rho){\xi_{p}}^{2}[4\xi_{A}+\xi_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1)]+(2\pi i\rho)^{2}{\xi_{p}}^{4},

(397)

\displaystyle\frac{\langle A|L_{2}\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}M_{-2}|A\rangle}{\langle A|\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}|A\rangle}=4\xi_{A}+6c_{M}+4\xi_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1),

(398)

\displaystyle\frac{\langle A|M_{2}\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}L_{-2}|A\rangle}{\langle A|\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}|A\rangle}=4\xi_{A}+6c_{M}+4\xi_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1)+(2\pi i\rho)8{\xi_{p}}^{2},

(399)

\displaystyle\frac{\langle A|M_{1}L_{1}\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}L_{-1}M_{-1}|A\rangle}{\langle A|\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}|A\rangle}=4\xi_{A}^{2}+4\xi_{A}\xi_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1)+\xi_{p}^{2}[2(\Delta_{A}+1)+\Delta_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1)]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+(2\pi i\rho){\xi_{p}}^{2}[4\xi_{A}+\xi_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1)],

(400)

\displaystyle\frac{\langle A|M_{2}\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}M_{-2}|A\rangle}{\langle A|\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}|A\rangle}=4\xi_{p}^{2},

(401)

\displaystyle\frac{\langle A|M_{2}\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}M_{-1}^{2}|A\rangle}{\langle A|\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}|A\rangle}=\frac{\langle A|M_{1}^{2}\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}M_{-2}|A\rangle}{\langle A|\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}|A\rangle}=2\xi_{p}^{3},\text{ (using \eqref{eq4})},

(402)

\displaystyle\frac{\langle A|M_{1}^{2}\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}M_{-1}^{2}|A\rangle}{\langle A|\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}|A\rangle}=\xi_{p}^{4},

(403)

\displaystyle\frac{\langle A|L_{2}\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}M_{-1}^{2}|A\rangle}{\langle A|\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}|A\rangle}=2(\Delta_{p}-1)\xi_{p}^{2},

(404)

\displaystyle\frac{\langle A|M_{1}^{2}\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}L_{-2}|A\rangle}{\langle A|\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}|A\rangle}=2(\Delta_{p}-1)\xi_{p}^{2}+4(2\pi i\rho)\xi_{p}^{3},

(405)

\displaystyle\frac{\langle A|M_{1}L_{1}\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}M_{-1}^{2}|A\rangle}{\langle A|\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}|A\rangle}=\xi_{p}^{2}[4\xi_{A}+\xi_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1)],

(406)

\displaystyle\frac{\langle A|M_{1}^{2}\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}L_{-1}M_{-1}|A\rangle}{\langle A|\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}|A\rangle}=\xi_{p}^{2}[4\xi_{A}+\xi_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1)]+(2\pi i\rho)\xi_{p}^{4},

(407)

\displaystyle\frac{\langle A|M_{1}L_{1}\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}M_{-2}|A\rangle}{\langle A|\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}|A\rangle}=2(\Delta_{p}-1)\xi_{p}^{2}+3\xi_{p}^{2}=(2\Delta_{p}+1)\xi_{p}^{2},

(408)

\displaystyle\frac{\langle A|M_{2}\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}L_{-1}M_{-1}|A\rangle}{\langle A|\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}|A\rangle}=(2\Delta_{p}+1)\xi_{p}^{2}+2(2\pi i\rho)\xi_{p}^{3}.

(409)

Appendix D
Matrix elements for level 1

In this appendix we give another way of calculating
\langle\Psi_{i}|\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}|\Psi_{j}\rangle
for level 1. For the first matrix elements we have

\displaystyle\langle\Psi_{1}|\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi iM_{0}}|\Psi_{2}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|L_{1}\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi iM_{0}}M_{-1}|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle

(410)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|L_{1}\phi_{p}(0,1)M_{-1}e^{2\pi iM_{0}}|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle

(411)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{2\pi i\xi_{A}}\langle\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|L_{1}\phi_{p}(0,1)M_{-1}|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle.

(412)

The three point function

\langle\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|L_{1}\phi_{p}(0,1)M_{-1}|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle,

(413)

can be calculated using

\langle\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|\phi_{p}(t,x)|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle=C_{ApA}\,x^{-\Delta_{p}}e^{\xi_{p}\frac{t}{x}},

(414)

and

\displaystyle[L_{n},\phi_{\Delta,\xi}(t,x)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[x^{n+1}\partial_{x}+(n+1)x^{n}t\partial_{t}+(n+1)(\Delta x^{n}-n\xi x^{n-1}t)]\phi_{\Delta,\xi}(t,x)

(415)

\displaystyle[M_{n},\phi_{\Delta,\xi}(t,x)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[-x^{n+1}\partial_{t}+(n+1)\xi x^{n}]\phi_{\Delta,\xi}(t,x).

(416)

More precisely, we have

\displaystyle\hskip-42.67912pt\langle\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|L_{1}\phi_{p}(t,x)M_{-1}|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle

(417)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|L_{1}[\phi_{p}(t,x),M_{-1}]|\Delta,\xi\rangle+\langle\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|L_{1}M_{-1}\phi_{p}(t,x)|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle

(418)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\langle\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|[L_{1},\phi_{p}(t,x)]|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle+\langle\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|2M_{0}\phi_{p}(t,x)|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle

(419)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(t^{2}\partial_{x}+2xt\partial_{t}+2(\Delta_{p}x-\xi_{p}t))x^{-\Delta_{p}}e^{\xi_{p}\frac{t}{x}}+2\xi x^{-\Delta_{p}}e^{\xi_{p}\frac{t}{x}}

(420)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-x^{-1}\xi_{p}\left(-\Delta_{p}x+x+\xi_{p}t\right)\langle\Delta,\xi|\phi_{p}(t,x)|\Delta,\xi\rangle+2\xi_{A}\langle\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|\phi_{p}(t,x)|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle.~

(421)

Thus

\langle\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|L_{1}\phi_{p}(0,1)M_{-1}|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle=C_{ApA}(\xi_{p}\Delta_{p}-\xi_{p}+2\xi_{A}).

(422)

So, finally

\langle\Psi_{1}|\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi iM_{0}}|\Psi_{2}\rangle=C_{ApA}\,y^{\xi_{A}}(\xi_{p}\Delta_{p}-\xi_{p}+2\xi_{A}).

(423)

Using (
362
) and calculating the three-point functions like what we have just done, we get

\displaystyle\hskip-17.07182pt\langle\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|M_{1}\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi iM_{0}}L_{-1}|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle

(424)

\displaystyle=\langle\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|M_{1}\phi_{p}(0,1)L_{-1}e^{2\pi iM_{0}}|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle+2\pi i\rho\langle\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|M_{1}\phi_{p}(0,1)M_{-1}e^{2\pi iM_{0}}|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle

(425)

\displaystyle=e^{2\pi i\xi_{A}}\langle\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|M_{1}\phi_{p}(0,1)L_{-1}|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle+2\pi i\rho e^{2\pi i\xi_{A}}\langle\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|M_{1}\phi_{p}(0,1)M_{-1}|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle

(426)

\displaystyle=y^{\xi_{A}}C_{ApA}(\xi_{p}\Delta_{p}-\xi_{p}+2\xi_{A}+2\pi i\rho\xi_{p}^{2}).

(427)

For the fourth term in (
155
), we have

\displaystyle\hskip-45.52458pt\langle\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|M_{1}\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi iM_{0}}M_{-1}|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle

(428)

\displaystyle=\langle\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|M_{1}\phi_{p}(0,1)M_{-1}e^{2\pi iM_{0}}|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle

(429)

\displaystyle=y^{\xi_{A}}\langle\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|M_{1}\phi_{p}(0,1)M_{-1}|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle

(430)

\displaystyle=y^{\xi_{A}}C_{ApA}\,\xi_{p}^{2}.

(431)

Appendix E
Proof for minimum number of uncontracted
M
-operators

In this appendix we will proof that for the generic
BMS_{3}
Gram-matrix element with all
N_{j}\geqslant k_{j}\geqslant 0
,
N_{j}^{\prime}\geqslant k_{j}^{\prime}\geqslant 0
and
\sum_{j=1}^{N}jN_{j}=\sum_{j=1}^{N}jN_{j}^{\prime}=N
:

\displaystyle\langle A|(\prod_{j=N}^{1}M_{j}^{k_{j}^{\prime}})(\prod_{j=N}^{1}L_{j}^{N_{j}^{\prime}-k_{j}^{\prime}})(\prod_{j=1}^{N}L_{-j}^{N_{j}-k_{j}})(\prod_{j=1}^{N}M_{-j}^{k_{j}})|A\rangle,

(432)

the minimum number of ‘uncontracted’
M
-operators inside the bra-ket of inner-product is 2.

Let’s assume that the total number of ‘uncontracted’
M
-operators inside the bra-ket of inner product is
m\geqslant 2
and that collection is denoted by
\{M_{i_{r}}\}
with
1\leqslant r\leqslant m
and
i_{r}\in\mathbb{Z}-\{0\}
. Now, define the sum of
L
-indexes of a collection
L
of
n
-numbers of
L
-operators
\{L_{j}\}
with
j\in\mathbb{Z}-\{0\}
simply as
\sum_{L_{j}\in L}j
; the sum of
M
-indexes is similarly defined. To get an
M_{j}
with
j\in\mathbb{Z}-\{0\}
contracted, a collection of
L
-operators with sum of indexes equaling to
-j
is needed. Thus,

\text{sum of}\,M\text{-indexes that have been contracted}=-\text{sum of}\,L\text{-indexes used in contraction}.

(433)

Also, according to the starting assumption, along with the uncontracted
\{M_{i_{r}}\}
, there must also remain a collection (including the empty one) of
L
-operators, the elements of which can’t be grouped into sub-collections with sum of indexes
-i_{r}
for any
1\leqslant r\leqslant m
, to leave the whole
\{M_{i_{r}}\}
uncontracted. But, as the two states giving rise to the inner product are at the same level of the
BMS_{3}
module, we have:

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{N}j(k_{j}^{\prime}-k_{j})=-\sum_{j=1}^{N}j[(N_{j}^{\prime}-k_{j}^{\prime})-(N_{j}-k_{j})]

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\text{(sum of all $M$-indexes)}=-\text{(sum of all $L$-indexes)}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\text{(sum of `contracted' $M$-indexes)}+\sum_{r=1}^{m}i_{r}=-[\text{(sum of `contracted' $L$-indexes)}

(434)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+\text{(sum of remaining $L$-indexes)}]

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\text{ }-\sum_{r=1}^{m}i_{r}=\text{(sum of remaining $L$-indexes)}

(435)

Even though no summand from the L.H.S. equals to any summand from the R.H.S., it is always the case that the two sums are equal to one another for
m\geqslant 2
. But for
m=1
, i.e. when only one
M_{i}
is uncontracted, we also have a collection (including the empty one) of
L
-operators, with sum of indexes
-k
, with
k\neq i
to leave that
M_{i}
uncontracted. But the condition of same level implies that
i=k
. Thus, we are led to a contradiction for
m=1
and hence, we conclude that at least 2 among all the
M
-operators must be left uncontracted.

Appendix F
Global torus blocks

As an aside, to highlight an application of the rather cumbersome formula (
372
), we outline the steps to calculate the global
BMS_{3}
torus block. In the highest-weight representation of the global
BMS_{3}
algebra, the descendant states at level N in the
BMS_{3}
module of the primary field with dimension
(\Delta_{A},\xi_{A})
are given by
\{L_{-1}^{N-k}M_{-1}^{k}|A\rangle\}
, with
0\leqslant k\leqslant N
; so, there are
N+1
descendants at level N. The generic matrix elements in the global version of (
149
) can be computed very easily using (
372
) with
n=1
(for convenience, we assume
j\leqslant k
):

\displaystyle\frac{\langle A|M_{1}^{j}L_{1}^{N-j}\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}L_{-1}^{N-k}M_{-1}^{k}|A\rangle}{\langle A|\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}|A\rangle}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{s=0}^{N-k}\binom{N-k}{s}(2\pi i\rho)^{N-k-s}\sum_{m=0}^{N-j}\binom{N-j}{m}m!(\Delta_{p}+m-N+j)_{N-j-m}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{l=0}^{m}{(2\xi_{A})}^{m-l}\binom{N-s}{m-l}\binom{s}{l}(2\Delta_{A}+2N-s-m)_{l}\sum_{r=0}^{s-l}{(2\xi_{A})}^{r}r!\binom{j}{r}

\displaystyle\times\binom{s-l}{r}(\Delta_{p}+N-j-m-s+l+r)_{s-l-r}{\xi_{p}}^{N-s+l+j-m-r}.

(436)

While deriving the previous equation we have replaced
k
by
N-s
, and
l
by
m-l
in (
372
).
Also, it can be easily shown that the generic element of the global
BMS_{3}
Gram-matrix takes the following form:

\displaystyle\langle A|M_{1}^{j}L_{1}^{N-j}L_{-1}^{N-k}M_{-1}^{k}|A\rangle=\langle A|M_{1}^{k}L_{1}^{N-k}L_{-1}^{N-j}M_{-1}^{j}|A\rangle

\displaystyle={(2\xi_{A})}^{j+k}\sum_{l=0}^{j}\binom{j}{l}\frac{(N-j)!(N-k)!}{(N-j-k-l)!}(2\Delta_{A}+k+l)_{N-j-k-l}.

(437)

From the above expression, it is evident that
\langle A|M_{1}^{j}L_{1}^{N-j}L_{-1}^{N-k}M_{-1}^{k}|A\rangle=0
, when
N<j+k
, i.e. for the global
BMS_{3}
algebra also, the Gram-matrix can be brought into an upper anti-triangular form. Hence, the inverse Gram-matrix must be lower anti-triangular. Thus, for
N>j+k
, the
\langle A|M_{1}^{j}L_{1}^{N-j}\phi_{p}(0,1)e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}L_{-1}^{N-k}M_{-1}^{k}|A\rangle
matrix elements don’t contribute to the global torus block. Finally, putting all the components in right places as in the global version of (
149
), we get the expression for the global torus block. This method thus offers a different route to calculate these global block as compared to the differential equation way of doing this outlined in the main text.

Appendix G
Alternate derivation: Large
\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}
limit of one-point blocks

In this appendix we gave an alternative derivation of the leading term in the large
\xi_{A}
limit. We also explicitly find the subleading term for level 2.

G.1
Leading term

We insert the identity operator for states at level
N

{\mathbb{1}}_{N}=\sum_{k,l}K^{kl}_{(N)}|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A};N,k\rangle\langle\Delta_{A},\xi_{A};N,l|

(438)

to the RHS of (
149
) giving us

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{N}(\Delta_{p},\xi_{p};\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|c_{L},c_{M}|\rho)

(439)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{y^{\xi_{A}}C_{ApA}}\sum_{i,j,k,l}K^{ij}_{(N)}K^{kl}_{(N)}\langle\Delta_{A},\xi_{A};N,i|\phi_{p}(0,1)|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A};N,k\rangle

(441)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\times\langle\Delta_{A},\xi_{A};N,l|e^{2\pi iM_{0}}|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A};N,j\rangle.

Let us schematically write the states as

|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A};N,k\rangle=L_{\overrightarrow{r}_{k}}M_{\overrightarrow{s}_{k}}|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle,

(442)

where
\overrightarrow{r}_{k}
and
\overrightarrow{s}_{k}
represents a concatenation of the the
L
’s and
M
’s for the
k
-th state. In this notation

\displaystyle\langle\Delta_{A},\xi_{A};N,i|\phi_{p}(0,1)|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A};N,k\rangle

(443)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|(M_{\overrightarrow{s}_{i}})^{\dagger}(L_{\overrightarrow{r}_{i}})^{\dagger}\phi_{p}(0,1)L_{\overrightarrow{r}_{k}}M_{\overrightarrow{s}_{k}}|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle

(444)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|(M_{\overrightarrow{s}_{i}})^{\dagger}(L_{\overrightarrow{s}_{i}})^{\dagger}L_{\overrightarrow{r}_{k}}M_{\overrightarrow{s}_{k}}\phi_{p}(0,1)|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle

(445)

\displaystyle+\langle\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|(M_{\overrightarrow{s}_{i}})^{\dagger}(L_{\overrightarrow{r}_{i}})^{\dagger}[\phi_{p}(0,1),L_{\overrightarrow{r}_{k}}M_{\overrightarrow{s}_{k}}]|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle

(446)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|(M_{\overrightarrow{s}_{i}})^{\dagger}(L_{\overrightarrow{r}_{i}})^{\dagger}L_{\overrightarrow{r}_{k}}M_{\overrightarrow{s}_{k}}|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle\langle\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|\phi_{p}(0,1)|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle

(447)

\displaystyle+\langle\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|(M_{\overrightarrow{s}_{i}})^{\dagger}(L_{\overrightarrow{r}_{i}})^{\dagger}[\phi_{p}(0,1),L_{\overrightarrow{r}_{k}}M_{\overrightarrow{s}_{k}}]|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle

(448)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K_{ik}^{(N)}C_{ApA}+\langle\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|(M_{\overrightarrow{q_{i}}})^{\dagger}(L_{\overrightarrow{r}_{i}})^{\dagger}[\phi_{p}(0,1),L_{\overrightarrow{s}_{k}}M_{\overrightarrow{s}_{k}}]|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle.

(449)

Substituting this in (
441
), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{N}(\Delta_{p},\xi_{p};\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|c_{L},c_{M}|\rho)

(450)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{y^{\xi_{A}}}\sum_{i,j,k,l}K^{ij}_{(N)}K^{kl}_{(N)}K_{ik}^{(N)}\langle\Delta_{A},\xi_{A};N,l|e^{2\pi iM_{0}}|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A};N,j\rangle

(453)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{y^{\xi_{A}}C_{ApA}}\sum_{i,j,k,l}K^{ij}_{(N)}K^{kl}_{(N)}\langle\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|(M_{\overrightarrow{q_{i}}})^{\dagger}(L_{\overrightarrow{q_{i}}})^{\dagger}[\phi_{p}(0,1),L_{\overrightarrow{p_{k}}}M_{\overrightarrow{q_{k}}}]|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\times\langle\Delta_{A},\xi_{A};N,l|e^{2\pi iM_{0}}|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A};N,j\rangle.

The presence of a commutator inside a three-point function decrease the exponent of
\Delta_{A}
or
\xi_{A}
. Thus the first term which does not have a commutator is the leading order in
\Delta_{A}
and
\xi_{A}
. As we will see this is zeroth order in
\Delta_{A}
and
\xi_{A}
. The other term contain subleading term (order
\mathcal{O}(\frac{1}{\Delta_{A}},\frac{1}{\xi_{A}})
)and higher order corrections.
We can simplify the first term
using
\sum_{k}K^{kl}_{(N)}K_{ik}^{(N)}=\delta^{l}_{i}
and using that fact that tracing the operator
e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}
over the states in level
N
gives
y^{\xi_{A}}
times the number of states in level
N

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\frac{1}{y^{\xi_{A}}}\sum_{i,j,l}K^{ij}_{(N)}\delta^{l}_{i}\langle\Delta_{A},\xi_{A};N,l|e^{2\pi iM_{0}}|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A};N,j\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{y^{\xi_{A}}}\sum_{i,j}K^{ij}_{(N)}\langle\Delta_{A},\xi_{A};N,i|e^{2\pi iM_{0}}|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A};N,j\rangle

(454)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{y^{\xi_{A}}}{\rm Tr}_{N}e^{2\pi iM_{0}}=\frac{1}{y^{\xi_{A}}}\widetilde{\rm\dim}_{N}\,y^{\xi_{A}}=\widetilde{\rm\dim}_{N}.

(455)

Thus the leading term of
\mathcal{F}_{N}
is just the number states at level
N

\mathcal{F}_{N}(\Delta_{p},\xi_{p};\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|c_{L},c_{M}|\rho)=\widetilde{\rm\dim}_{N}+\mathcal{O}(\frac{1}{\xi_{A}},\frac{\Delta_{A}}{\xi_{A}^{2}})+...

(456)

G.2
Sub-leading term for level 2

We can read off the subleading term in level 1 from (
159
), it is given by

\frac{1}{\xi_{A}}\left(\Delta_{p}\xi_{p}-\xi_{p}-\frac{\Delta_{A}}{2\xi^{2}_{A}}\xi_{p}^{2}+\pi i\rho\xi_{p}^{2}\right).

(457)

In this subsection we will explicitly obtain the subleading term for level 2. For this level the basis states are

\begin{array}[]{l}|\Psi_{1}\rangle=L_{-1}^{2}|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle,\,\,\,|\Psi_{2}\rangle=L_{-2}|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle,\,\,\,|\Psi_{3}\rangle=L_{-1}M_{-1}|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle,\,\,\,|\Psi_{4}\rangle=M_{-2}|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle\\
|\Psi_{5}\rangle=M_{-1}^{2}|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle.\end{array}.

(458)

From (
441
), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{N}(\Delta_{p},\xi_{p};\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|c_{L},c_{M}|\rho)

(459)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{y^{\xi_{A}}C_{ApA}}\sum_{i,j,k,l}K^{ij}_{(N)}K^{kl}_{(N)}\mathcal{P}_{ik}\langle\Psi_{l}|e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}|\Psi_{j}\rangle,

(460)

where
\mathcal{P}_{ik}=\langle\Psi_{i}|\phi_{p}(0,1)|\Psi_{k}\rangle
. We rewrite the last factor as

\displaystyle\langle\Psi_{l}|e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}|\Psi_{j}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle\Psi_{l}|e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}L_{\overrightarrow{r}_{j}}M_{\overrightarrow{s}_{j}}|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle

(461)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle\Psi_{l}|L_{\overrightarrow{r}_{j}}M_{\overrightarrow{s}_{j}}e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}}|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle+\langle\Psi_{l}|[e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}},L_{\overrightarrow{r}_{j}}M_{\overrightarrow{s}_{j}}]|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle

(462)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{2\pi i\rho\xi_{A}}\langle\Psi_{l}|L_{\overrightarrow{r}_{j}}M_{\overrightarrow{s}_{j}}|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle+\langle\Psi_{l}|[e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}},L_{\overrightarrow{r}_{j}}M_{\overrightarrow{s}_{j}}]|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle

(463)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle y^{\xi_{A}}K_{lj}^{(N)}+\langle\Psi_{l}|[e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}},L_{\overrightarrow{r}_{j}}M_{\overrightarrow{s}_{j}}]|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle.

(464)

Substituting back in the previous equation and using
\sum_{l}K^{kl}_{(N)}K_{lj}^{(N)}=\delta^{k}_{j}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{N}(\Delta_{p},\xi_{p};\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}|c_{L},c_{M}|\rho)

(465)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{C_{ApA}}\sum_{i,j,k}K^{ij}_{(N)}\mathcal{P}_{ik}\delta^{k}_{j}+\frac{1}{y^{\xi_{A}}C_{ApA}}\sum_{i,j,k,l}K^{ij}_{(N)}K^{kl}_{(N)}\mathcal{P}_{ik}\langle\Psi_{l}|[e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}},L_{\overrightarrow{r}_{j}}M_{\overrightarrow{s}_{j}}]|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle

(466)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{C_{ApA}}\sum_{i,j}K^{ij}_{(N)}\mathcal{P}_{ij}+\frac{1}{y^{\xi_{A}}C_{ApA}}\sum_{i,j,k,l}K^{ij}_{(N)}K^{kl}_{(N)}\mathcal{P}_{ik}\langle\Psi_{l}|[e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}},L_{\overrightarrow{r}_{j}}M_{\overrightarrow{s}_{j}}]|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle.

(467)

The Gram Matrix for level 2 is

\displaystyle K^{(2)}=\left[\begin{array}[]{ccccc}4\Delta_{A}(2\Delta_{A}+1)&6\Delta_{A}&4\xi_{A}(2\Delta_{A}+1)&6\xi_{A}&8\xi^{2}_{A}\\
6\Delta_{A}&4\Delta_{A}+6c_{L}&6\xi_{A}&4\xi_{A}+6c_{M}&0\\
4\xi_{A}(2\Delta_{A}+1)&6\xi_{A}&4\xi^{2}_{A}&0&0\\
6\xi_{A}&4\xi_{A}+6c_{M}&0&0&0\\
8\xi^{2}_{A}&0&0&0&0\end{array}\right].

For the inverse of the matrix, all the entries are of order
\frac{1}{\xi_{A}}
or less. To order in
\frac{1}{\xi^{2}_{A}}
, we have

K_{(2)}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccccc}0&0&0&0&\frac{1}{8\xi^{2}_{A}}\\
0&0&0&\frac{1}{4\xi_{A}}&-\frac{3}{16\xi^{2}_{A}}\\
0&0&\frac{1}{4\xi^{2}_{A}}&-\frac{3}{8\xi^{2}_{A}}&-\frac{\Delta_{A}}{(4\xi_{A})\xi^{2}_{A}}\\
0&\frac{1}{4\xi_{A}}&-\frac{3}{8\xi^{2}_{A}}&-\frac{\Delta_{A}}{(4\xi_{A})\xi_{A}}&\frac{3\Delta_{A}}{(8\xi_{A})\xi^{2}_{A}}\\
\frac{1}{8\xi^{2}_{A}}&-\frac{3}{16\xi^{2}_{A}}&-\frac{\Delta_{A}}{(4\xi_{A})\xi^{2}_{A}}&\frac{3\Delta_{A}}{(8\xi_{A})\xi^{2}_{A}}&\frac{\Delta^{2}_{A}}{\left(8\xi^{2}_{A}\right)\xi^{2}_{A}}\\
\end{array}\right).

(475)

Next, for
\mathcal{P}_{ij}\equiv\langle\Psi_{i}|\phi_{p}(0,1)|\Psi_{i}\rangle
we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{ApA}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccccc}-&-&-&-&K_{15}\\
-&-&-&K_{24}&K_{25}\\
-&-&K_{33}&K_{34}&K_{35}\\
-&K_{42}&K_{43}&K_{44}&K_{45}\\
K_{51}&K_{52}&K_{53}&K_{54}&K_{55}\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle+C_{ApA}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccccc}-&-&-&-&8\xi_{A}\left(\Delta_{p}-1\right)\xi_{p}\\
-&-&-&4\left(\Delta_{p}-1\right)\xi_{p}&0\\
-&-&4\xi_{A}\left(\Delta_{p}-1\right)\xi_{p}+2\Delta\xi_{p}^{2}&0&4\xi_{A}\xi_{p}^{2}\\
-&4\left(\Delta_{p}-1\right)\xi_{p}&0&4\xi_{p}^{2}&0\\
8\xi\left(\Delta_{p}-1\right)\xi_{p}&0&4\xi_{A}\xi_{p}^{2}&0&0\end{array}\right),

where we have shown only the terms needed to calculate to order
\frac{1}{\xi_{A}}
. We already know that the leading order is
5
, the number of sates at level
2
. Using the above matrices, we can find the sub-leading term in
\sum_{i,j}K^{ij}_{(2)}\mathcal{P}_{ij}

\frac{1}{C_{ApA}}\sum_{i,j}K^{ij}_{(2)}\mathcal{P}_{ij}=5+\frac{5}{\xi_{A}}\left(\Delta_{p}\xi_{p}-\xi_{p}-\frac{\Delta_{A}}{2\xi_{A}}\xi_{p}^{2}\right).

(489)

Now, let us calculate the sub-leading order in
\sum_{i,j,k,l}K^{ij}_{(2)}K^{kl}_{(2)}\mathcal{P}_{ik}\langle\Psi_{l}|[e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}},L_{\overrightarrow{r}_{j}}M_{\overrightarrow{s}_{j}}]|\Delta_{A},\xi_{A}\rangle
. For this we have

\displaystyle\langle\Psi_{l}|[e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}},L_{-1}L_{-1}]|\Delta,\xi\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\pi i\rho\,y^{\xi_{A}}K_{l3}+(2\pi i\rho)^{2}\,y^{\xi_{A}}K_{l5}

(490)

\displaystyle\langle\Psi_{l}|[e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}},L_{-2}]|\Delta,\xi\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\pi i\rho\,y^{\xi_{A}}K_{l4}

(491)

\displaystyle\langle\Psi_{l}|[e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}},L_{-1}M_{-1}]|\Delta,\xi\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\pi i\rho\,y^{\xi_{A}}K_{l5}

(492)

\displaystyle\langle\Psi_{l}|[e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}},M_{-2}]|\Delta,\xi\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

(493)

\displaystyle\langle\Psi_{l}|[e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}},M_{-1}M_{-1}]|\Delta,\xi\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.

(494)

Using
\sum_{cd}K^{ad}K_{d\lambda}=\delta^{c}_{\lambda}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j,k,l}K^{ij}K^{kl}\mathcal{P}_{ik}\langle\Psi_{l}|[e^{2\pi i\rho M_{0}},L_{\overrightarrow{r}_{j}}M_{\overrightarrow{s}_{j}}]|\Delta,\xi\rangle

(495)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle y^{\xi_{A}}\sum_{i}4\pi i\rho K^{i1}\mathcal{P}_{i3}+(2\pi i\rho)^{2}K^{i1}\mathcal{P}_{i5}+4\pi i\rho K^{i2}\rho(\Psi_{i},\Psi_{4})+2\pi i\rho K^{i3}\mathcal{P}_{i5}

(496)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle y^{\xi_{A}}\left(4\pi i\rho K^{51}\mathcal{P}_{53}+4\pi i\rho K^{42}\mathcal{P}_{44}+2\pi i\rho K^{33}\mathcal{P}_{35}\right)

(497)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle y^{\xi_{A}}8\pi i\rho\frac{\xi_{p}^{2}}{\xi_{A}}.

(498)

So, the
\frac{1}{\xi_{A}}
order contribution to level 2 is given by

\frac{5}{\xi_{A}}\left(\Delta_{p}\xi_{p}-\xi_{p}-\frac{\Delta_{A}}{2\xi_{A}}\xi_{p}^{2}\right)+8\pi i\rho\frac{\xi_{p}^{2}}{\xi_{A}}.

(499)

Appendix H
BMS torus blocks as a limit of CFT

The form of the BMS one-point torus blocks (in large
\xi_{A}
limit) up to subleading term for general level was obtained through an intrinsics analysis in Section
6.2
(equation (
206
)). In this appendix we give another derivation of (
206
) by taking the non-relativistic limit of the known CFT result (
337
)

F_{h_{A}}(q)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}F_{n}(h_{p},h_{A},c)q^{n}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left(1+\frac{h_{p}(h_{p}-1)}{2h_{A}}n+\mathcal{O}(h^{-2})\right)p(n)q^{n},

(500)

where we use
F_{h_{A}}(q)\equiv F_{h_{A},c}^{h_{p}}(q)
to shorten the notation.
Combining this with the anti-holomorphic part, the full torus block up to subleading term is

\displaystyle F_{h_{A}}(q)F_{\bar{h}_{A}}(\bar{q})

(501)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\bar{n}=0}^{\infty}\left(1+\frac{h_{p}(h_{p}-1)}{2h_{A}}n+\frac{\bar{h}_{p}(\bar{h}_{p}-1)}{2\bar{h}_{A}}\bar{n}+\mathcal{O}(h^{-2}_{A},\bar{h}^{-2}_{A},h^{-1}_{A},\bar{h}^{-1}_{A})\right)p(n)p(\bar{n})q^{n}\bar{q}^{\bar{n}}.

(502)

Let us focus only on the subleading term

\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\bar{n}=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{h_{p}(h_{p}-1)}{2h_{A}}n+\frac{\bar{h}_{p}(\bar{h}_{p}-1)}{2\bar{h}_{A}}\bar{n}\right)p(n)p(\bar{n})e^{2\pi i\tau n}e^{-2\pi i\bar{\tau}\bar{n}}.

(504)

If we take the ultra-relativistic limit of this, it would give us the BMS block in the induced representation. In order to get the BMS block in the highest weight representation we have to take the non-relativistic limit. Thus, we use the following contraction

(h,\bar{h})\rightarrow\frac{1}{2}\left(\Delta\mp\frac{\xi}{\epsilon}\right),\,\,\,\,(\tau,\bar{\tau})\rightarrow\pm\sigma-\epsilon\rho,

(505)

which give us

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\bar{n}=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{(\Delta_{p}-\frac{\xi_{p}}{\epsilon})(\frac{1}{2}(\Delta_{p}-\frac{\xi_{p}}{\epsilon})-1)}{2(\Delta_{A}-\frac{\xi_{p}}{\epsilon})}n+\frac{(\Delta_{p}+\frac{\xi_{p}}{\epsilon})(\frac{1}{2}(\Delta_{p}+\frac{\xi_{p}}{\epsilon})-1)}{2(\Delta_{A}+\frac{\xi_{A}}{\epsilon})}\bar{n}\right)

(506)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\times p(n)p(\bar{n})e^{2\pi i\sigma(n+\bar{n})}e^{2\pi i\epsilon\rho(\bar{n}-n)}

(507)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\bar{n}=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{(\epsilon\Delta_{p}-\xi_{p})(\Delta_{p}-\frac{\xi_{p}}{\epsilon}-2)}{4(\epsilon\Delta_{A}-\xi_{A})}n+\frac{(\epsilon\Delta_{p}+\xi_{p})(\Delta_{p}+\frac{\xi_{p}}{\epsilon}-2)}{4(\epsilon\Delta_{A}+\xi_{A})}\bar{n}\right)

(508)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\times p(n)p(\bar{n})e^{2\pi i\sigma(n+\bar{n})}e^{2\pi i\epsilon\rho(\bar{n}-n)}

(509)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\bar{n}=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{4\xi_{A}}\left(\frac{(-\epsilon\Delta_{p}+\xi_{p})(\Delta_{p}-\frac{\xi_{p}}{\epsilon}-2)}{(1-\epsilon\frac{\Delta_{A}}{\xi_{A}})}n+\frac{(\epsilon\Delta_{p}+\xi_{p})(\Delta_{p}+\frac{\xi_{p}}{\epsilon}-2)}{(1+\epsilon\frac{\Delta_{A}}{\xi_{A}})}\bar{n}\right)

(510)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\times p(n)p(\bar{n})e^{2\pi i\sigma(n+\bar{n})}(1+2\pi i\epsilon\rho(\bar{n}-n)+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2}))

(511)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\bar{n}=0}^{\infty}\frac{e^{2\pi i\sigma(n+\bar{n})}}{4\xi_{A}}\left((n+\bar{n})\left(2\Delta_{p}\xi_{p}-2\xi_{p}-\frac{\Delta_{A}}{\xi_{A}}\xi_{p}^{2}\right)+2\pi i\rho(\bar{n}-n)^{2}\xi_{p}^{2}+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)\right)

(512)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\times p(n)p(\bar{n}).

(513)

In the above equation we take the limit
\epsilon\rightarrow 0
. We also convert the summation: we sum over
n
and
\bar{n}
such that
n+\bar{n}=N
and sum over
N
from
0
to infinity. Doing this we get

\displaystyle\sum_{N=0}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{N}\frac{e^{2\pi i\sigma N}}{4\xi_{A}}\left(N\left(2\Delta_{p}\xi_{p}-2\xi_{p}-\frac{\Delta_{A}}{\xi_{A}}\xi_{p}^{2}\right)+2\pi i\rho(N-2k)^{2}\xi_{p}^{2}+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)\right)p(N-k)p(k)

(514)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{N=0}^{\infty}e^{2\pi i\sigma N}\left(\frac{N}{2\xi_{A}}\left(\Delta_{p}\xi_{p}-\xi_{p}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\Delta_{A}}{\xi_{A}}\xi_{p}^{2}\right)\sum_{k=0}^{N}p(N-k)p(k)\right.

(515)

\displaystyle\left.\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+\frac{\pi i\rho}{2}\frac{\xi_{p}^{2}}{\xi_{A}}\sum_{k=0}^{N}p(N-k)p(k)(N-2k)^{2}\right)

(516)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{N=0}^{\infty}e^{2\pi i\sigma N}\left(\frac{N}{2\xi_{A}}\left(\xi_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1)-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\Delta_{A}}{\xi_{A}}\xi_{p}^{2}\right)\widetilde{\text{dim}}_{N}\ +\frac{\pi i\rho}{2}\frac{\xi_{p}^{2}}{\xi_{A}}\sum_{k=0}^{N}p(N-k)p(k)(N-2k)^{2}\right).

(517)

Thus the subleading correction to
\mathcal{F}_{N}
is given by

\frac{N}{2\xi_{A}}\left(\xi_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1)-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\Delta_{A}}{\xi_{A}}\xi_{p}^{2}\right)\widetilde{\text{dim}}_{N}\ +\frac{\pi i\rho}{2}\frac{\xi_{p}^{2}}{\xi_{A}}\sum_{k=0}^{N}p(N-k)p(k)(N-2k)^{2}.

(518)

If we take
\Delta_{A}<<\xi_{A}
, then the subleading term is

\frac{N}{2\xi_{A}}\xi_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1)\widetilde{\text{dim}}_{N}\ +\frac{\pi i\rho}{2}\frac{\xi_{p}^{2}}{\xi_{A}}\sum_{k=0}^{N}p(N-k)p(k)(N-2k)^{2}.

(519)

We note that

\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=0}^{N}p(N-k)p(k)(N-2k)^{2}=\sum_{k=0}^{N}p(N-k)p(k)(N-k)(N-2k).

(520)

Thus, we can also write the subleading term (
519
) as

\frac{N}{2\xi_{A}}\xi_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1)\widetilde{\text{dim}}_{N}\ +\pi i\rho\frac{\xi_{p}^{2}}{\xi_{A}}\sum_{k=0}^{N}p(N-k)p(k)(N-k)(N-2k).

(521)

Appendix I
Asymptotic form of partition of integers

In this appendix we will use Cardy like analysis to find the asymptotic form of the partition of integers
p(N)
, the Hardy-Ramanujan partition formula.The generating function of partition of integers
p(N)
is given by

\prod_{N=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{1-q^{N}}=\sum_{N=0}^{\infty}p(N)q^{N}.

(522)

In terms of the Dedekind eta function

\eta(q)=q^{\frac{1}{24}}\prod_{N=1}^{\infty}(1-q^{N}),

(523)

we have

\frac{q^{\frac{1}{24}}}{\eta(q)}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}p(n)q^{n}.

(524)

Then we define
\mathcal{Z}(q)\equiv\frac{q^{\frac{1}{24}}}{\eta(q)}
. So we have

\mathcal{Z}(q)=\sum_{N=0}^{\infty}p(N)q^{N}.

(525)

We could invert the above relation to express
p(N)
in terms of
\mathcal{Z}(q)

p(N)=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int\frac{dq}{q^{N+1}}\mathcal{Z}(q).

(526)

In term of
q=e^{2\pi i\tau}
, the above equation is

p(N)=\int d\tau e^{-2\pi i\tau(N+1)}\mathcal{Z}(\tau).

(527)

Under the S modular transformation
\eta(\tau)
transforms as

\eta\left(-\frac{1}{\tau}\right)=\sqrt{-i\tau}\eta(\tau).

(528)

So we have

\displaystyle e^{\frac{2\pi i\tau}{24}}\frac{e^{-\frac{2\pi i}{24\tau}}}{\eta\left(-\frac{1}{\tau}\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{-\frac{2\pi i}{24\tau}}\frac{e^{\frac{2\pi i\tau}{24}}}{\sqrt{-i\tau}\eta(\tau)}

(529)

\displaystyle\implies e^{\frac{2\pi i\tau}{24}}\mathcal{Z}\left(-\frac{1}{\tau}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{e^{-\frac{2\pi i}{24\tau}}}{\sqrt{-i\tau}}\mathcal{Z}(\tau)

(530)

\displaystyle\implies\mathcal{Z}(\tau)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{\frac{\pi i\tau}{12}}e^{\frac{\pi i}{12\tau}}\mathcal{Z}\left(-\frac{1}{\tau}\right)\sqrt{-i\tau}.

(531)

Substituting this in (
527
), we obtain

\displaystyle p(N)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d\tau e^{-2\pi i\tau(N+1)}e^{\frac{\pi i\tau}{12}}e^{\frac{\pi i}{12\tau}}\mathcal{Z}\left(-\frac{1}{\tau}\right)\sqrt{-i\tau}

(532)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int d\tau e^{f(\tau)}\mathcal{Z}\left(-\frac{1}{\tau}\right)\sqrt{-i\tau},

(533)

where

f(\tau)=-2\pi i\tau(N+1)+\frac{\pi i\tau}{12}+\frac{\pi i}{12\tau}.

(534)

For large
N
we can use saddle point method to find
p(N)
. It is given by

p(N)\approx\sqrt{\frac{2\pi}{-f^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{0})}}e^{f(\tau_{0})}\mathcal{Z}(-\frac{1}{\tau_{o}})\sqrt{-i\tau_{0}},

(535)

where
\tau_{0}
