is the saddle point

\frac{df(\tau)}{d\tau}|_{\tau_{0}}=0.

(536)

The saddle point equation is given by

24N\tau_{0}^{2}+23\tau^{2}_{0}+1=0,

(537)

with solutions

\tau_{0}=i\frac{1}{\sqrt{23+24N}},\,\,-i\frac{1}{\sqrt{23+24N}}.

(538)

Since
N
is large

\tau_{0}\approx i\frac{1}{\sqrt{24N}},\,\,-i\frac{1}{\sqrt{24N}}.

(539)

We have to choose

\tau_{0}\approx i\frac{1}{\sqrt{24N}},

(540)

since this give the maxima for
f(\tau)
. More precisely

f(\tau_{0})\approx e^{\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}\pi\sqrt{N}}.

(541)

We also have

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{2\pi}{-f^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{0})}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{6^{\frac{1}{4}}2N^{\frac{3}{4}}},

(542)

\displaystyle\sqrt{-i\tau_{0}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(24)^{\frac{1}{4}}N^{\frac{1}{4}}}.

(543)

Thus, we get

\sqrt{\frac{2\pi}{-f^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{0})}}\sqrt{-i\tau_{0}}=\frac{1}{4\sqrt{3}N}.

(544)

Now

\mathcal{Z}(-\frac{1}{\tau_{0}})=\sum_{n}p(n)e^{-2\pi i\frac{1}{\tau_{0}}n}=\sum_{n}p(n)e^{-4\pi\sqrt{6N}n}\approx P(0)=1.

(545)

So, for large
N

p(N)\approx\sqrt{\frac{2\pi}{-f^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{0})}}e^{f(\tau_{0})}\mathcal{Z}(\tau_{o})\sqrt{-i\tau_{0}}\approx\frac{1}{4\sqrt{3}N}e^{\sqrt{\frac{2N}{3}}\pi}.

(546)

This is the Hardy-Ramanujan formula.

Appendix J
Asymptotic form of
\widetilde{\rm{dim}}_{n}

\widetilde{\rm{dim}}_{n}
is equal to the number of ways to partition an integer
N
using two colors. For example, for the integer 3, the possibilities are

{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}3},\,{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}12},\,{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}111},\,{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}2}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}1},\,{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}11}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}1},\,{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}2},\,{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}11},\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}111},\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}12},\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}3},

(547)

giving us
\widetilde{\rm{dim}}_{3}=10
. In general, we have

\widetilde{\rm{dim}}_{n}=\sum_{k=0}^{n}p(n-k)p(k).

(548)

It is easy to see that

\displaystyle\sum_{n_{1}=0}^{\infty}p(n_{1})q^{n_{1}}\sum_{n_{2}=0}^{\infty}p(n_{2})q^{n_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{n}p(n-k)p(k)q^{n}

(549)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{\rm{dim}}_{n}q^{n}.

(550)

Substituting (
522
) in the above equation

\left(\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{1-q^{n}}\right)^{2}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{\rm{dim}}_{n}q^{n}.

(551)

Expressing this in terms of the Dedekind eta function

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\frac{1}{12}}}{\eta(q)^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{\rm{dim}}_{n}q^{n}

(552)

\displaystyle\implies\frac{q^{\frac{1}{12}}}{\eta(q)^{2}}=\mathcal{Z}(q)^{2}\equiv\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{\rm{dim}}_{n}q^{n}.

(553)

Inverting the above equation, we obtain

\widetilde{\rm{dim}}_{N}=\int d\tau e^{-2\pi i\tau(N+1)}\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}(\tau).

(554)

We could see that

\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}=\mathcal{Z}^{2}.

(555)

Using (
531
) in the above equation, we get

{}\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}(\tau)=e^{\frac{\pi i\tau}{6}}e^{\frac{\pi i}{6\tau}}\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\tau}\right)(-i\tau).

(556)

Substituting this in (
554
), we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\rm{dim}}_{N}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d\tau e^{-2\pi i\tau(N+1)}e^{\frac{\pi i\tau}{6}}e^{\frac{\pi i}{6\tau}}\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\tau}\right)(-i\tau)

(557)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int d\tau e^{\tilde{f}(\tau)}\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}\left(-\frac{1}{\tau}\right)(-i\tau),

(558)

where

\tilde{f}(\tau)=-2\pi i\tau(N+1)+\frac{\pi i\tau}{6}+\frac{\pi i}{6\tau}.

(559)

For large
N
, using the saddle point analysis

\widetilde{\rm{dim}}_{N}\approx\sqrt{\frac{2\pi}{-\tilde{f}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{0})}}e^{\tilde{f}(\tau_{0})}\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}(-\frac{1}{\tau_{o}})(-i\tau_{0}),

(560)

where the saddle point equation
\frac{d\tilde{f}(\tau)}{d\tau}|_{\tau_{0}}=0
is

1+11\tau_{0}^{2}+12N\tau_{0}^{2}=0,

(561)

whose solutions are

\tau_{0}=i\frac{1}{\sqrt{12N+11}},\,-i\frac{1}{\sqrt{12N+11}}.

(562)

For large
N

\tau_{0}\approx i\frac{1}{\sqrt{12N}},\,-i\frac{1}{\sqrt{12N}}.

(563)

Once again, the maxima is given by the point
\tau_{0}\approx i\frac{1}{\sqrt{12N}}
.
Thus

e^{\tilde{f}(\tau_{0})}=e^{\sqrt{\frac{N}{3}}2\pi}.

(564)

We also have

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{2\pi}{-\tilde{f}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{0})}}

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3^{\frac{1}{4}}2N^{\frac{3}{4}}},

(565)

\displaystyle-i\tau_{0}

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\sqrt{3N}}

(566)

\displaystyle\implies\sqrt{\frac{2\pi}{-\tilde{f}^{\prime\prime}(\tau_{0})}}(-i\tau_{0})

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4\ 3^{\frac{3}{4}}N^{\frac{5}{4}}}.

(567)

Finally, the large
N
asymptotic formula for
\widetilde{\rm{dim}}_{N}
is given by

\widetilde{\rm{dim}}_{N}\approx\frac{1}{4\ 3^{\frac{3}{4}}N^{\frac{5}{4}}}e^{\sqrt{\frac{N}{3}}2\pi}.

(568)

Appendix K
Condition for leading term to dominate asymptotics of torus blocks

From (
206
), we could see that the large
\xi_{A}
expansion of the torus block upto first sub-leading term is given by

\sum_{N=0}^{\infty}\left[\left(1+\frac{\xi_{p}(\Delta_{p}-1)}{2\xi_{A}}N\right)\widetilde{\text{dim}}_{N}+\pi i\rho\frac{\xi_{p}^{2}}{\xi_{A}}\sum_{k=0}^{N}p(N-k)p(k)(N-k)(N-2k)\right]q^{N}.

(569)

We would like to find the condition which would allow us to keep only the leading term
\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{\text{dim}}_{N}q^{N}
and neglect the higher order terms in
\frac{1}{\xi_{A}}
.
If we look at the term inside the first bracket in the summand above, we can neglect
\frac{N}{\xi_{A}}
in comparison to 1 if
N
is finite and
\xi_{A}
is large. However,
N
is unbounded as we have to sum over
N
from
0
to infinity. So, however large
\xi_{A}
is, we always have a
N
comparable and larger than
\xi_{A}
so that we cannot neglect the term
\frac{N}{\xi_{A}}
. So, we have to make
q=e^{-2\pi\beta}
small i.e, make
\beta
small enough such that the sum can be truncated at some value
N<<\xi_{A}
.

Using the asymptotic form of
\widetilde{\text{dim}}_{N}
(
568
), we have
\widetilde{\text{dim}}_{N}\,q^{N}\sim N^{-\frac{5}{4}}e^{\sqrt{\frac{N}{3}}2\pi}e^{-2\pi\beta N}
. This has a saddle point at
N=N_{\star}=\frac{-15\beta+\sqrt{\pi}\sqrt{\pi-30\beta}+\pi}{24\pi\beta^{2}}
. Since
\beta<<1
we may approximate this as
N_{\star}\approx\frac{1}{24\beta^{2}}
. As we can see,
N_{\star}\gg 1
. Since
\widetilde{\text{dim}}_{N}q^{N}
is maximum at
N_{\star}
, we at least have to sum upto
N_{\star}
. So, in order to truncate the sum at some value
N<<\xi_{A}
, we at least require
N_{\star}<<\xi_{A}
which is same as the requirement that
\beta^{2}\xi_{A}>>1
. If we consider the second term in
\frac{1}{\xi_{A}}
and perform the similar analysis, we obtain the same condition. So, we have

{}\mathcal{F}_{\Delta_{A},\xi_{A},c_{L},c_{M}}^{\Delta_{p},\xi_{p}}(\sigma,\rho)\approx\sum_{N=0}^{\infty}q^{N}\widetilde{\text{dim}}_{N}=\frac{q^{\frac{1}{12}}}{\eta(\sigma)^{2}},~~~~~~\text{provided~}\beta\ll 1,\beta^{2}\xi_{A}\gg 1.

(570)

Appendix L
Details regarding number theory conjecture

In this section, we will provide some details regarding the validity of conjecture (
205
). This conjecture is important as it matches our intrinsic analysis of sub-leading term of BMS torus block with the limiting analysis from known CFT result. Though we have not been able to prove it, we have checked for its validity using two colours. We define
\widetilde{\rm{dim}}_{N}
to be the partition of an integer N using two colours .
Thus,

{}\begin{split}&\widetilde{\rm{dim}}_{1}=2:~~1,{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}1}\\
&\widetilde{\rm{dim}}_{2}=5:~~2,11,1{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}1},{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}11},{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}2}\\
&\widetilde{\rm{dim}}_{3}=10:~~3,12,111,11{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}1},1{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}11},{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}111},1{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}2},{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}12},{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}3}\\
&\widetilde{\rm{dim}}_{4}=20:~~4,31,211,1111,111{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}1},11{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}11},1{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}111},{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}1111},21{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}1},2{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}11},{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}2}11,{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}21}1,{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}211},3{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}1},{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}3}1,{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}31},{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}4},22,2{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}2},{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}22}\\
&\widetilde{\rm{dim}}_{5}=36:~~5,41,32,311,11111,1111{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}1},111{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}11},11{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}111},1{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}1111},{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}11111},31{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}1},3{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}11},{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}3}11,{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}31}1,{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}311},3{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}2},{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}3}2,{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}32},\\
&~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~4{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}1},{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}4}1,{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}41},221,22{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}1},2{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}21},{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}2}2{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}1},{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}221},{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}22}1,1112,111{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}2},11{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}1}2,11{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}12},1{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}112},{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}1112},{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}11}12,{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}111}2,{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}5}.\end{split}

(571)

We also have the partition of integer
N
defined as
p(N)
. Thus,

{}\begin{split}&\widetilde{\text{dim}}_{N}=\sum_{k=0}^{n}p(N-k)p(k).\\
&\text{where,~}p(0)=1,~p(1)=1,~p(2)=2,~p(3)=3,~p(4)=5,~p(5)=7.\end{split}

(572)

The conjecture described in (
205
) is

\displaystyle\sum_{\{\{l_{j}\},\{m_{j}\}\}\in A}\,\,\sum_{j=1}^{N}j^{2}l_{j}(m_{j}+1)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=0}^{N}p(N-k)p(k)(N-2k)^{2}

(573)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{N}p(N-k)p(k)(N-k)(N-2k).

(574)

We have checked for the validity of the conjecture upto
N=5
. We will describe here the calculation involved in level 5 as an example. For the RHS,

{}\begin{split}&\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=0}^{5}p(N-k)p(k)(N-2k)^{2}\\
&=\frac{1}{2}\Big[2p(5)p(0)5^{2}+2p(2)p(3)1^{2}+2p(1)p(4)3^{2}\Big]\\
&=\sum_{k=0}^{5}p(N-k)p(k)(N-k)(N-2k)=226.\\
\end{split}

(575)

Now, we will concentrate on the LHS.
We have

{}\sum_{\{\{l_{j}\},\{m_{j}\}\}\in A}\,\,\sum_{j=1}^{5}j(l_{j}+m_{j})=5.

(576)

Our aim is to calculate the quantity

\mathcal{I}=j^{2}l_{j}(m_{j}+1)

for the values of
\{j,l_{j},m_{j}\}
for
(j=1,\ldots,5)
satisfying (
576
). The table below shows the allowed values of
\{j,l_{j},m_{j}\}
and
\mathcal{I}
.
